# 14. SEO and content

## SEO Strategy, Priorities and Timelines

### What SEO and content do for a store

- SEO means making a store's pages appear in the unpaid, organic results of search engines, as opposed to paid search ads for which the advertiser pays per placement. [L0594.001]
- SEO targets Google and Bing. The lesson names Bing as the second major target after Google. [L0594.006]
- Organic Google search means people find the store through unpaid text results, usually on page one. It costs nothing per click, but it depends on the store ranking. [L0586.005]
- Organic search traffic comes when people search for a brand name, a product name or a SKU number and click through an unpaid result. It costs nothing per click but is not directly paid for. [L0676.006]
- Organic traffic is any traffic that does not come from paid ads, including search results, organic social posts and free shopping listings. [L0855.151]
- SEO produces free organic traffic from Google and Bing search results and is the free counterpart to paid search ads. [L0597.009]
- SEO is one of the core traffic sources because it brings in visitors without paying for each click. It is treated as free traffic, although it requires work to build. [L0759.004]
- Content marketing sits between publishing and advertising. You build an audience-owning platform and advertise your own products on it, instead of letting third parties monetize that audience. Owning the channel gives you control over how customers are influenced and serviced. [L0382.001]
- Blog posts keep generating traffic for years after a one-time production cost, while pay-per-click charges for every incremental visit. Content therefore has a long-lasting return on investment. [L0382.002]
- Ranking for keywords that matter to your business works like owning the best retail locations in your industry online. If you hold those search results, competitors are pushed out of them. [L0382.003]
- Organic search traffic is one of the funnel's traffic sources, brings in visitors without paid ads, adds to Google Shopping traffic and works alongside email and retargeting. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0458.049]
- A single piece of content that ranks on the first page of Google can deliver steady monthly organic traffic for years, potentially tens of thousands of visits over time. Judge content cost by this long-term return rather than the short-term price. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0379.019]
- Content marketing is a medium- to long-term traffic strategy. Early on it brings only small amounts of traffic through promotion, and it can lift conversion on the site when the content solves a buyer's problem. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0383.001]
- Content and SEO build awareness at the top of the funnel, so a shopper who later searches a high-value term already knows the brand. This raises the conversion chance on those high-value searches. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0740.017]
- Once SEO is fully built, drive large volumes of top-of-funnel traffic from people with no loyalty to a brand who want the best product, and guide them to your product. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0768.015]
- Low-value generic searches should be targeted through SEO and content marketing rather than by bidding on Google Shopping, because these channels reach that audience more profitably than Shopping ads do. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0740.016]
- Organic search traffic from keywords that problem-aware people type can be won at a much lower cost than paid ads for the same keywords. This makes it affordable to target the problem-aware stage at scale. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1392.010]

### How the search results page is laid out

- On a Google results page for a niche search, the top product listings are Google Shopping product listing ads, the "Sponsored" sections are paid search text ads, and further down come product feed results and organic listings. Only the organic listings are the target of SEO. [L0594.004]

### Ranking factors

- Website search rankings depend on three primary factors: quality backlinks from other domains, quality content on the site, and onsite optimizations. [L0173.001]
- Google's own description names three ranking factors above the rest: quality content, backlinks from other websites, and optimizing the site for the RankBrain algorithm. Other SEO activities can help a bit more, but without these three a site will struggle to rank. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0414.032, L1500.003]
- The two sources phrase the third factor differently. Source A calls it onsite optimization; Source B, citing Google, calls it optimization for RankBrain. Both describe the same area of work. [L0173.001, L0414.032]
- Neglecting any one of the three factors limits search rankings, however well the other work is done. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1500.003]
- SEO work falls into three main categories: on-page SEO, technical SEO, and off-page SEO, which is mainly link building from other websites to your site. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1500.001]
- On-page and technical SEO alone rarely produce strong organic search traffic. They multiply the results of content marketing and link building, so all three should run together. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1500.002]
- Backlinks are a core ranking factor, and deep content earns more links. Building deep content is therefore a prerequisite for reaching the top of Google's organic results. Link building is a separate task that must also be done to generate significant organic traffic. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1461.007]
- Backlinks matter most for very competitive keywords in crowded industries, not for niche e-commerce stores. (Older, 2022.) [L0429.067]
- Read together, the backlink statements apply as follows: the core-factor view (Source B) applies to competitive keywords, and the 2022 crowded-industry view applies to niche stores. [L1461.007, L0429.067]
- Ranking signals include user behavior: bounce rate, time on page and return visits. A good store experience can improve Google Shopping and organic performance. (Older, 2022.) [L0969.083]
- Google evaluates aggregated, anonymized user interaction data to judge whether a result is relevant to a query. Visitors who click an organic result and leave within seconds signal a poor match, so keeping visitors on the page and having them interact further is a ranking lever. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1099.001]
- Google states that it uses aggregated, anonymized interaction data to judge relevance, but it does not publish how much bounce rate or time on page weighs in ranking. Treat both as indicators of relevance and page quality, not as confirmed direct ranking factors. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1099.020]
- Organic traffic depends on many combined factors, so a quick list of tips is not enough. The full SEO material should be studied in depth. (Older, 2022.) [L1092.101]
- Established stores rank better in organic search mainly because they have existed longer, built more links and done SEO work for longer. A newer store can overtake them by applying the same SEO practices consistently over time. (Older, 2022.) [L0963.153]
- Domain authority alone should not decide whether to enter a niche. Checking competitor scores only shows which keyword competition level is realistic for your store. [L1227.030]
- Organic clicks concentrate on the first few Google results, and deeper result pages are rarely visited. Product pages should therefore aim for the top organic positions, not just to be indexed. [L1227.029]
- Clicks concentrate at the top: about three quarters of traffic goes to the top three organic results, and the number one spot takes roughly two thirds of that. Reaching position one matters more than reaching position three. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1461.024]

### On-page SEO

- On-page SEO means optimizing each individual web page so that search engines can understand what the page is about, which context it provides, and which searcher it serves. The goal is to make the page match the search terms it should rank for. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1118.001]
- On-page SEO works through many small adjustments to a page's content and structure that tell search engines which search terms the page should match. It is the first SEO work most sites do after technical SEO is in place, and it should be reviewed even if some of it was already done. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1118.002]
- Product pages need SEO work for the search engine listing and the product description. Collection pages, standard pages and blogs also need SEO work, using the newest SEO guides. (2024.) [L0806.076]
- Apply the SEO optimizations from day one, as part of setting up product pages and the store. They are not a later project. (Older, 2018.) [L1109.061]
- For a new store, plan page titles, URLs and categories before launch, because it is much harder to add SEO structure to a finished site later. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0949.057]
- Do not delay paid ads on a new Shopify store because most URLs show as not indexed. Only the key pages need to be indexed, and indexing duplicate URL variants would hurt search results rather than help them. [L0373.003]
- Outsourcing alt text and meta descriptions to freelancers on Upwork is acceptable, because the writing does not require expert skill. AI tools can speed up the work where you can use them. (2024.) [L0965.081]

### Target the products you sell

- SEO should target the products, brands and SKU numbers you sell, because product pages are what bring organic buyers. Ranking for a broad niche keyword does not produce the same sales. [L0941.076]
- A narrow customer focus simplifies content and SEO planning, because one buyer's interests answer the question of what to publish first. Broad stores with many product types and buyer types often stall because they cannot decide where to start. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0905.014]
- For a nationwide high-ticket dropshipping store, geo-targeted local SEO is usually not worth it, because customers can be anywhere in the country. Local SEO normally covers a location within one state; once the area spans several states it is no longer local SEO. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0879.007]
- Low-margin brand products can still rank organically if the site is set up correctly, which can produce some sales without paid traffic. (Older, 2019.) [L1068.074]
- A new store without traffic data can find its likely best sellers by checking the top product placements in the category pages of established competitor stores. Optimize those product pages first, because top placement usually reflects strong sellers. [L1190.018]
- A store can earn its first sale without any paid ads by ranking organically in Google for long-tail searches such as brand name, product name and SKU number. These rankings can be captured before Google Shopping product listings are set up, and the sale came at zero ad cost. [L1223.006]

### When to start

Sources differ on whether paid traffic or SEO comes first. Each position applies under the conditions below.

| Position | Sources | Applies when |
|---|---|---|
| Paid traffic first, organic in the background | L0274.061 (2023); L0801.084 (older, 2021); L1111.119 (older, 2020); L1112.032 (older, 2021) | Paid campaigns can be run profitably; in the early life of a store, money goes to paid traffic for customers and cash flow; blogs follow once the store is profitable from paid traffic, unless the approach is purely long-term SEO; paid can bring early sales while organic develops |
| Free channels and SEO from the start | L0360.058 (2024); L0857.047 (2025); L0713.003 (older, 2021-22); L0853.077 (older, 2021) | Before buying traffic, use free channels; start organic early even while doing other work; SEO runs alongside paid traffic from the start for high-ticket dropshipping; free Google listings are used early, including when relaunching a stalled store |
| Content after validation and supplier approval | L0249.066 (older, 2022); L0962.145 (older, 2021); L0544.010 | Niche validation and supplier approval come first; a tight budget goes to product pages and supplier outreach; SEO and content are an advanced later-stage layer, and reaching initial sales does not depend on them |

- Before buying traffic, use free channels such as SEO on product pages and free Merchant Center listings to bring visitors in. Paid ads can be funded from the profit those visitors generate. (2024.) [L0360.058]
- Start organic SEO early, even while doing other work, because traffic takes a long time to build. (2025.) [L0857.047]
- Free Google product listings can drive traffic without ad spend, so list products there early. Relaunching a stalled store also benefits from these free listings. (Older, 2021.) [L0853.077]
- Do not prioritize getting content written immediately. Start with niche validation and supplier approval, and build content gradually as the store proves profitable. (Older, 2022.) [L0249.066]
- A blog is not needed to get suppliers to approve a new store, so a tight budget should go to product pages and supplier outreach first. Blog content can be added later to build organic traffic over time. (Older, 2021.) [L0962.145]
- SEO and content marketing are an advanced, later-stage layer. Reaching initial sales does not depend on them. [L0544.010]
- Improve store pages and collection pages for SEO as the store grows, since organic traffic builds slowly. Free Google product listings and paid Google Shopping ads give data much faster than SEO. (Older, 2021.) [L0968.067]

### What to prioritize first

- A new store should prioritize product pages over blog content. Blogs and content pages should come only after product pages are optimized and the site is being crawled through Search Console. (Older, 2022.) [L0429.067]
- For a store that has not done SEO yet, improve the collection pages and product pages first, since those are the pages you want people to land on. Build blog content around them afterward. (Older, 2021.) [L0801.085]
- Start SEO work with the top 20% of products, which bring in about 80% of website visits. Find those products in Shopify analytics or Google Analytics and optimize them first. (2024.) [L0965.079]
- Sources agree on the order of the work: product pages come first and blog posts come after. The first step differs by source. [L0594.005, L0245.045, L0962.154]

Sequence A, a Shopify store SEO order (undated):
1. Install the recommended SEO app.
2. Do organic keyword research.
3. Optimize product pages.
4. Optimize collection pages.
5. Optimize standard pages.
6. Optimize blog posts.
7. Promote the content to get early traffic.
8. Finally, optimize the search results once everything is set up. [L0594.005]

Sequence B, a 2020 checklist (older):
1. Increase site speed (measure with Google Page Speed Test and fix identified errors).
2. Mobile optimize (automatically handled by Source A theme).
3. Customize top 20% of products by traffic with more photos, embedded videos, and Q&A.
4. Create 3-5 anchor blog posts (massive value pieces) with contributions from niche authorities.
5. Publish guest posts on high-ranking competitor sites (free or paid).
6. Appear as guest on niche YouTube channels or podcasts, or start your own interviewing suppliers and customers. [L0245.045]

Sequence C, a 2021 priority order (older):
1. Optimize product pages so they rank for buying keywords such as product names, brand names and SKU numbers.
2. Create buyer's guides directly in Shopify.
3. Add blog posts later, since they target lower-converting search terms and make up a smaller share of traffic. [L0962.154]

### Realistic timelines

Older sources give different start points for results. Use the figure that matches the situation: L0383.002 is for a fresh start, L0429.063 describes when SEO work starts to show, and L0962.155 gives the longest range.

| Timeline | Note | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Results from a fresh start typically visible around 3, 6 and 12 months | Older, 2021-22 | L0383.002 |
| Visible results usually start around months two and three, and keep improving through months four, five and six | Older, 2022 | L0429.063 |
| Optimization results can take six months to a year to show | Older, 2021 | L0962.155 |
| Title and meta rewrites on blog pages are a 60-day effort rather than a 7-day result | Plan as medium-term work | L1269.028 |
| Blog SEO works through consistency over months and does not produce traffic after a few posts | 2025 | L0857.047 |
| First product SEO results often appear quickly; the largest gains build up over months | Undated | L1190.047 |
| SEO results cannot be tested like paid ads, where budget or bid changes show numbers within days | Older, 2022 | L0429.004 |
| Free Google listings and paid Google Shopping give data much faster than SEO | Older, 2021 | L0968.067 |
| Judge content over months, not days | Older, 2021-22 | L0383.010 |

- Organic traffic builds over time as Google recognises a site as a quality destination. Alexa rank improves as organic and direct traffic grow. (Older, 2020.) [L1111.118]
- Organic traffic is the goal in the long run, but it is not required to start. (Older, 2020.) [L1111.119]
- A new SEO-focused store reached about $20,000 in sales in its second or third live month, and the owner credited the SEO work as the main driver. This is one reported result, so treat it as an example of what early SEO work can produce, not as a benchmark. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0949.068]
- SEO is a long-term channel that requires patience, consistency and admin work. Over time it tends to be more profitable, consistent and reliable than paid traffic alone. (Source C, undated.) [L1251.017]

### SEO and paid traffic compared

- In a Shopify Spring 2026 analytics example, search visitors converted at a higher rate than social visitors, but search drove far fewer sessions. All 51 products had no SEO titles or descriptions set, so product SEO needed to be added.

| Channel | Conversion rate | Sessions per month |
|---|---|---|
| Search | 5.75% | 174 |
| Social | 3.06% | 28,496 |

[L0351.044]

- A 2018 SEMrush study across e-commerce businesses found 50% of e-commerce traffic came from organic search results, 6.8% from paid ads, 4.7% from social media, 28% from direct visits and 10% from referrals. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0414.031]
- Organic search is the largest traffic opportunity for high-ticket dropshipping. Top businesses in the $10 million-plus range get most of their traffic from organic rather than paid ads, and without meaningful organic traffic it is hard to reach low seven figures in annual revenue. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0414.029]
- The four main traffic channels (Google Ads, organic search, social media and YouTube) combined reach over 97% of internet users, which is the stated reason to be on all four. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0414.035]
- Doing the three core SEO factors consistently was associated with up to 50,000 additional visits per month for the presenter's businesses and clients. Organic traffic can supply thousands of visits per day at a fraction of paid traffic cost. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0414.033]
- With consistent output, organic search and YouTube traffic can eventually exceed what the same traffic would cost in paid ads. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0383.002]
- Content and SEO traffic usually sits higher in the sales funnel, with a longer time between first discovery and purchase. The more organic traffic a store generates, the more important retargeting and email marketing become. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1239.011, L1240.011]
- For high-ticket dropshipping, SEO usually becomes the biggest long-term traffic source, but it has to be started early and needs a medium to long time horizon before results arrive. It should run alongside paid traffic from the start. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0713.003]
- For high-ticket dropshipping, SEO is described as the strongest traffic source over the long term. Once paid ads work, adding SEO, content marketing and backlink building is the logical next step for extra traffic. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1495.007]
- SEO is a top marketing priority because its results take time to appear. Once it generates results, the traffic costs less than paid traffic and can grow significantly larger over time. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1270.002]
- Organic is the long-term primary traffic focus, but Bing Ads are worth running alongside it. Several members got some of their first sales through Bing. (2023.) [L0470.070]
- A profitable store can run on free organic traffic without any paid Google or Microsoft ads, so a platform suspension does not automatically end the business. (2024.) [L0471.032]
- A store with only organic traffic can produce sales from a very small number of visits when SEO is set up well and the targeted keywords have low competition. Paid ads are not required in every case. [L1254.017]
- An organic-only store with six website visits produced one order worth $3,710.70 without paid ads. A small number of visits can convert when the targeted keywords have low competition and the store's search setup is correct, but one large sale from a few visits is not a reliable sign of steady traffic. [L1254.022]
- A store started in 2016 with 80% referral traffic from influencer links and external blogs. Its owner realized customers research extensively before buying and built guides, tutorials and product reviews on the store itself. Within one year, traffic flipped to 80% organic (Google search), and referral traffic dropped to third place. The affiliate program was kept but no longer relied on; organic content became the primary growth engine. [L0226.010]

### Content strategy and cadence

- Build content over time to become the authority in your niche: buying guides, tutorials and detailed product pages. This is a long-term strategy and does not need to be done on day one. (2024.) [L0806.075]
- Start creating blog content only after your paid advertising campaigns are profitable. Prioritize paid traffic first and build organic traffic in the background. (2023.) [L0274.061]
- Begin with one piece of content per month minimum, ideally weekly. (2023.) [L0274.061]
- Content pieces work best when they are run alongside ongoing SEO work rather than as a separate effort. Operators who combined both reached a point where organic traffic exceeded their paid traffic. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0383.013]
- Stopping or slowing content and SEO work lets competitors catch up and take the top market position. Organic traffic efforts must never be paused, even after success. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0383.009]
- Do keyword research before writing content, since without it you do not know what the content should target. Content also performs better when backlinks point to it. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1456.030]
- A realistic SEO cadence is one quality content piece per month, each followed by backlink building in the months after it is published, rather than many pieces in one month. Each piece gets some traffic on its own, but sustaining organic traffic takes a steady stream of pieces. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1456.031]
- Build blog content around product and collection pages. Create buyer's guides directly in Shopify before adding blog posts. [L0962.154]

Cadence statements differ by source. Both apply, depending on the source you follow.

| Source | Cadence | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Source A, 2023 | Minimum one piece per month, ideally weekly | Starts after paid campaigns are profitable [L0274.061] |
| Source B, 2021-22 (older) | One quality piece per month, each followed by backlink building | Many pieces in one month is not the realistic cadence [L1456.031] |

### Outsourcing, agencies and ownership

- Before hiring SEO help from freelancers or agencies, cover the basic on-page SEO lesson first, which takes about two and a half hours. (Older, 2022.) [L0429.033]
- For these store types, simple on-page techniques are usually enough. Outsource only the specific tasks you still want done. Avoid broad e-commerce SEO services, which often cost a lot for few results. (Older, 2022.) [L0429.033]
- Keyword research can be outsourced in raw form, but the owner must still decide which search terms to target and what content to produce. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1456.032]
- Raw keyword research can be bought from freelancers on Fiverr. The store owner must still decide which search terms to target and what each piece of content should cover, because a freelancer cannot make that decision. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1456.043]
- Content writing is commonly outsourced because most people write poorly for this purpose. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1456.032]
- Backlink building is worth trying once yourself first. After that, it can be outsourced to an agency or to a virtual assistant trained on the process, which is cheaper. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1456.032]
- Be even more cautious when hiring an SEO agency than when hiring a Google Ads agency. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1053.017]
- Be cautious about SEO agencies that charge large monthly retainers for slow results. (Older, 2021.) [L0962.155]
- SEO work on an existing store often comes down to a few tweaks to product pages. Hiring an expensive SEO agency rarely returns its cost. (2023.) [L0855.150]
- Done For You Content Marketing is listed as an outsourced content marketing option in the December 2020 coaching call. The lesson names it without describing its scope or cost. (Older, 2020.) [L0426.014]
- SEO work can be split into on-page and off-page SEO and assigned to a dedicated SEO person. Content such as blog posts can be written by a separate content writer who reports to the marketing manager. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1334.030]
- The grow track includes a dedicated module on search engine optimization, content marketing and backlink building, and a separate module on Google Ads. An owner strong in one area but weak in SEO should go to the SEO module first. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0545.018]

### From action list to work schedule

- SEO works only through continuous work; occasional or cram sessions produce little effect. The three biggest SEO levers work together and should run at the same time: keyword research, content production and backlinks. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1456.029]
- SEO, content marketing and link building each deliver value alone, but they work much better together. Plan them as one connected effort over time instead of choosing one over the others. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1270.003]
- A full SEO action item list is often too long to work through in one go. Before starting, decide which items come first and which approach you will use for each one, so your time is not wasted. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1270.004]
- Keep one action item spreadsheet that lists all SEO tasks for the product pages, collection pages and blog pages. Use it to track which tasks are open, in progress or done. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1270.005]
- A 90-day rock for a six-month SEO priority can be completing six pieces of content, each with promotion. This turns the six-month target into a smaller quarterly milestone that can be measured. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0447.027]

Turning the action list into a work schedule:
1. Go through every SEO action item and note what each one requires.
2. Check the site and mark items that are already done so they are not repeated.
3. Decide which of the remaining items you will implement.
4. Sort the chosen items into higher-priority and lower-priority groups.
5. Place the higher-priority items first in your work schedule, then add the lower-priority items after them. [L1270.001]

## Search Console, Bing Webmaster Tools and Sitemaps

### Overview: What the Search Tools Do

- Google Search Console is a free Google tool for tracking organic (non-paid) search traffic. It shows how your store appears in Google's organic results, tracks organic clicks and impressions, reveals the search keywords people used to find your site, displays indexed pages and products, and identifies site experience issues such as page speed problems, errors, and usability problems. [L0171.001]
- It is reached at search.google.com. It reports organic search performance and site errors, and it needs no paid traffic. [L0735.001]
- It does not run ads or report paid ad performance. It reports free organic listings and their clicks, while paid campaign data lives in the ad platform. [L0736.013]
- It shows which problems hold back organic rankings, which keywords bring free traffic, and how much free traffic arrives. Its error reports should be fixed so the site can rank higher. [L0735.002]
- Set it up early. It collects data over time, and data only starts filling in after verification. [L0736.001]
- Set it up early so organic traffic has time to build. Organic rankings in Google can produce buyers without paid ads. [L1393.001]
- A newly verified property shows no data at first, because coverage data (valid pages and pages with errors) needs time to accumulate. [L0736.006]
- Organic search traffic and keyword data appear over time. Only sites that receive organic traffic show meaningful performance reports, and unbranded product stores may see little organic traffic. [L0736.010]
- *Older (2019):* Google Webmaster Tools, now Search Console, is free and reports errors when Google cannot read the website. It is mainly used for SEO monitoring. [L1275.038]
- *Older (2020):* Search Console is listed as a resource in the December 2020 coaching call for monitoring how a store appears in Google search. The lesson names it without describing setup steps. [L0426.013]
- *Older (2021-22):* Search Console is mandatory. It shows how Google crawls the site and which pages are and are not indexed, and the domain must be registered in the tool. [L1272.002]

### Account, Email and Ownership

- Log in with the same email address used for the Google Analytics account, so that both tools link together. [L0735.001]
- Use the same Google account email in Search Console as in Google Analytics, so the two tools link without extra work. [L0736.002]
- One source gives a different login: log in with your store's Google Workspace email. [L0171.003]
- Email rule in practice: the sources name two emails. The Analytics email is the one that links the tools [L0735.001, L0736.002, L0735.012]. The Workspace email is the wording in the setup procedure [L0171.003].
- Mistake: signing in with a different email than the one used for Google Analytics makes the two tools harder to link. [L0735.012]
- Mistake: adding a verification TXT record for a domain already verified in another Search Console account can transfer ownership away from the existing account. Check ownership before adding a new verification record. [L0736.005]
- One Search Console account can hold several domains. After login, every verified property appears, and more store domains can be added to the same login later. [L0735.014]

### Setup Procedures: Three Source Versions

The sources give three complete setup procedures. They differ in admin path and record wording and are kept as written.

**Version A (16 steps, Settings > Domains)**

1. Go to search.google.com and log in with your store's Google Workspace email.
2. Select Domain as the property type.
3. Enter your full domain (example: yourstore.com) and click Continue.
4. Copy the entire TXT verification record provided by Google.
5. In Shopify Admin, go to Settings > Domains.
6. Click your domain.
7. Open Domain settings.
8. Add a TXT record.
9. Paste the verification record.
10. Click Save/Confirm.
11. Return to Google Search Console and wait for verification to complete.
12. Click Go to Property.
13. Copy your sitemap URL: yourstore.com/sitemap.xml (use your actual domain).
14. In Search Console, click Sitemaps in the left menu.
15. Paste your sitemap URL.
16. Click Submit. [L0171.003]

**Version B (9 steps, Domain settings > Add custom record)**

1. Open search.google.com and log in with the same email address used for the Google Analytics account.
2. Enter the domain in the property field and select the property type 'Domain' (not URL prefix).
3. Click Continue; Search Console shows a DNS TXT verification record. Copy the full text line.
4. In the store admin, go to Settings > Domains, click the domain, then open Domain settings.
5. Click Add custom record, choose the record type TXT, paste the copied value, and confirm.
6. Return to Search Console and click Verify. Propagation can take a minute or so; refresh if it does not pass at once.
7. After ownership is verified, click 'Go to property'.
8. Open Sitemaps in the left navigation.
9. Enter the sitemap URL in the form yourdomain.com/sitemap.xml (the store's own sitemap file, copied from the browser) and click Submit. [L0735.004]

**Version C (11 steps, Sales channels > Online Store > Domains)**

1. Go to search.google.com/search-console and click 'Start now'.
2. Enter the domain name and click Continue.
3. Choose verification via DNS record and copy the TXT (text) record value that Google shows.
4. In the Shopify admin, go to Sales channels > Online Store > Domains.
5. Click 'DNS settings'.
6. Click 'Add custom record' and choose 'Text record'.
7. In the Name field enter @.
8. In the Text value field paste the code copied from Google.
9. Click Confirm.
10. Return to Google Search Console and click Verify.
11. Once verified, open the Search Console dashboard for the domain. [L0736.003]

| Element | Version A | Version B | Version C | Source ids |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Admin path | Settings > Domains, then click the domain, then Domain settings | Settings > Domains, then the domain, then Domain settings | Sales channels > Online Store > Domains, then DNS settings | [L0171.003, L0735.004, L0736.003] |
| Record type | TXT | TXT | Text record | [L0171.003, L0735.004, L0736.003] |
| Name field | Not specified | Not specified | @ | [L0736.003] |
| Adding the record | Add a TXT record | Add custom record | Add custom record | [L0171.003, L0735.004, L0736.003] |
| Verify step | Wait for verification to complete | Click Verify | Click Verify | [L0171.003, L0735.004, L0736.003] |

Admin paths differ between the sources. Give both paths and use whichever matches the admin menu you see. [L0171.003, L0735.004, L0736.003]

### Verification Details

- Choose the property type Domain, not URL prefix. This verifies the whole domain (all subdomains and protocols) through a DNS text record. [L0735.006, L0735.004, L0171.003]
- Add the TXT record on the store's domain in the store admin, using the path shown in the table above. [L0735.005]
- If the domain is registered outside Shopify, add the same TXT record in that registrar's DNS settings. Look up how to change DNS records for that provider. [L0736.004]
- Propagation can take a minute or so. Refresh the page if verification does not pass at once. [L0735.004]
- If the verification TXT record is already present in the store's DNS settings, ownership can show as verified immediately. A page refresh is only needed when the record is not yet visible in the store admin. [L0735.015]

### Shopify Sitemap: Behaviour and Limits

- Shopify generates the sitemap automatically at yourstore.com/sitemap.xml. You do not need to create it manually. [L0171.004]
- The sitemap is generated automatically and does not need to be created or edited. [L0736.008]
- *Older (2021-22):* Shopify generates an XML sitemap automatically at the store's root domain under /sitemap.xml, so no manual creation is needed. [L1333.001]
- The sitemap index links to child sitemaps: products (sitemap_products_1.xml), pages (sitemap_pages_1.xml), collections (sitemap_collections_1.xml) and blogs. [L0736.008]
- In the Search Console Sitemaps page, the submitted Shopify sitemap is listed with type 'Sitemap index'. [L1333.015] *(Older, 2021-22.)*
- If the store has videos, they are listed in the Sitemaps report too. [L1271.006]
- To see exactly what is sent to Google, open yourstore.com/sitemap.xml in a browser. The page shows every URL being sent to Google, so you can confirm the right pages are included. [L0965.077]
- The sitemap URL follows the format yourdomain.com/sitemap.xml. Copy the full URL from the browser and submit it under Sitemaps. [L0735.009]

| Limit or behaviour | Detail | Source ids |
|---|---|---|
| Images per product | The standard Shopify XML sitemap lists only one image per product. Product images beyond the first may never be submitted to search engines. Products with many distinct images, each with its own file name and alt text, lose potential image-search visibility. *(Older, 2021-22.)* | [L1333.002] |
| Separate image sitemap | For an image sitemap, enter the URL the app provides (for example images.xml) and submit it the same way. *(Older, 2021-22.)* | [L1333.004] |
| Sitemap is a convenience | Search engines can still discover pages through internal links. The sitemap speeds up discovery and picks up updates faster. *(Older, 2021-22.)* | [L1333.001] |
| Unpublished pages | The sitemap submits unpublished pages by default unless they are excluded. | [L1271.004] |
| Google without submission | Google will often find and index a store on its own. Submitting helps Google crawl the site consistently. *(Older, 2021.)* | [L0671.135, L0671.270] |
| Ranking without submission | Pages can still rank without submission, but Google crawls the whole site regularly once submitted. *(Older, 2021.)* | [L0962.156] |

### Submitting the Sitemap

Four procedures are given. Each is kept as written.

**Procedure 1 (undated)**

1. In the sidebar, under Index, click Sitemaps.
2. In the 'Add a new sitemap' field, the domain prefix is already shown; type sitemap.xml after it.
3. Click Submit.
4. Status first shows 'Pending', then 'Success'.
5. Once successful, the report shows how many URLs were found and any errors to fix. [L0736.007]

**Procedure 2 (*older, 2021-22*)**

1. Verify the website in Google Search Console (usually a quick process when first opening the tool).
2. Open the Sitemaps section in the left menu.
3. In the field "Add a new sitemap", enter the sitemap path only, e.g. sitemap.xml for Shopify, after the domain already shown.
4. Press SUBMIT.
5. Check that the submitted sitemap appears in the list with status Success and a last-read date.
6. For an image sitemap, enter the URL the app provides (e.g. images.xml) and submit it the same way. [L1333.004]

**Procedure 3 (2023)**

1. Find your sitemap URL, for example yourdomain.com/sitemap.xml.
2. Open Google Search Console and go to the Sitemaps section.
3. Submit the current sitemap URL as a new sitemap.
4. Check that the pages listed reflect the current store. Old 404 pages should disappear because they are no longer in the current sitemap. [L0835.090]

**Procedure 4 (*older, 2021*)**

1. Open Google Search Console.
2. Sign in with the same Google account used for Google Analytics and Google Ads.
3. Submit the store's sitemap. Google then crawls the whole site regularly and reports errors that affect organic ranking, although pages can still rank without submission. [L0962.156]

**Entry format in the submission form**

- The form shows the domain prefix already. Enter the path only, for example sitemap.xml. [L1333.004, L0736.007]
- Other sources say to paste the full sitemap URL, for example yourdomain.com/sitemap.xml. [L0171.003, L0735.004]

**Reading the submitted sitemap**

- Status first shows Pending, then Success. Success is expected. [L0736.007, L1333.005, L1333.015] *(Older, 2021-22 for the last two.)*
- The submitted sitemaps table shows Type, Submitted date, Last read date, Status and discovered URL count. [L1333.005, L1333.015] *(Older, 2021-22.)*
- Check the submitted sitemap and the number of discovered pages in Sitemaps. [L1271.006]
- Submitting a sitemap makes Google crawl the store more often. Updates to pages, collections, blog posts and products are picked up sooner, so ranking changes can happen faster. A regularly updated sitemap is the basis for organic visibility. [L1271.003]
- Submitting a sitemap lets Google know the site exists and which pages and products it contains, so it can crawl and index them for organic results. Indexing takes time, so data only fills in gradually after setup. [L0735.003]

### Checking Indexing and Reports

**Check that the right pages are indexed**

1. Open the indexed pages report.
2. Click view examples of indexed pages.
3. Confirm that the homepage, contact page, privacy policy, shipping and returns page, blog posts and all product page URLs appear there. [L0373.002]

**What the reports show**

- Once data populates, the reports show organic impressions and clicks, indexed page counts, the search queries (keywords) that brought visitors, and any site experience issues. [L0171.005]
- The Coverage report is the most useful free SEO diagnostic, because it reflects what Google's own crawler experienced on the site. Check it regularly to spot pages Google cannot index or cannot reach. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1333.007]
- Third-party audit tools are useful, but their findings may differ from Google's view. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1333.007]
- Search Console and Bing Webmaster Tools report which external links point to a site and which search terms generate impressions. This data is limited in both tools, so use it as a basic view next to a third-party SEO tool. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1272.015]

### Smart SEO Sitemap Control

Smart SEO is a Shopify app that controls which pages go into the sitemap submitted to Google.

1. Open the Smart SEO app from inside the Shopify admin. [L1271.001]

- Google Search Console must already be set up for the store before Smart SEO is connected to it. The app then adds the website to Search Console and submits the sitemap through that existing property. [L1271.020]
- The app's sitemap section lists the store's pages individually. To keep a specific page out of the sitemap submitted to Google, click Exclude next to that page. Excluded pages are no longer submitted, so they will not rank. [L1271.019]
- Excluding a page stops it from being submitted to Google, so it will not rank. Exclusions are useful for inactive products, duplicate pages or pages you do not want in search results. Use them sparingly. [L1271.004]
- Mistake: excluding pages without need removes them from the sitemap and therefore from Google's search index. Do not exclude products that should rank. [L1271.005]
- Mistake: do not assume unpublished pages are hidden from Google unless they are explicitly excluded. [L1271.005, L1271.004]

### Recrawling and Updating After Changes

**Recrawl by resubmitting the sitemap (*older, 2022*)**

1. Open Google Search Console.
2. Click Sitemaps in the left navigation.
3. Delete the old sitemap if needed, then add your sitemap address (yourdomain.com/sitemap.xml).
4. Submit it so Google crawls it again; results appear in real time, usually within minutes. Search Console only offers community support, with answers from forum experts. [L0429.020]

**Recrawl old pages after changing collections (*older, 2019*)**

Source wording: Google Webmaster Tools (google.com/webmasters). This name predates Search Console.

1. Log in with the same email address used for Google Ads, Google Shopping and Google Analytics, creating the account if it does not exist.
2. Verify the URL of the website.
3. Use the option to crawl the website so that Google's bots revisit the site and update the pages.

Pages that still show old URLs and return 404 errors are usually still indexed by Google and get updated after the recrawl. [L0467.019]

**Timing**

- Resubmitting the sitemap makes Google read it again, and results can appear within minutes. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0429.020]
- After changing page content, Google recrawls the site over a few weeks. Request a recrawl after changing product page meta or content to speed up ranking changes. [L0964.137]
- The best time to request indexing is after updating product page meta information. Waiting a couple of weeks lets the rankings settle. [L0964.138]
- Use both timings together. The sitemap read can show within minutes, while ranking changes from content updates take a few weeks. [L0429.020, L0964.137, L0964.138]

### Bing Webmaster Tools

**Why add Bing**

- Bing is a second organic search channel that works without paid advertising. It adds traffic on top of Google. [L0303.012]
- Bing tools work like Search Console for Bing. Register the site there too, because Bing can deliver organic traffic and sales even though it is a smaller search engine. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1272.003]
- Set up Bing before or alongside paid campaigns. Set it up before building Shopping ads, so organic results can be added alongside the paid campaigns. [L1034.009, L1034.010]
- Most checks available in Search Console have an equivalent in Bing Webmaster Tools. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1333.014]

**Setup by import from Search Console**

1. Open bing.com/webmasters.
2. Click Get started.
3. Sign in with a Microsoft, Google or Facebook account.
4. On the Welcome to Bing Webmaster Tools page, use the import option for sites already verified in Google Search Console.
5. Click Import.
6. Select the Google account that owns the Search Console property; Bing identifies the account by its email address.
7. Click Allow to approve sharing.
8. If several domains are linked to that Search Console account, select the domain you want and keep it ticked.
9. Click Import.
10. When Bing confirms that one site was imported, click Done.
11. Wait while Bing processes the data, which can take up to 48 hours.
12. Review the dashboard, whose sidebar links mirror Google Search Console. [L0303.002]

**Sitemap in Bing**

- If the site was imported from Search Console, the sitemap is already carried over and does not need to be submitted again in Bing. [L0303.008]
- A sitemap can also be submitted manually in Bing, or linked through the Google Search Console account. Both methods work. [L0303.008]
- Submit the same XML sitemap URL in Bing as in Search Console. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1333.014]
- Submitting the sitemap makes sure every page of a Shopify store, including product pages, is crawled by Bing and added to its search index. Indexed pages can appear in Bing search results and be checked in the Bing tools. [L0303.009]

**Mistakes and alerts**

- Mistake: Bing matches the Google account during import by its email address. If you sign in with a Google account that uses a different email than the one that set up the Search Console property, the site will not be available to import. [L0303.016]
- Bing sends automatic email notifications when it detects problems on a website. Fixing these issues helps the site earn as much organic traffic from Bing as possible. [L0303.011]

### Ongoing Routine

- Fix the error reports in Search Console so the site can rank higher. [L0735.002]
- Check the Coverage report regularly for pages Google cannot index or reach. *(Older, 2021-22.)* [L1333.007]
- Respond to alerts. Google emails the account owner when it detects errors on the site, so these issues can be fixed promptly. [L0736.009]
- Bing also sends automatic email notifications for detected problems. [L0303.011]
- Keep the sitemap current. Submitting a regularly updated sitemap keeps updates to pages, collections, blog posts and products visible to search engines sooner. [L1271.003]
- Give organic data time. Keyword and performance data fill in gradually after setup. [L0171.005, L0735.003]

### Older Procedure (2019)

The following procedure predates the current Search Console interface. It is kept as written.

1. Open Google Webmaster Tools (today Google Search Console) with the same login used for Google Analytics; it is free.
2. Review the report for every URL and any errors, and fix errors on the homepage that stop Google from showing it.
3. Submit the sitemap at yoursite.com/sitemap.xml so Google crawls and indexes every URL, including the homepage. [L0356.019]

## Keyword Research and Tools

### Tool stack and when to use each tool

- Google Keyword Planner is free with a Google Ads account and shows search volume, competition level and bid estimates. Use it to find high-volume, low-competition keywords for content [L0226.017, L1272.004]. Without a Google Ads account it is not needed for SEO [L1272.004].
- Keyword Planner is most useful for SEO keyword research in a niche, and its data can serve as a fallback if the main research tool is not working [L1097.038].
- Keyword Planner has a landing page option. Enter the URL of a competitor's or manufacturer's page and it returns keywords relevant to that page. Export the results into a spreadsheet to build a master keyword list [L0380.010].
- Keyword Planner numbers can lag behind Google Trends, so do not rely only on it for trending topics [L1120.035].
- Keyword Finder allows 2-3 free keyword searches per day. Paid access provides keyword suggestions, search volume, competition metrics, and shows how competitors rank for those keywords [L0226.018].
- Ubersuggest costs about $10 per month and is a cheap option for keyword research [L0758.006].
- Google and YouTube autosuggest are free and generate ideas from root terms [L0758.006].
- Google Ads search data from existing Google Shopping campaigns is a usable source of keywords [L0758.006].
- Freelancers on Fiverr can be hired to pull keywords [L0758.006].
- Answer the Public is a question-based keyword tool for finding the questions buyers ask [L0949.055].
- Alexa.com provides website traffic rankings, organic and paid traffic sources, keyword performance, backlink analysis and competitor performance metrics. Use it to assess micro-influencer websites before outreach and to analyse competitor traffic [L0226.015].
- SpyFoot.com, SimilarWeb.com and Alexa.com show where competitor traffic originates, which keywords competitors rank for, and their backlink sources. Free versions offer limited data; paid versions provide detailed insights [L0226.016].
- Ahrefs is the SEO research tool used for keyword and competitor research and has a free trial [L0801.086]. It is preferred over Keyword Planner for keyword research because Keyword Planner has hidden more data over time [L0382.016].
- Ahrefs Competing Domains and Content Gap show competitor keyword gaps [L0382.016].
- Ahrefs is expensive and is not needed in the early stage of a store. Invest in it once paid traffic is profitable enough to fund content work (older advice, 2021) [L0801.086].
- Paid suites such as Ahrefs, Moz and SEMrush are more accurate and show more data points, but are not necessary at the early stage (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.007].
- Cheap tools such as Ubersuggest and Google Keyword Planner are sufficient for content keyword research (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.007].

| Situation | Tool | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Early stage, tight budget | Ubersuggest, Keyword Planner, autosuggest | L0758.006, L0758.007 (older, 2021-22) |
| Paid traffic not yet profitable | Keyword Planner and free Google methods; Ahrefs only on free trial | L0801.086 (older, 2021) |
| Competitor keyword gaps | Ahrefs Competing Domains and Content Gap | L0382.016 |
| Niche SEO research with a Google Ads account | Keyword Planner | L1097.038 |

### Market settings and the data you need

- Set the country in every keyword tool to the market where customers are, for example English / United States for a US store or Australia for an Australian store, before pulling data (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.008, L0877.016].
- Search volume and difficulty differ by country, and data from the wrong country gives wrong numbers (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.008, L0877.016].
- At the content research stage you need only two data points per keyword: search volume in the country your customers search from, and search difficulty (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.008].
- Search difficulty is usually scored from 0 to 100 in tools such as Ubersuggest and Ahrefs. A higher score means harder to rank (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.009, L0877.008].
- Paid difficulty and average CPC are also reported in tools like Ubersuggest and are useful for paid ads (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.009].
- Extra metrics and backlink-level data are needed only later, when a specific page will be promoted with a link building campaign (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.031].

### Collecting keywords

#### Keyword research procedure

1. Broadly collect all relevant keywords in your market for your products, niche and the interests you serve (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.002, L0877.003].
2. Group the keywords into themes or topics (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.002, L0877.003].
3. Allocate the keywords to page types: content, collection or product page. Map them to specific pages as you build content (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.002, L0877.003].
4. Record everything in a living keyword research document that is easy to access and update, typically a spreadsheet (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.002, L0877.003].
- Before writing a content piece, decide which search term it should rank for, so it does not overlap with product or collection pages (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.002].
- Keyword research is needed for both content marketing and general SEO (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.002].

#### Starting from root terms

- Start with root keywords, meaning the broadest search terms relevant to your products. Run each root term separately through the keyword tool (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.015].
- Expand from three kinds of root terms: the product type (for example surfboards), the activity or use it serves (for example surfing), and each product line (for example wetsuits) (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.023].
- Activity and use terms bring in entertaining and problem-solving content ideas that product-type terms alone do not reveal (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.023].
- Run terms about the use or problem your product solves, such as a sport or activity, to get content ideas (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.015].
- Run every relevant root through the same export process (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.023].

#### Ubersuggest collection workflow

1. Enter a root keyword in Ubersuggest.
2. Open Keyword Ideas.
3. Export the list to CSV.
4. Repeat for every relevant root term and related root term. For a surf shop, this includes surfboards, surfing and wetsuits.
5. Keep only the terms that match the business (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.010].

#### Master sheet procedure

1. Paste all exported CSV files one after the other into one spreadsheet (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.011, L0877.018].
2. Sort all rows by search volume, highest first (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.018].
3. Remove terms with less than 100 searches per month (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.018].
4. Remove terms you will never rank for, such as *used* if you only sell new products (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.018].
5. Move the remaining terms to the Content, Collection or Product Page tab according to their intent (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.018].
- Sort by search volume and judge each term manually. Terms with zero or very low volume are ignored (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.011].
- Create separate tabs or categories for Content, Collection and Product Page, and move each term into the matching category (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.011].
- Exclude terms irrelevant to the business, such as used products when only new products are sold, or drawings when no art is sold (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.011].
- Some terms are not relevant to your business, such as *drawing* for a surfboard shop or *rentals* if you do not rent. Ignore them even if their volume and difficulty look attractive (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.026].

#### Grouping variations

- Term variations with the same meaning, such as *surfboards for sale* and *surfboards on sale*, or singular and plural, or word-order differences, should be treated as one theme. Combine their volumes for a realistic estimate, since ranking for one usually helps with the other. Treat them as one target instead of two (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.014, L0877.025].
- Keyword research action item for this stage: run the full research for the market and products, export the raw data to a spreadsheet, sort and sift it, and build a list of search terms to target with content (video, blog or other format). Keep the list updated as the program continues (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.018].

### Finding related keywords on Google

#### Related searches procedure

1. Search the focus keyword on google.com.
2. Scroll to the bottom of the first page of results.
3. Read the box labelled *Searches related to* for the most common variations.
4. Skip variations that name a competitor, for example a marketplace site (older advice, 2021-22) [L0881.015].
5. Copy each relevant related search into the worksheet as a related keyword (L1120.016).
6. Use the remaining relevant variations, such as adjectives like big, retro, hybrid or cheap, on the page, unless another page already targets that exact term (older advice, 2021-22) [L0881.015].
7. Repeat with the next keyword ideas if needed [L1120.016].

- Related searches are a free way to find keywords and variations [L1120.016, L0881.015].

#### Keyword groups

- The main keyword of a group is the keyword with the highest search volume found in Keyword Planner. Related keywords and variations are added to that main keyword to form a keyword group [L1120.015].
- Brand names such as IKEA are not targeted, because people searching a brand already know what they want and the brand is not yours. Variations with the prefix *best* can be used on collection pages [L1120.017].

### Competitor research

#### Competitor check on Google

1. Search the keyword on google.com.
2. Ignore the sponsored ads at the top and look only at the organic results.
3. Open the top results and note the page type, such as a product page, a collection page or a blog.
4. Note what each top page includes, so your own page can offer more value and rank better [L1120.018].

- Use competitor pages to see what already ranks, then build a better page with more useful content around the same keywords. Copying other sites' content is not part of this process [L1120.019].
- A competitor's top results also show which marketplaces, large retailers and specialist stores compete for the term [L1486.029].

#### Competitor blog topics

1. Search your niche keyword followed by the word *blog* on Google.
2. Open the top two ranked blogs.
3. Note the topics in their posts, including buying tips, news sections and older posts.
4. Add each relevant topic as a keyword in the worksheet [L1120.028].

- A competitor blog that looks unmaintained, is not fully functional and has not been updated for a long time is usually easier to outrank for its topics. Its older posts and news section still show which topics are covered and can be used as a list of ideas. Check these sections as well as the newest posts [L1120.040].

#### Ahrefs Content Gap procedure

1. In Ahrefs, open the Competing Domains tool and enter your domain. Look at the list sorted by common keywords and pick a competitor that is closely related to your store [L0382.015].
2. Open the Content Gap tool. Set the competitor as the target that ranks for keywords and your own site as the one that should not rank for them, so it shows keywords the competitor ranks for and you do not [L0382.015].
3. Enable the checkbox *At least one of the targets should rank in the top 10* so only keywords that the competitor ranks in the top 10 for appear [L0382.015].
4. Run the search and export the keyword list [L0382.015].
5. Vet each keyword with the RAW method (see Vetting keywords) [L0382.015].

- The Content Gap method produces a long list of article ideas that audiences already search for, because the keywords come from competitor rankings. Every keyword must still be vetted before writing an article for it [L0382.017].
- Keywords from competitor rankings are often irrelevant to your audience, such as brand names or product-specific terms of a competitor. Use judgement to remove keywords your buyers would not care about [L0382.018].

#### Ahrefs Keywords Explorer

- Research starts in Keywords Explorer. Its keyword ideas tabs are All keyword ideas, Phrase match, Having same terms, Also rank for, Search suggestions, Newly discovered and Questions (older advice, 2021-22) [L0878.009].
- Filters include KD, Volume, Word count, SERP features, Include, Exclude and More filters (older advice, 2021-22) [L0878.009].
- The Questions tab is an Ahrefs feature that other tools do not offer in the same way (older advice, 2021-22) [L0878.009].
- The SERP overview lists the top-ranking pages with Backlinks, Domains and Traffic columns. Clicking a page's backlink count opens that page's backlink report, which shows the linking sites to contact for outreach (older advice, 2021-22) [L0878.024].
- The Parent topic field shows the broader topic a keyword belongs to, with its own search volume and the #1 ranking page for that topic. Use it to check whether a niche term is broad enough to attract links, and to compare it with narrower variations (older advice, 2021-22) [L0878.027].

#### Ranking difficulty benchmarks from Ubersuggest

The GOOGLE SERP list in Ubersuggest's Keyword Overview shows the domains currently ranking in the top 10 for a keyword (older advice, 2021-22) [L1486.029].

| Keyword | Avg. backlinks (top 10) | Avg. domain authority (top 10) |
|---|---|---|
| 4x4 accessories | 536 | 56 |
| truck accessories | 513 | 59 |
| winches | 68 | 71 |

(older advice, 2021-22) [L1486.030]

### Vetting keywords

#### RAW vetting procedure

1. **Relevance:** Does your audience care about this keyword? Remove keywords specific to a competitor or that do not match what your buyers want. Keep keywords that answer a real question buyers have about your category [L0382.019].
2. **Achievability:** In Ahrefs, use Site Explorer to find the keywords your site already ranks for, add filters, export, and paste the list into Keyword Explorer to see keyword difficulty. Use the difficulty of those keywords as a benchmark for what you can rank for [L0382.019].
3. **Worth it:** Keep keywords with cost per click of at least $1 and a monthly search volume that matches your site size [L0382.019].

#### Difficulty

| Difficulty signal | Meaning | Applies when | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| KD below 20 in Ahrefs | Target for a store building its content presence | Store is building content | L0382.020 |
| Higher difficulty | Dominated by large brands such as big media companies | Competing with major brands | L0382.020 |
| About 45 | Moderately hard; needs backlinks to reach the top of page one | Planning a content page | L0758.015 (older, 2021-22) |
| Very low difficulty with some volume | Good target for a content page, relatively easy to rank for | Choosing content targets | L0758.016 (older, 2021-22) |

- Keyword difficulty of about 45 was the example for *surfboards for beginners*. It is not easy but not extremely hard to rank, and page one still requires backlinks, so it is a good candidate for an ultimate guide content page (older advice, 2021-22) [L0877.024].
- Very low difficulty with some volume, such as *surfboard volume calculator*, is a good target for a content page (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.016].
- Work on low-competition keywords with good search volume and a high CPC first, because they are the easiest to rank for. High-competition keywords are still worth creating content for later [L1120.020].

#### Cost per click

- Choose keywords with a cost per click of at least $1 in Ahrefs. A higher cost per click means advertisers consider the keyword profitable, so the traffic may be worth more [L0382.021].
- The $1 CPC rule is the worth-it check in the RAW method [L0382.019].
- Average CPC is also available in Ubersuggest and is useful for paid ads (older advice, 2021-22) [L0758.009].

#### Search volume

| Rule | Value | Applies when | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Minimum monthly volume for a small site | 500 average monthly searches | Small site | L0382.022 |
| Minimum monthly volume for a larger site | 1,000-2,000 average monthly searches per keyword | Larger site | L0382.022 |
| Common cut-off in the master sheet | 100 or more searches per month; below that is usually not worth including | Building the target list (older, 2021-22) | L0877.019, L0877.018 |
| Low-volume keywords with high purchase intent | 1,000 or less monthly searches still worthwhile | High purchase intent | L0278.011 |

- Keywords with lower monthly search volume (1,000 or less) are still worthwhile if they show high purchase intent. One thousand monthly searches of qualified traffic can yield significant sales over time [L0278.011].
- A store with a standing desk height calculator keyword showed 260 monthly searches, low competition and a $5.17 CPC, and it was a breakout term in Google Trends. Because it is evergreen, it was changed from a planned blog post to a permanent page [L1120.024].

#### Validating a category keyword

1. Enter a candidate keyword such as *non-toxic running shorts* into Ahrefs or SEMrush [L1389.014].
2. Check how many people search for it per month [L1389.014].
3. Create the category if monthly searches exist, and skip it if they do not [L1389.014].
4. Repeat the check monthly for hybrid keywords, since their volume can change [L1389.014].

### Long-tail versus broad terms

- Long-tail keywords reportedly make up over 70% of online searches, according to a Moz figure (older advice, 2021-22) [L0462.038].
- Longer queries, such as a product with size, colour and price, show a later buying stage than short broad queries (older advice, 2021-22) [L0462.038].
- Broad, generic niche keywords, for example the name of the whole niche, bring a lot of traffic but few sales. Ranking for them takes a lot of time and money, so a dropshipping store should not aim for them first [L0594.002].
- Long-tail keywords are the better organic target. They are easier to rank for and their searchers have buying intent, while broad keywords attract people who are only curious [L0594.003].
- Long-tail keywords include collection names (store subcategories), brand names the store sells, and combinations of product names, brand names and SKU numbers [L0594.003].
- A new store can start ranking sooner for search terms that name specific supplier brands or exact SKU numbers, because those searches are narrow and specific [L0586.006].
- For a single-brand store, most search volume goes to the brand name, while product-name and niche keywords often have little volume. Long-tail questions and categories are where extra traffic can come from (older advice, 2021-22) [L0949.056].
- Term variations and word-order differences may carry the same intent. Group them rather than treating each as a separate target [L0758.014, L0877.025].

### Search intent

- Choose informational long-tail queries such as how-to questions, setup questions and questions about choosing a product, since each one can become an article. For a podcast gear store, examples are *how to start a podcast*, *how to get a show listed on Apple Podcasts* and *which hosting service to choose* [L0380.012].
- Comparison keywords such as *X versus Y* and definition keywords such as *what does X mean* passed the relevance and value checks for a dropshipping store. These queries match buyers who are comparing options or learning category terms [L0382.056].
- Keywords for brands are not targeted; keywords with the prefix *best* can be used on collection pages [L1120.017].

### Question-based research

- Research the questions buyers ask before they buy, using a question-based keyword tool such as Answer the Public. Write content that answers each question and cover each stage of the buying journey, including material, colour and feature variants (older advice, 2021-22) [L0949.055].
- The Questions tab in Ahrefs Keywords Explorer provides question keywords (older advice, 2021-22) [L0878.009].
- Informational questions are the content targets for how-to, setup and product-choice pages [L0380.012].

### Year-stamped and trend keywords

- Search queries that include a year usually lag the current calendar year by about one year, so people may still type the previous year's version of a term. Before writing a headline or ad for a year-stamped topic, check which year the query actually uses and match it (older advice, 2021-22) [L0385.025].

#### Google Trends procedure

1. Go to trends.google.com [L1120.021].
2. Enter the niche keyword and click Explore [L1120.021].
3. Set the country to the country where you do business [L1120.021].
4. Set the time filter to Past 90 days to see what is relevant now [L1120.021].
5. Scroll down to see the trending searches and note any breakout terms that are doing better than ever before [L1120.021].

- Breakout search terms in Google Trends show a rise before Keyword Planner volumes catch up. Keyword Planner numbers for these terms can lag, so content can pay off early even when the planner shows low volume [L1120.022].
- Do not rely only on Keyword Planner for trending topics. Missing a breakout term while waiting for planner numbers means arriving late to the topic [L1120.035].

#### Verifying a breakout term in Keyword Planner

1. Copy the trending keyword from Google Trends [L1120.038].
2. Open Keyword Planner and go back to keyword ideas [L1120.038].
3. Search the copied keyword to get its average monthly searches, competition rank and high-range top-of-page bid [L1120.038].
4. Record these values in the worksheet even when search volume is low, because Keyword Planner numbers lag behind Google Trends [L1120.038].

### Search Console query data

#### Finding keywords to optimise

1. Open Performance and scroll down to the Queries report [L1271.012].
2. Sort queries by impressions to see which searches show your store most often [L1271.012].
3. Click a relevant query, for example a product name your store actually sells. This filters the graph to that query over the last three months [L1271.012].
4. Open the Pages tab to see which page ranked for that query and compare its impressions, average position and clicks [L1271.012].

- Search Console shows which queries get clicks, which Google Analytics hides as *not provided*. Connect it with the same Google account used for Analytics (2020 advice, older) [L0468.132].
- High-impression queries that do not match what the store sells, such as generic phrases about dropshipping or a category, bring impressions but almost no clicks. Optimise pages for queries that match the actual product, not for broad industry terms [L1271.013].

### Keyword worksheet

- The keyword research worksheet exists as a Google Sheets file, which can be copied into your own Google Drive, and as an Excel download for Excel, Apple Numbers or other spreadsheet software [L1120.042].
- Make your own copy before adding keywords so the original template stays unchanged [L1120.042].
- Organic keyword research finds topics that people in your niche already search for on Google or Bing, so you can create content that can rank for those terms. Keywords are stored in a tracking sheet, and that sheet becomes the basis for content planning [L1120.001].

## Keyword-to-Page Mapping and Search Intent

### Core rule: one keyword, one page

- Assign each target keyword to exactly one page on the site. Google generally shows only one result per website for a given query (branded searches are the usual exception), so several pages competing for one term split ranking signals, waste effort, and leave Google unsure which page is primary, so none ranks well. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0758.001, L0877.001, L0879.001, L1120.036]
- Pointing five or more pages at one search term is a common error, based on the belief that one of them will rank better. In practice, none of them ranks well. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0879.004]
- Ten pages each optimized for one term is the same error. Optimize one dedicated page per keyword and spread the rest of the keyword list across other pages. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.002]
- A single page with focused, deliberate keyword use has a much higher chance of reaching page one than many pages with scattered keyword targeting. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.003]
- Two pages aimed at the same keyword compete and weaken each other. Search engines treat them as duplicates, so keeping both splits relevance. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0949.050]
- Do not optimize the whole site around one keyword. Assign each keyword to one specific page, post, collection or product, with as little overlap as possible, so the site can rank for many keywords across different pages. [L1120.013]
- Do not put the same keyword on multiple pages. The overlap weakens the ranking of each page. [L1120.036]
- For a keyword with search volume, only one page from a site typically ranks on page one of Google, about 95% of the time. Consolidate similar pages rather than expecting several of them to rank on the same results page. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1465.028]

### Splitting the keyword list and mapping it to pages

- Split the keyword list into two groups. Category and product keywords are kept for category and product pages. Informational keywords go into the article list. Exception: a product keyword with strong search volume can also be used in product titles. [L0380.011]
- Map each keyword to a content type before the content is written. A product group or sub-category keyword becomes a Shopify collection. A comparison or educational keyword becomes a blog post. An evergreen tool, such as a calculator, becomes a page. [L1120.014]
- Content strategy for ranking keywords (Source A, 2025):
  1. Choose the main keyword (e.g., "hybrid surfboards").
  2. If the keyword is relevant to a product collection, make that collection page rank for the keyword.
  3. In blog posts and other content, link to the collection page.
  4. This focuses link authority and ranking power on the collection.
  [L0255.063]
- Keyword workflow from research to tracking (older, 2021-22):
  1. Build a large keyword list through keyword research, which can be outsourced to a freelancer for a spreadsheet.
  2. Select the keywords you want to target.
  3. Reduce them to a top 20 or top 50 list.
  4. Map each keyword to an existing page, or add a planned page to your SEO plan if no page fits.
  5. Track the ranking of the top keywords over time with a tool such as Ubersuggest, and watch organic traffic alongside it.
  [L0879.005]

### Choosing the page type by intent

- Search intent determines the page type. Commercial or non-branded product-type searches with no specific item in mind lead to collection or content pages. A precise product search leads to a product page. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.010]
- A shopper who searches a generic product type without a specific item in mind is better served by a collection or content page that shows options and guidance. Sending them straight to one product page forces extra navigation, and each extra click raises the chance they leave. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.023]
- Judge the user's intent behind each term, not only the product name. A broad term such as "surfboards with motors" is a middle-of-funnel search, so it fits a content or collection page rather than a single product page. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0877.020]
- Search intent must match the landing page. Informational queries should land on pages that answer the question. Queries for a named product model should land on a page about that exact product, not on a general category or content page. [L1099.002]
- For organic traffic, identify the search terms that trigger each page. If a page receives the wrong searches, either adapt the landing page to the terms that should bring buyers, or rank a separate page for those other terms. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0473.008]
- Map terms to page types by intent (older, 2021-22):
  - A term asking what kinds of a product exist, or how to choose one, such as "types of surfboards", fits a content piece.
  - A low-difficulty tool or calculator term with real volume, such as "surfboard volume calculator", fits a content piece.
  - A term for one specific model or product number fits a product page.
  - A broad group term for a buyer segment can go to a guide page, or to a collection page with added content.
  [L0877.033]
- Buying-intent pattern (older, 2021-22): combine the product category with a buying-intent modifier, such as "for sale", "best", or "top seven", plus the year. It works because people close to a purchase search with these modifiers, so pages built this way match high-intent searches. Two new examples in this pattern: "standing desk converters for sale" and "top seven espresso machines for 2026". [L0768.009]

### Checking the SERP before you write

- Before writing, check the top 10 Google results for a search term and note their format, such as product pages or guides. If most of the top results are product pages, a product page is needed to compete for that keyword. [L0382.023]
- Pattern matching: the page you publish must look like the pages Google already ranks for that keyword. Transactional product or collection pages do not rank for informational queries, and blog posts do not rank for commercial queries. Check the top 10 results first to see which page type wins. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L0883.005]
- Check search intent with a Google search before choosing the page type (older, 2021-22):
  1. Search the target keyword in Google.
  2. Look at the first page of results (the first five results are used as the reference).
  3. If most results are product pages, Google sees commercial intent, so target a product page with the best matching product.
  4. If most results are content pages, Google sees informational intent, so create a content page for the keyword.
  [L0881.014]
- Choosing between a collection page and a content page (older, 2021-22):
  1. Search the keyword in Google and review the results on the first page.
  2. If the first page is mostly collection pages, Google wants to show product listings, so a collection page fits.
  3. If the first page is mostly content pages such as listicles, build a content page that is better than the existing results.
  4. Check the backlink counts of the top results in Ahrefs or Ubersuggest.
  5. If the top results have many backlinks, choose a content page, because backlinks are easier to earn for content than for collection pages.
  [L0879.003]
- Manual SERP check to confirm a keyword tool's SERP picture (older, 2021-22):
  1. Search the candidate keyword in a normal Google search with the same country setting used in the keyword tool.
  2. Compare the organic results with the SERP overview from the keyword tool.
  3. Confirm that most top results are informational articles with many backlinks, not store, government or navigational pages.
  4. Keep the topic only if the manual search confirms the tool's SERP picture.
  [L0878.029]
- For a "best [product]" search term, check the first page of results. If most results are listicles, a single product page is unlikely to rank. Target that term with a listicle and place your own product at the top. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0949.067]

Case, product-group keyword "L-shaped standing desk" (Source A): the team checked the organic results and ignored sponsored ads. The first organic result was a manufacturer's product page optimized for the keyword. The second was an Amazon page that looked like a product but was a collection of L-shaped standing desks. A Wayfair result, reached through its brand section, was also a collection page for L-shaped standing desks. Because the top pages for this product-group term were mostly collection pages, the keyword was assigned to a Shopify collection rather than a blog post. [L1120.039]

### Product pages

- Product pages should target brand name, product name and SKU terms. These have little competition and often rank in the top three with minimal work. Category and blog pages should keep targeting their own broader topics. (Source A, 2023-2024.) [L0855.149, L0965.080]
