- Product pages target branded terms, specific product names, model names or product numbers, and very specific product-type searches that one product can fully answer. Do not assign broad terms to product pages. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0758.005, L0877.006]
- Product pages rarely rank for anything beyond the product name, because Google reads the product name first from the page title. For broader terms, use a blog, content page or collection page instead. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0879.002]
- Ranking a product page for a broad product-type term is much harder than ranking a content piece for it, although it is not impossible. Product pages should therefore be assigned only narrow, specific search terms. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0877.007]
- Product pages are poor targets for broad, non-branded search terms. Many other pages on the site also use those terms, and a product page has limited text space to optimize for them. A product page works best for terms tied to its own title, model or part number, and brand plus product type. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.022]
- Product pages can rank for a brand name plus a specific product type, such as a brand and model-type combination, but not for the brand name alone. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.008]
- Broad group terms with many suitable products are not product-page targets. A term such as "surfboards with motors" is mid-funnel and belongs on a content or collection page. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0758.012]
- Do not send a search for a broad group, such as "surfboards for beginners", to one specific product page. If the shop holds dozens of matching products, the chance that one page is the right product for that searcher is very low. A visitor may leave and not return if the page does not match. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0877.021, L0758.012]
- A term that matches one or several products is best left off the product-page list when the business has many suitable items. Such terms belong on content or collection pages. Product pages are reserved for brand names, model names, product numbers and very specific single-product searches. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0758.025]
- On e-commerce sites with more than about 50 products, especially grouped in a few product types with 30 or more items each, product pages should target specific keywords: product title, model or part number, or brand plus product type, not broad generic terms. On sites with only one or two products, a more liberal keyword approach is acceptable. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.006]
- Keyword-to-product exception: a product keyword with strong search volume can also be used in product titles. [L0380.011]

### Collection pages

- Collection pages target broad product-type terms, brand names, and specific product-type terms. They suit users looking for a type of product, or a brand, that has a selection of products. Competitive collection-page terms need substantial written content on the page to rank. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0758.004, L0877.005]
- Collection or category pages suit product-type keywords and, for resellers, brand names or brand plus product type combinations. They are the right page when a shopper searches for a type of product without knowing which exact item they want. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.009]
- For a product-group term where the top organic results are mostly collection pages, assign a Shopify collection rather than a blog post. [L1120.039]

### Content pages

- Content pages target informational terms, problem-based terms (searches where the user seems to be looking for an answer to a problem), and broad product-type and product-use terms (what a product is used for). (Older, 2021-22.) [L0758.003, L0877.004]
- Content pages such as blog posts, guides and resource pages suit informational keywords (for example, how to choose a product), broad product-type keywords, and specific product-type keywords. Collection and product pages are not suited to informational queries. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.011]
- Content pages allow longer text, so they can hold more keyword variations and synonyms and can go deeper on a term than a collection page. A content page can reach about 10 or more keyword occurrences when the post runs around 3,000 words. A product page with little text usually needs only 2 to 3 mentions beyond the first 100 words. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.012]
- Deep informational targeting, such as how-to guides or buying advice, belongs on content pages. Collection pages carry some content, but within a tighter limit. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.025]
- Prefer content pages over collection pages for broad, educational terms, such as "surfboards for beginners". Content pages attract backlinks, while collection and product pages rarely do. Link acquisition is needed for competitive terms, and content is the most practical page type for earning links. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0758.013]
- Broad, high-volume product-type keywords are more competitive and need high-quality content and backlinks to rank. Content pages are the usual choice because they attract backlinks more easily than product pages. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.013]
- When a broad "for beginners" term fits many products, a content page such as an ultimate guide works better than a product or collection page. The guide should explain which product type the visitor needs, then link to the matching collection or specific product pages. If a collection page is used instead, it still needs written content to rank. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0758.024]
- For a term that asks for guidance, create one ultimate guide page that educates the visitor on the right product choice, then link from it to the matching collection or product pages. If no content piece is planned, a collection page for the same term is the fallback. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0877.022]
- Lower-volume variations of a head term, such as adjective versions like "hybrid", "retro" or "cheap", can sit on the same page when no other page targets that exact phrase. This lets one page rank for many terms, each with smaller search volume. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.024]
- Example: a laptop stand for desk keyword (14,800 monthly searches, high competition, $2.16 CPC) can support a blog post comparing a laptop stand with an adjustable-height desk, even if the store does not sell laptop stands. A related keyword about ergonomics can support a second post comparing an ergonomic laptop stand with a standing desk. (Source A.) [L1120.034]

### Keyword placement on a page

- Google uses on-page text (headings, body and the words visible on the page) as its most basic relevance signal, so a page meant to rank for a term should contain that term. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.004]
- A target keyword should appear roughly 5 to 10 times across the whole page, counting every on-page element: title, product or post heading, image alt text, H2 subheadings and body copy, not just the body text. The range exists because a page has many places where keywords can sit. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.001]
- Use synonyms and close variations of the focus keyword to avoid repeating the exact phrase too often. If the exact phrase would have to appear more than about 10 times, switch to close variations that no other page on the site is targeting. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.016]
- Keyword placement workflow for a new page (older, 2021-22):
  1. Start from the keyword research list and pick one focus keyword for the page.
  2. Confirm the page type fits the keyword intent (product, collection or content page).
  3. Place the focus keyword in the title tag, ideally at the front without making the title read awkwardly.
  4. Place it in the first 100 words of the body text, plus 2 to 3 more occurrences depending on the page length.
  5. Use it in at least one H2 subheading (not every H2 needs it).
  6. Use it in at least one image file name.
  7. Use it in at least one image alt text.
  8. Include it in the page URL.
  9. Include it in the meta title.
  10. Include it in the meta description.
  11. Check the total count stays within about 5 to 10 occurrences across the page, and use synonyms or close variations for further mentions.
  [L0881.005]

### Reviewing existing pages in Search Console

- Case, hoodie store (Source A): the store checked Google Search Console for its product queries over the last three months. The query "Source A hoodie" had 88 total impressions with an average organic position of 5. A unisex zip hoodie page appeared for this query with 85 impressions at position 5 but received zero clicks, which made it the page to optimize first for that keyword. [L1271.014]

### Mapping an existing site to keywords

- Mapping an existing large site (older, 2021-22):
  1. Map each page to one keyword it should target.
  2. Start with the most important pages.
  3. Make sure no other page targets the same keyword.
  4. Work through the rest of the site gradually, since this cannot be done overnight on a large site.
  [L0881.020]
- Case, surf shop (older, 2021-22): the shop has about 30 surfboard variations across brands, sizes and configurations, and one product-type term appears on many of its pages. Choose one page for that head term, preferably a content or collection page rather than a product page, and use the term 5 to 10 times on that page only. Other pages keep it at one or two uses, so Google can see the chosen page as the most relevant match for the term. [L0881.021]
- Among very similar product pages that compete for the same keywords, choose one as the main page and point more internal links to it. This tells search engines which page to rank and stops them from splitting ranking power across similar pages. (Older, 2021-22.) [L1465.027]
- Link content to the collection it should rank for. Blog posts and other content should link to the collection page, which focuses link authority and ranking power on the collection. [L0255.063]

### Merging competing pages

- Merging competing blog posts (older, 2021-22):
  1. Identify the posts that target the same keyword.
  2. Choose the stronger post to keep as the winner.
  3. If the weaker post has no links and no authority, delete it and redirect its URL to the winner.
  4. Copy any useful information from the deleted post into the winner.
  5. If the weaker post has links and authority, reassign it to a different keyword instead of deleting it.
  6. If the content fits another term, update the post for that term rather than deleting it.
  [L0949.051]

### Category mapping and topical authority

- A 12-month content plan should aim at topical authority: becoming the go-to source for one subject area. Topical authority is built over time through a connected set of pages, not single articles. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.002]
- One product can belong to several categories at once. A men's black running short, for example, fits men's running shorts, black running shorts, and running shorts with pockets. A category for each relevant search variant lets the store target more specific keywords and reach shoppers who search for exactly that item. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.003]
- Broad head terms, such as "running shorts", face heavy competition and often attract shoppers with a different intent, such as a woman searching for women's products. Specific category terms are easier to rank for faster and convert better, which brings sales sooner. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.004]
- Build a mix of broad parent categories and narrower niche categories, with narrow ones based on specific use cases. Furniture example: sectional categories for piece count (two-piece, three-piece, four-piece), color (brown, leather, black, canvas, white), and room (living room sectionals). A single product can sit in several of these at once. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.017]
- Attribute categories pair a product kind with one or more traits, such as shade, cut, or purpose. In clothing, the structure runs gender, then product, then trait:
  - men's running shorts with pockets
  - men's running shorts with a lining
  - men's running shorts without a lining
  - men's compression running shorts
  - women's running shorts with a high waist

  For a swimwear shop, the same structure yields women's high-waisted swim bottoms with ruching and men's quick-dry board shorts with a zip pocket. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.018]
- Granular category pages capture shoppers with very specific searches, and these shoppers are more likely to buy. Most competitors do not build pages at this level of detail, so the store gains a ranking and sales advantage. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.019]
- Blog articles or Shopify pages can both serve as topical authority content, and either format works. Each article should link to the relevant collection pages, since that internal linking is how the collections are ranked. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.022]
- Hybrid categories, such as a non-toxic version of a product, are only worth creating if people actually search for them on Google. SEO captures existing demand rather than creating it, so a hybrid category without search volume will gain little traction. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.012]
- Claim-type modifiers, such as non-toxic or PFAS-free, can be tested as hybrid categories at the broad product level, for example "non-toxic running clothes", rather than at a narrow level such as "non-toxic running tights". Narrow claim-plus-product combinations often show too little monthly search volume on Google, so check the volume before creating them. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.025]
- Map out every category you can think of, including an authority cluster for each, while skipping top-of-funnel and AI Overview-type queries. [L1389.011]
- Leave out top-of-funnel terms that everyone already understands, such as the basic product name in its most obvious form. Also leave out query types that an AI Overview would likely answer directly, because articles on those queries rarely earn clicks. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.005]
- Put the exclusion rules into the prompt from the start, such as telling the AI to skip AI Overview-type queries and top-of-funnel terms. Do not let the AI generate every query and then delete the unwanted ones afterward. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.006]
- Give the AI a list of direct competitors when building the map, since better input produces better output and speeds up the work. Choose competitors you actually want to model your categories on. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.007]
- Firecrawl can scrape competitor websites so that their category structure and page content can be analyzed. The scraped data serves as input for mapping the competitor's categories. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.008]
- Before creating pages, remove any category you do not stock or sell. Example: if the store does not sell high-waisted running leggings, drop that category from the map. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.021]
- Once the category list is cleaned, put the final map into one shared document in a fixed format, so the whole team can read and review it before any collection page copy is written. This document is the handover point between the category map and the content work. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.024]
- Category mapping procedure (Source E, 2025-26):
  1. Map out every category you can think of, including an authority cluster for each one, while skipping top-of-funnel and AI Overview-type queries.
  2. Give the AI your chosen competitors and have it map the categories against them.
  3. Refine and trim the map inside the chat.
  4. Validate each category's search demand with a keyword tool.
  5. Remove the categories you do not sell.
  6. Export the cleaned map.
  7. Create a collection page for each remaining category.
  8. Create blog articles or Shopify pages that link to these collections to build topical authority.
  [L1389.011]
- AI-generated map exports can contain multiple tabs and hundreds of rows with hallucinated, wrong, or irrelevant entries. Do the analysis and trimming inside the chat first, then export a cleaned version instead of reviewing a raw spreadsheet. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1389.009]
- Case, hypothetical running brand (Source E, 2025-26): the brand had hundreds of non-toxic SKUs across all running subcategories, and the category mapping was tested on them. The competitors came from an athleisure strategy built a month earlier, with Happier Earth and Allware named as the main examples, and the AI was asked to map categories against them. The lesson reports no traffic or sales result for this map, only the planning setup. [L1389.026]

### Pitfalls

- Do not optimize one page around a single item from a set of similar topics, such as one fun fact out of many. Create separate content for each item instead, because a rewrite around one sub-topic can move rankings backward. (Source E, 2025-26.) [L1269.021]
- Common mistake: keyword stuffing across many pages. Keep one dedicated page per keyword and use the rest of the list on other pages. (Older, 2021-22.) [L0881.002, L0879.004]

#### Further details

- Product pages should rank for product-specific terms such as a model name, but not for broad category terms that are very hard to rank for on a product page. Broad terms are targeted with separate content pages that link to the product page. [L0396.028]

## Site Structure, Navigation and Internal Linking

### Site structure and link depth

- A basic site structure should be flat rather than deep, and it should contain no orphan pages. An orphan page is a page that no internal link includes in the site structure. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.003]
- In a flat structure, no page should be more than four internal links away from the home page. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.004]
- Treat the home page as the hub of the site. Aim for three or even two links to key pages if the structure still makes sense for shoppers, but four links is the maximum, not the target. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.005]
- Count links, not clicks, when measuring depth. Google follows links and does not click menus, so a mega menu that displays subcategories on the same page still counts each step as a link. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.006]
- Mega menus can make a deep hierarchy look shallow because all subcategories appear without a click. Collections nested inside collections inside a mega menu add links that search engines follow, so check the link count instead of the number of visible clicks. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.007]
- The right depth is not a fixed number. It depends on the nature of the products and how customers expect them to be organized, so a flat structure must still make sense from a shopper's point of view. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.009]
- Site structure should be laid out so that visitors and search engine crawlers can both move through it easily. [L1227.026]
- Link value passes from a page that receives an external backlink to the pages it links to, so a backlink to one page benefits the linked pages beneath it. Site structure determines how far that value reaches. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.002]
- Internal site structure matters for SEO for three reasons: it helps search engine crawlers move through the site and discover new pages, it lets link value from backlinked pages flow to other pages, and it helps shoppers find products, which indirectly supports SEO through better user engagement. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.001]

**Audit procedure to flatten the structure** (older, 2021-22) [L1465.008]

1. Go through the product pages, which usually sit at the bottom of the structure.
2. For each product page, count the internal links needed to reach it from the home page, including links inside mega menus.
3. Flag every product page that needs more than four links.
4. Restructure menus and collections so each flagged page is reached in four links or fewer, and try for three or two where the product organization still makes sense for customers.

### Orphan pages

- Orphan pages are pages with no internal links pointing to them, which makes them hard for crawlers to find and less likely to be crawled. Orphan pages should generally be avoided, although there are exceptions where they are intentional. (older, 2021-22 for Source B; the exception wording is from Source B 2021-22) [L0397.010]
- A page that receives no internal links, and is not reachable from the main menu or homepage, is an orphan page. Search engines find and interpret orphan pages poorly, so every blog or collection needs at least one internal link from the site structure. [L1269.006]
- Orphan pages are pages with no internal links pointing to them, often created for a single ad or search term. If still needed, add at least one internal link so Google can crawl them; if not needed, delete and redirect to the nearest relevant collection page; if they should stay out of search, noindex them. (older, 2021-22) [L1372.015]
- Orphan pages can only be discovered by search engine crawlers through the sitemap or through external backlinks, and visitors cannot reach them from any other page on the site. Each orphan page therefore needs either an internal link or a deletion with redirect. (older, 2021-22) [L1372.030]
- On e-commerce sites with many products, orphan pages are typically collection or category pages, but they can also be product pages, opt-in landing pages for a lead magnet, or admin pages. Orphan pages mess up site structure and crawling and are harder for both shoppers and search engines to find, so avoid them. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.010]
- Creating collection pages only as landing pages for paid ads (for example Google search text ads or Facebook ads) and leaving them out of all navigation creates orphan pages. Even a landing page should get at least one internal link from another page on the site. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.011]

**Fix options for an orphan page** (older, 2021-22) [L1465.012]

1. Add the page to the main menu.
2. Add it as a sub-collection inside an existing collection.
3. Add at least one link to it from another page on the site, such as a content page.

- A page audit in an SEO tool such as Ahrefs or Raven Tools finds orphan pages automatically. Fixing them is a medium-priority task rather than an urgent one, but it is worth doing. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.013]

### Navigation and main menu

- Every product must belong to at least one collection and be reachable from the main navigation, because customers browsing the site would otherwise never find it. Linking products into the navigation also helps search engines reach every page. (older, 2021-22) [L1145.039]
- Put the most important pages in the main menu so they receive internal links from the home page. Ensure they are linked there as well as from other places. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.020]
- A well-planned main menu creates many internal links from the home page. Over time the home page usually collects the most backlinks, because people who discover a business often link to its home page, and its link value flows out through the main menu. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.019]
- A high-value collection page should be featured in the main menu. Sub-collections can also link back up to their parent collection to add internal links. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.038]
- Do not label main menu items generic terms such as Shop or Catalog. Use product types and keywords that customers search for, such as the specific product names of your store, because generic labels lead to long link dumps that help neither customers nor SEO. (older, 2021-22) [L1303.017]
- Blog pages are usually added after a store starts growing, because they serve informational purposes. Create the blog post, then add its link to the navigation like any other page. [L1326.022]
- Keep the home link in the navigation, since most sites link to their homepage and it does not hurt search rankings. Users click the logo, so a separate home link is optional. [L0964.134]

### Breadcrumbs

- On Shopify, breadcrumb navigation is a site-wide setting that is either turned on or off in the theme; on other platforms it may need to be implemented page by page. (older, 2021-22) [L1191.002]
- Breadcrumb navigation helps customers find their way and also builds internal links quickly, which search engines value. (older, 2021-22) [L1191.003]
- Breadcrumbs can be added to a Shopify store because product categories already exist in the store. Their SEO impact was uncertain at the time of the call, so test before relying on them. (Source A, 2023) [L0835.095]
- **Where the sources differ:** Source A (2023) calls the SEO effect uncertain and recommends testing; Source B (2021-22) treats breadcrumbs as a quick way to build internal links. Both agree on the customer benefit. Enable them, then measure before counting on an SEO gain. [L0835.095, L1191.003]

### Internal link types, anchor text and placement

- Internal links come in two types: navigational links, which come from menus and site navigation, and contextual links, which sit inside the body text of pages such as blog posts. A site needs both types to get the best SEO results. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.014]
- Contextual links help search engines discover new content and understand which pages are related. If several articles on one sub-topic link to each other, search engines treat the site as more authoritative on that topic. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.015]
- Internal links between a store's own pages also help Google judge the site's context and relevance, and they are a separate lever from external backlinks. Both need to be planned, since strong external links do not replace a sound internal link structure. (older, 2021-22) [L0944.009]
- Link to related blog posts, product pages and other pages to keep readers on the site. Suggested-post blocks at the bottom of articles, added with an app, are one way to do this. (Source A) [L0382.029]
- Search engines treat pages with many internal links pointing to them as more important than pages with few. Use this to signal which pages matter most and to give those pages more attention. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.016]
- The more internal links a page receives, the more important search engines consider it and the more context they have about it. Revenue-driving bottom-of-funnel product and collection pages should therefore receive the most internal links, while blogs should not be treated as equally important. [L1269.010]
- Link value also flows through contextual links to other pages. Link from content pages to the commercially important pages, such as best-selling products, popular collections, and products with better margins, rather than to pages that add little business value. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.018]
- Do not point many internal links at low-value pages, such as admin pages linked from every page, because search engines may treat them as important. If such a link must exist, make it a nofollow link. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.017]
- Do not put internal links to low-value pages, such as admin pages or products that rarely sell and do not convert. Instead, decide which pages are most important and build a strategy to link to them more than to other pages. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.026]
- Place the focus keyword in one or two internal links per page. Do not stuff keywords into every anchor text, because search engines read the text around a link as well as the anchor text itself. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.023]
- Over-optimized anchor text, such as keywords in every internal link on a page, looks artificial and can hurt rankings instead of helping them. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.024]
- Internal links between related product pages are acceptable where they make sense. Place them near the bottom of the product description rather than early in the copy. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.025]
- **Where the sources differ on product-to-product links:** Source E (2025-26) sets the default rule that products must not link to other products, as part of the automation rules (see below). Source B (2021-22, older) allows related product links placed near the bottom of the description. Apply the stricter Source E rule to automated linking, and treat Source B's placement advice as the reference for manual links written into a product description. [L1269.011, L1465.025]

### Links by funnel level and cluster building

- Do not link from product or collection pages to top-of-funnel blog articles that only answer general questions, because that moves shoppers away from checkout. Links that answer a purchase question on the page itself, such as a sizing guide on a product page, keep shoppers closer to buying. [L1269.008]
- Internal link priority by funnel level:

| Funnel level | Typical pages | Internal link share |
|---|---|---|
| Top of funnel | Informational blog articles | Smallest |
| Middle of funnel | Guides and category-style blog articles, middle-funnel collections | Medium |
| Bottom of funnel | Product pages and collection pages | Largest share, clearly more than every other level |

  Links should flow from top to bottom of the funnel, not in reverse. [L1269.009]
- Use 3 to 5 internal links on each collection page, placed as a mini navigation. On each blog post, build a couple of internal links to collection pages or other blog posts so content forms clusters. [L0883.023]
- Plan URLs before writing: list the planned collection URLs (for example /collections/men's-running-shorts) and blog URLs, including pages that do not exist yet. Give that list to the writing prompt so every new page links to the planned pages, which builds clusters as content is produced. [L0883.024]
- Linking from an older page that already gets daily traffic to a new, related page gives the new page two sources of visitors: link equity from the older page's backlinks and readers who click through from the existing link. This makes internal linking from high-traffic old posts a practical way to kick-start a new page. (Source B, 2021-22) [L0385.028]
- Internal linking is the first priority in an SEO clean-up because it shows search engines which pages matter and how they relate to each other. Pages without solid internal links are poorly understood and rarely rank. [L1269.005]
- Both zero internal linking and excessive, random internal linking hurt SEO. Zero linking leaves pages disconnected, while linking wherever possible dilutes the signal of which pages are important. [L1269.007]

### Cornerstone content

- Cornerstone content, also called pillar content, is a small set of pages at the heart of the business that are most valuable either financially or as the entry point to a competitive, high-volume search term. These pages are built to rank at the top of page one. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.029]
- The cornerstone strategy is an advanced SEO method for competitive keywords against established sites. Most sites skip it, so it can help a newer site overtake them. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.034]

**Cornerstone strategy steps** (older, 2021-22) [L1465.030]

1. Identify the content pages that sit at the heart of what the business is about.
2. Include those pages in the main navigation, not only in a blog or resources section.
3. Feature those pages on the home page.
4. Send as many internal links as possible from other content and collection pages to the cornerstone content.
5. Build four or five smaller content pieces on related topics, link each of them to the cornerstone content, and build external backlinks to each smaller piece.
6. Build external backlinks directly to the cornerstone content as well.

- A surfboard shop could make an ultimate guide to surfboards its cornerstone page, aimed at a broad keyword that gets tens or even hundreds of thousands of searches a month. The page is large, detailed, and comprehensive, and it sends traffic that can later be retargeted or added to an email list. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.031]
- Link flow in a cornerstone structure runs upward: supporting pages link up to the cornerstone page. Do not link the cornerstone page back down to them, so that it keeps more internal links than most other pages on the site. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.032]
- Supporting pages on related angles let you build many more backlinks in total than one page can receive. Their link value flows into the cornerstone page, which raises its page authority or page rank far beyond what direct backlinks alone would achieve. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.033]
- A cornerstone guide on a high-ticket product can link to sub-guides split by budget, such as best options under $10,000 and under $5,000. This lets one hub page cover several related search intents at different price points. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.043]
- Cornerstone pages in the main menu also serve shoppers who browse the navigation rather than search for a specific product. Those shoppers are usually in the middle of the funnel, so these pages can support conversion as well as SEO. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.040]

**Plan cornerstone content from the content plan** (older, 2021-22) [L1465.035]

1. Review the content plan and identify which pages will be the cornerstone content. There can be more than one.
2. Decide which other content pages will be created and receive backlinks, and link them into the cornerstone content.
3. Decide which collection pages should link into the cornerstone content.

- A collection page can serve as cornerstone content for a high-volume, competitive keyword. Backlinks to collection pages are hard to build, so link to them heavily from content pages that do receive backlinks. (older, 2021-22) [L1465.036]

**Case: Rain or Shine Golf, cornerstone structure in 2020** (older, 2021-22) [L1465.039]

Rain or Shine Golf, a golf simulator and accessories store, used a cornerstone structure in 2020. Its main menu showed its main product types, brands, and resource pages, and product drop-downs led to collections, which is the usual setup. Several individual high-priority products were also placed at the top level of the menu, such as the SkyTrak Practice Golf Simulator Package at $3,399.00, the SkyTrak SwingBay Golf Simulator at $7,299.00 (compare-at $7,799.00), and the Uneekor QED SwingBay Golf Simulator at $11,299.00.

The store also placed several guides in the main menu, including Best Golf Simulators of 2020, Best Golf Launch Monitors, and Best Golf Hitting Mats, plus a Best Golf Simulator Screens and Enclosures guide. These guides target keywords with high search volume that shoppers use early in their buying journey.

A Resources page collected dozens of content pages, and many of these pages linked into the few guides in the main menu. The guides also linked to collection and product pages, giving the store a pyramid of internal links.

The guide page opens by saying a home simulator once cost a $20,000 minimum and can now be had for well under $10,000, or even $5,000. It then lists links to sub-guides such as Best Indoor Home Golf Simulator Under $10,000 and Best Golf Simulators Under $5,000.

The coach says the approach gets good traffic and sales results for the store, and the cornerstone pages also serve shoppers in the middle of the funnel who browse the menu rather than searching for a product.

### Internal link automation and review

- Define explicit internal linking rules before any automation: blog URLs may link to other blog URLs or to collections, collection URLs may link to other collections, and products must not link to other products. Four or five such rules are enough to control a store's link structure. [L1269.011]
- In one audit of 300 AI-suggested internal links from an export of 300 blog articles, about 100 suggestions were usable as they were. About 200 were invalid: the URL existed but the referenced content did not, or the suggested link was poor. This shows AI link suggestions need a manual check before anything goes live. [L1269.012]

**Safer workflow for internal link suggestions** [L1269.013]

1. Crawl or export the relevant pages with their URLs and content.
2. Let the AI propose candidate internal links from existing content, based on your linking rules.
3. Review every candidate manually and mark it as green light, needs edit, or delete.
4. Apply only the approved links, and only after the review is complete.

- Small teams and solo operators can accept more automation for internal links, but the AI should still never publish link changes unreviewed. Even with a small site, check suggested links for hallucinated content before applying them. [L1269.014]

#### Further details

- Internal links show search engines how the site is structured and which pages cover which subjects. They are also a way to move ranking value between pages. [L0462.014]

- Backlinks to a content page pass part of their value through its internal links to other pages. A content page with many backlinks that links to a collection page can lift that collection page even if the collection page has no backlinks itself. [L0462.015]

## Technical SEO: Crawling, Indexing, Speed and Mobile

### Technical SEO: Role in the Overall Process

- Technical SEO covers the work that lets search engine crawlers reach, understand and index a website. If a crawler cannot properly access a site, the quality of on-page content or on-page SEO does not matter, because the pages will never appear in search results. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1362.001]
- Technical SEO is the first layer to fix before on-page SEO and content work, since indexing is a prerequisite for ranking. Crawlers also decide which pages belong in the index, so some pages should be kept out of it on purpose. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1362.002]
- Fixing access and crawl problems is part of crawl optimization, not just a separate technical issue. If the crawler has trouble accessing pages, it may cover only part of a site on a given visit, leaving some pages uncrawled until a later pass. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1333.013 is not in scope; see L1362.001] [L1397.013]

### Crawl Budget

- Search engines allocate a limited amount of crawl time and a limited number of pages per visit, so they rarely crawl an entire large site in one pass. Pages can be crawled incompletely or skipped, which delays indexing of new pages and updates. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.001]
- Crawl budget only matters for larger sites, such as e-commerce stores with hundreds, thousands or tens of thousands of pages. A site with about five pages is crawled completely on every visit and needs no crawl budget work. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.002]
- If a large site contains many low-value pages, the crawler may spend its budget on them and miss the optimized, high-priority pages. The crawler does not follow your priorities by default, so the goal is to make its time as efficient as possible. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.003]
- Low-value pages that absorb crawl time can push well-optimized pages out of a crawl. Because the same pattern tends to repeat on each later visit, priority pages may be indexed late or missed for a long period. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.012]
- You cannot increase, reduce or see the exact value of a site's crawl budget. The only lever is to remove obstacles so the crawler works more efficiently. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.004]
- The overall aim of crawl optimization is to make sure crawl time goes to pages that matter for SEO, and that the site is easy to navigate for crawlers. Pages that add no value should not consume crawl time that better pages need. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.011]

#### Factors That Make Crawling More Efficient

- **Internal links:** They give crawlers paths between pages and help them understand site structure. Few internal links leave some pages hard to reach and less likely to be found. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.006]
- **Flat structure:** A flat site structure, where important pages are reachable within few clicks from the main navigation, makes crawling considerably easier. Site architecture and menu structure both influence how effectively a site is crawled. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.007]
- **Page speed:** Slow page speed increases the time a crawler needs to get through a site, so faster-loading pages let more of the site be crawled in the same budget. Slow loading and slow-to-process pages both lengthen each crawl pass, so a site with heavy pages gets fewer pages crawled in the same time budget. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.005, L0397.014]
- **Redirect batches:** Large batches of redirects, for example from mass URL changes that were not strictly necessary, slow down crawling. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.008]

### Site Audits and Indexation Checks

#### Free Crawlers

- Screaming Frog SEO Spider is a free website crawler for up to 500 URLs, useful for on-page SEO audits. Xenu's Link Sleuth is another free, similar crawler. [L0575.017]

#### Checking Indexation With a Site Search

1. Search `site:yourdomain.com` in Google.
2. Check the number of indexed pages:
   - The count should roughly match the number of pages on your site.
   - Too few suggests an indexation issue, such as a robots.txt or robots meta tag problem.
   - Too many suggests a canonical problem. This is common on large CMSs such as Magento or self-hosted platforms and less common on Shopify.
3. Check that title tags are not missing, duplicated or unprofessional.
4. Check that product titles and descriptions are optimized. [L0575.019]

#### Ahrefs Site Audit

- The Ahrefs Site Audit report menu includes Internal pages, Indexability, Links, Redirects, On page, Social tags, Duplicate content, Localization, Performance, Images, JavaScript, CSS and External pages. For finding thin and duplicate pages, the Indexability and Duplicate content reports are the relevant ones. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1372.028]
- The audit overview shows Health Score (share of internal URLs without errors), Issues distribution (errors, warnings, notices) and Top issues. Top issues include Orphan page (no incoming internal links), 404 page, 4XX page, Meta description tag missing or empty, Page has links to broken page, Page has no outgoing links, Title tag missing or empty, Duplicate pages without canonical and Redirected page has no incoming internal links. Click View all issues and then View affected URLs to export the list. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1372.020]
- Procedure for working through an audit:
  1. Open the Ahrefs site audit overview and read the Health Score, Issues distribution and Top issues.
  2. Click View all issues.
  3. Open the issue you need, for example Orphan page (has no incoming internal links).
  4. Click View affected URLs.
  5. Export the list of affected URLs and their values.
  6. Paste the export into the audit spreadsheet, then check each URL for internal links or content and fix it. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1372.029]
- Example audit of a drop-shipping store with about 5,337 internal URLs crawled. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1372.021]

| Finding | Count |
|---|---|
| Duplicate pages without canonical | 49 |
| Orphan pages (no incoming internal links) | 330 |
| 404 pages | 275 |
| Pages with missing or empty meta descriptions | 248 |
| Pages with missing or empty title tags | 49 |

- The orphan and duplicate lists were exported into the tracker for review. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1372.021]
- Another example audit of an e-commerce store with 5,337 internal URLs had 24,702 issues in total: 1,517 errors, 13,058 warnings and 10,127 notices. Of the internal URLs, 4,502 had no errors and 835 had errors. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1372.031]
- The same audit found 406,554 links in total, of which 345,992 were crawled, 55,556 were uncrawled and 5,006 were blocked by robots.txt. Read crawl totals with the uncrawled and blocked share in mind. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1372.032]
- Automated audit tools are not always fully accurate, so relying only on them can miss issues. A manual review is recommended to be certain nothing is missed. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1372.026]

### Crawl Control: robots.txt, noindex and Redirects

#### robots.txt

- Only robots.txt directives block crawling. A noindex tag does not stop crawling. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1372.006]
- Editing robots.txt on Shopify:
  - Current (2024): robots.txt can be edited by adding a theme template named robots.txt.liquid. Go to Online Store > Themes > Edit code, then add the template under Templates. Check the changes in Google Search Console after saving. [L1105.014]
  - Older (2021-22): robots.txt cannot be edited by the merchant on Shopify, so noindex is the practical tool there. This statement reflects older platform behaviour and conflicts with the 2024 method above. [L1372.006]
- Procedure for a 'Submitted URL blocked by robots.txt' error in Google Search Console:
  1. Click the page in the Examples table to open the side panel, then click 'Test robots.txt blocking'.
  2. The robots.txt tester highlights the rule that blocks the URL.
  3. Click 'See live robots.txt' in the tester to see where the file is located.
  4. Edit the rule only if the block is unwanted. If you lack permission to edit the file, contact your hosting provider or check its documentation. [L1333.020]

#### noindex

- Place this tag in the head of a page to keep it out of the index:

```html
<meta name="robots" content="noindex">
```

[L1372.007]

- Google treats noindex as a suggestion that keeps the page out of the index. The page can still be crawled. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1372.007]
- Adding noindex by editing theme code can accidentally noindex the whole site. One such error went unnoticed for about two weeks, and recovery requires re-indexing from scratch. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1372.025]

#### Redirects

- Use redirects only where they are needed, such as when a page is deleted and should point to a replacement. Large batches of unnecessary redirects slow down crawling. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.008]
- Minimize redirects as part of Shopify speed work. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.014]

### Handling 404 Errors

- A 404 error means the URL points to a page that does not exist. Every 404 found on the store should be resolved, usually by updating the link or setting up a redirect. Smart SEO reports these errors so they can be fixed. [L1271.002]
- In Search Console, 404 errors mean the page no longer exists. They often remain because the old URL is still listed in the sitemap. Submitting a fresh sitemap usually removes them. [L0835.091]
- Use Google Webmaster Tools (called Google Search Console in the 2022-2025 units) to find indexing errors:
  1. Check the Coverage section for 404 errors, server errors and excluded pages.
  2. Review the Performance section to see which queries drive clicks and impressions.
  3. Monitor for manual penalties or security issues.
  4. Submit sitemaps to ensure all pages are crawlable. [L0226.026]

### Getting Pages Indexed

#### Submitting New Pages

- Submit new product pages, blog posts and optimized URLs directly to Google for faster indexing. Without this, Google may take weeks or months to discover and index new pages. [L0226.019]
- Request indexing in Google Search Console (older version, 2022):
  1. Go to search.google.com/search-console.
  2. Log in with your Google account.
  3. Select your property.
  4. Paste the new page URL.
  5. Click 'Request Indexing'.
  6. Google will crawl and index it immediately or very quickly. *Older advice (2022).* [L0249.064]
- Request indexing with URL Inspection (2025):
  1. Go to Google Search Console.
  2. Open URL Inspection from the left sidebar.
  3. Paste the product page URL in the search field.
  4. Click 'Request Indexing' to add the page to Google's crawl queue.
  5. Google will recrawl the page faster than waiting for random indexing. [L0278.015]

#### Pages Crawled but Not Indexed

- For pages marked crawled but currently not indexed, confirm that they are real pages you want indexed and not duplicates. Only then look for a fix. [L0835.094]

#### Sitemaps

- Submit sitemaps to ensure all pages are crawlable. [L0226.026]
- A fresh sitemap usually clears old 404 entries from Search Console. [L0835.091]

### Page Speed: Ranking, Conversion and Crawling

#### Speed as a Ranking Factor

The sources disagree on how strongly speed ranks. Both views are given below, with the conditions under which each applies.

- **Strong view:** Site speed directly affects Google organic search rankings, and faster pages rank higher. This is independent of Shopify's hosting and depends on theme lightness, app efficiency and image optimization. *Older advice (2020).* [L0245.046]
- **Strong view:** Google uses page loading speed as a signal when deciding how high to rank a product page, so a slow page can lose organic position to faster competitors. Speed fixes are among the quickest SEO gains because their effect can show within days. [L1190.001]
- **Strong view:** Website speed is a ranking factor, and slow pages lose conversions. [L0226.020]
- **Weak view:** Page speed is a Google ranking factor, but it is a weak one. Relevant, high-quality content and search intent match outweigh it, and a slow page can still rank first if its content is better than competitors'. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.001]
- **When each applies:**
  - Speed matters most when competing product pages have similar content, because then experience becomes the tiebreaker. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.001]
  - The 2018 mobile speed update affects only the slowest pages and a small share of queries. It applies the same standard whatever platform the site is built on. Google has tracked page performance signals since about 2010. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.029]
- Site speed must be good enough that a mobile visitor on a weak connection still gets the page. Google treats a blank page after a click as a negative ranking signal. [L1227.025]
- Page speed on Shopify pages and blog posts depends first on the theme and the installed apps. Image optimization is an additional lever on top of a suitable theme and a lean app setup. [L1321.001]

#### How Much Speed Is Enough

- A store does not need the fastest page online or a perfect score in speed tools. It only needs to load faster than the sites it competes with in the search results for the same queries. Judge speed relative to those competitors before deciding it is a problem. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.002]
- Benchmark against competitors:
  1. Enter each main competitor's domain into Google PageSpeed Insights.
  2. Compare FCP, LCP and CLS with your own results.
  3. Only the gap to the sites you rank against matters for SEO. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.032]

#### Speed and Conversion

- Page speed matters for conversion rate separately from SEO. A visitor who can see and use the page after about three to four seconds is usually fine. Conversion may start to drop once the usable point goes beyond roughly four seconds. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.012]
- Fully loaded time (often 7 seconds or more) is less relevant, because customers can use the page before everything has finished loading. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.012]

#### Speed Case Study

- A store had a GT Metrics performance score of 99, a structure score of 95 and an LCP under one second before any changes. After compressing images with Smart SEO and adding descriptive alt text to the product images, scores rose to a performance of 100, a structure of 96 and an LCP of 678 ms, with only small changes. [L1190.014]

| Metric | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| GT Metrics performance score | 99 | 100 |
| GT Metrics structure score | 95 | 96 |
| LCP | under 1 second | 678 ms |

### Measuring Page Speed

#### Tools

- Test page speed with at least three tools, because each reports different data:
  - Google PageSpeed Insights: Google's own tool, the one that matters most for search. Free at developers.google.com/speed. It analyzes page load time and gives recommendations for optimization. Use it to identify performance bottlenecks and track improvements. [L1266.003, L0226.025]
  - GTmetrix: technical waterfall and Yahoo-developed YSlow scores. [L1266.003]
  - WebPageTest.org: real browser runs from chosen locations and devices, e.g. Android or iPhone, with three runs per test. [L1266.003]
  - Pingdom is similar to GTmetrix and optional. [L1266.003]

#### Test Order and Scope

1. Test the homepage.
2. Test a random sample of three or four product pages to confirm results are consistent.
3. Test collection pages.
4. Identify which problems to fix based on the results. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.005]

- Always test each page type separately. A fast homepage does not mean fast product or collection pages. A page-speed test of the homepage URL only tests the homepage. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.006]
- Always look at both mobile and desktop results in PageSpeed Insights. Mobile scores are usually lower on Shopify, and mobile matters more because most visitors arrive on mobile. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.007]

#### Reading Scores

| PageSpeed Insights score | Colour | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 0-49 | Red | Poor. Priority. |
| 50-89 | Orange | Needs improvement |
| 90-100 | Green | Good |

- Scores in red are the priority. A low score alone is not a reason to panic if competitors score similarly. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.008]

#### Waterfall Charts

- Each row in a waterfall chart is one asset loaded (HTML, CSS, JS, images, fonts). Long bars and blocking segments show what delays the page. Beige/sandy colour in GTmetrix means waiting for the asset to load. Orange in WebPageTest shows a connection problem. Filter by HTML, CSS, JS or image to isolate the cause. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.013]

### Core Web Vitals

#### Metrics and Thresholds

- LCP (Largest Contentful Paint) should be under 2.5 seconds for good. This is Google's threshold. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.009]
- Practical LCP target on Shopify: under 4 seconds. Shopify limits control over some parts of the stack, so the practical goal is 4 seconds even though Google's good threshold is 2.5 seconds. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.009, L1266.010]
- CLS (Cumulative Layout Shift) above 0.25 on mobile is flagged as a problem. [L1266.009]
- FID (First Input Delay) is the third metric, measured as input responsiveness. [L1266.009]
- Field data (real users, 28-day Chrome User Experience collection) and lab data (simulated test run) can differ for the same page. Read both, and treat field data as what Google measures for ranking. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.011]

#### Core Web Vitals in Search Console

- The Core Web Vitals report in Google Search Console groups URLs as Poor, Need improvement or Good. It is based on Chrome UX Report field data. It names the failing issue per group, such as LCP longer than 4s on mobile or CLS above 0.25 on mobile, and offers a validation step after fixes. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.004, L1266.031]
- Core Web Vitals and Page experience data only appear in Search Console about one week after the store is set up. A brand-new store will show no data at first. Once data appears, check for errors and resolve any critical issues. [L1271.009]
- After you submit a sitemap, Core Web Vitals data takes time to appear. If it is still empty after a while, resubmit the sitemap. [L0965.078]
- Status of Core Web Vitals as a ranking signal:
  - In the 2020 to 2021 guidance, Core Web Vitals were reported in Search Console and PageSpeed Insights but were not yet a confirmed ranking signal. Google usually gives reporting 6 to 12 months before a metric becomes one. *Older advice (2020-21).* [L1266.030]
  - Core Web Vitals were framed as mobile-focused user experience metrics. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.030]

### Fixing Page Speed on Shopify

#### Fix Order

Work through these steps in order:

1. Compress images as far as possible in a lossy way, up to the point where quality starts to suffer. Do this offline before uploading, using a bulk offline tool such as ImageOptim, instead of relying on compression apps that may run JavaScript or compress less.
2. Load videos in a thin way so their JavaScript only loads when the user clicks play. Use the provided code snippet or a lightweight app. Code is the preferred option.
3. Implement lazy loading for images so they load only when the user scrolls near them.
4. Remove unused apps and their leftover code from the theme.
5. Use a theme with minimal, clean code.
6. Minimize redirects.
7. Minimize JavaScript, e.g. do not use homepage sliders or quick view on collection pages. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.014]

#### Images and Media

- Remove slideshows, carousels and heavy media elements from product pages if they slow page load time. Website speed is a ranking factor, and slow pages lose conversions. [L0226.020]
- Videos placed in the product description should use a thin (lazy) load so they do not noticeably slow down the product page. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1191.015]
- The default Shopify video embed adds at least 1 MB to the page size and a large amount of JavaScript, which significantly hurts page speed and therefore SEO. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1191.016]

#### Apps and Code

- Installing unnecessary apps or plugins on your Shopify store increases load time, because each app adds to it. Only keep apps that are essential to store function. [L0226.021]

### Mobile Optimization

- Mobile visitors and mobile results matter most. Mobile scores are usually lower on Shopify, so review mobile results first. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.007]
- Mobile optimization steps:
  1. Test your store on mobile devices (phones, tablets).
  2. Ensure images, banners, titles and CTA buttons render correctly on small screens.
  3. Check mobile page speed using Google PageSpeed Insights.
  4. Optimize font sizes and touch targets (buttons) for mobile users. [L0226.024]
- Check mobile Core Web Vitals in Search Console, where the report names failing mobile issues such as LCP longer than 4s on mobile or CLS above 0.25 on mobile. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L1266.004]
- A site must load well enough on mobile that a visitor on a weak connection still gets the page, because a blank page after a click counts as a negative ranking signal. [L1227.025]

### Removing Deleted Pages and Old URLs From Google

#### After a Site Rebuild

Older procedure (2021):

1. Submit the site map to Google.
2. Request a recrawl so Google re-examines the store.
3. Google then removes the old pages from its index and adds the new ones. *Older advice (2021).* [L0853.079]

#### After Deleting Products

1. Delete the product from your store. The sitemap updates automatically at yourstore.com/sitemap.xml.
2. Wait for Google to re-crawl the sitemap. The deleted page then drops out of it and no longer shows as an error in Search Console.
3. Optional: open Google Search Console, click Sitemaps and resubmit yourstore.com/sitemap.xml to request an immediate re-scan.
4. Google Merchant Center also stops receiving deleted products, because the feed resubmits on its schedule and skips missing pages. [L0965.076]

#### Redirects for Deleted Pages

- Use redirects where a deleted page should point to a replacement. Do not redirect in large batches that are not needed. *Older advice (2021-22).* [L0397.008]
- Every 404 found on the store should be resolved, usually by updating the link or setting up a redirect. [L1271.002]

#### Further details

- If the crawler has trouble accessing pages, it may cover only part of a site on a given visit, leaving some pages uncrawled until a later pass. Fixing access and crawl problems is therefore part of crawl optimization, not just a separate technical issue. [L0397.013]

## Duplicate and Thin Content, Canonicals and URLs

### Duplicate and Thin Content: What Counts

- Duplicate content means substantial blocks of text that either fully match other content or are appreciably similar, whether within one domain or across domains. Google does not define how similar counts as appreciably similar, so minor word swaps on copied text will not make it look unique. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.001]
- Changing a few words in a competitor's text, such as swapping a single word or sentence, does not make the copy original and can cause conflicts with the original owner. Use competitor pages only to learn the structure, never the wording. (Older advice, 2019.) [L0467.010]
- Multiple URLs that show the same content waste crawl time and add no SEO value, even though duplicates do not directly reduce the crawl rate. Shopify stores are a known source of such duplicate URLs and need checking. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0397.009]
- Google usually does not index duplicate pages but still crawls them, which spends crawl budget and dilutes the perceived quality of the whole site. Duplicate content therefore mostly results in lost ranking opportunities rather than a penalty. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.003]
- A manual action penalty for duplicate content is rare and typically only applies when the copying looks deliberately manipulative, such as scraping a whole competitor site to take its rankings. Accidental duplication within a site is very unlikely to lead to a penalty. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.004]
- Search engines do not penalize duplicate product descriptions but rank them less willingly, which hurts organic traffic over time. Organic ranking matters later, not during the first paid-ads launch. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1163.020]
- Identical product descriptions across many products are common in dropshipping and make the site look weak to buyers. Rewrite them as unique descriptions by the six-month mark so the change has time to settle. [L1468.031]
- On a large site, duplicate and thin content on 20 to 30% of pages can noticeably hold back overall SEO. Auditing and fixing these areas is therefore often one of the fastest SEO improvements available. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.018]
- A large shop with about 1,000 product pages copied supplier descriptions that also appear on other websites and put real effort into only about 100 of them. Google still crawls all 1,000 pages and reads the site as 900 pages without value and 100 with value, so the whole site ranks lower and receives fewer visitors. Unique text on the pages that matter most is the fix, instead of reusing shared supplier copy across the catalogue. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.015]

### Thin Content: Definition and Effects

- Thin content means pages with little or no text content that search engines consider to have little value to users, which can drag down the ranking performance of the whole site. Because Google can choose from an enormous number of pages, such pages are unlikely to rank even if they are indexed. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.001]
- Thin pages dilute how Google rates the overall quality of a site, waste crawl budget by pulling crawler attention away from better pages, and forfeit a keyword opportunity that every page could otherwise target. Adding real content to nearly any page tends to improve customer experience, conversion rate and retention. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.002]
- A site should have fewer low-value pages in the index rather than more. Past a tipping point, too many indexed pages, especially low-quality, keyword-confused or never-visited ones, weaken overall SEO. Pages that attract no visitors and serve no purpose should be removed from the index. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.012]
- Content on a store is only worth adding if it is valuable to the reader. Thin content added only for search engines does not help results. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1092.102]
- Thin content is not a concern for very small stores with only a few pages, such as a homepage, one product page and an about page. The audit matters most for sites with hundreds or thousands of pages, such as drop shippers with large product catalogues or resellers of other brands' products. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.009]
- Blog posts are normally unique and well over 300 words, so they usually do not need to be included in a thin or duplicate content audit. A blog post of 300 words or less or copied content signals a problem. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.016]

### Word and Percentage Targets

These targets come from different sources and apply in different situations. Use the one that matches the page you are checking.

| Target | Applies to | Source |
|---|---|---|
| At least 300 words of unique text | Pages you want indexed and ranking; the minimum, and more is better | [L1372.003] |
| About 300 words as a guideline only | Duplicate-content pages, which need most of their text to be unique and original and of good quality | [L1372.013] |
| At least 75% unique text | Every page; a personal working target, not a figure documented by Google. Unavoidable factual specifications can stay identical, so extra unique content must make up the rest of the page | [L0556.005] |
| Less than half of SEO-relevant text duplicated | Step in the tab-replacement procedure on product pages | [L0556.011] |
| About 2% or less duplicated text | Product pages after the boilerplate pattern is applied | [L0556.012] |

- A product page with unique text of 300 words or more is neither duplicate nor thin and can be removed from the thin-content list. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.033]
- Shared content that is minimised as far as possible still cannot be removed entirely, because it often supports conversion. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1191.017]

### Original Content and Copying Rules

- You may copy a competitor's general layout, because most store layouts are similar, but never copy their written content or their logo and custom graphics. All product and store text must be original. (Older advice, 2019.) [L0356.018]
- Do not republish other sites' articles on your store, even with credit and a link. Write unique content yourself or hire a freelance writer. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0468.130]
- Do not paste text copied from other websites, such as supplier descriptions or blog posts, directly onto your pages. For data from another source in a blog post, summarize it in your own words and link out to the original source, which is acceptable. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.006]
- Your website should resolve under only one domain version, either www or non-www, and every other version should redirect to it. [L0575.020]

### Thin and Duplicate Content Audit: Procedure

- A page audit identifies problem pages before any change is made. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.010]
  1. Identify all pages with duplicate content. [L1372.010]
  2. Identify all pages with thin content. [L1372.010]
  3. Identify all pages with 0 views in the past 6 to 12 months. [L1372.010]
  4. Identify orphan pages that are not part of the site structure. [L1372.010]
  5. Decide per page whether to add content, delete with redirect, or noindex. [L1372.010]
- Order of options for fixing or avoiding thin content (older advice, 2021-22):
  1. Run a page audit to find the problem pages. [L1372.004]
  2. Add at least 300 words of unique text to pages worth keeping. [L1372.004]
  3. If a page cannot get content, delete it and set a 301 redirect to a relevant live page, for example the homepage. [L1372.004]
  4. If a page cannot be deleted but is thin, add a noindex tag. [L1372.004]
- Full audit procedure for product and collection pages (older advice, 2021-22):
  1. Start with product descriptions: if a product page has unique text of 300 words or more, it is neither duplicate nor thin and can be removed from the list. [L1372.033]
  2. Check collection pages: if a collection page has no unique content, add it to the thin content list. [L1372.033]
  3. Skip blog posts unless they are 300 words or less or copied; those are a separate problem to fix. [L1372.033]
  4. If product descriptions are duplicated across the store, add all of those product pages to the list. [L1372.033]
  5. For each remaining page, record the page title and URL, then mark duplicate content yes or no and thin content yes or no. [L1372.033]
  6. For each problem page, decide Fix content? If no, decide Delete? and enter the redirect target if deleting; if neither applies, mark No-Index. [L1372.033]
  7. Finish the full map before changing any page, then hand the work to a VA or writer if the site is large. [L1372.033]

### Audit Tracking and Tools

- Before using a shared audit spreadsheet, go to File > Make a copy, choose a folder in your own Google Drive and leave the sharing and comments options unchecked. Work only in your copy and never in the original. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.034]
- Audit tracker workflow (older advice, 2021-22):
  1. Make a copy of the shared spreadsheet into your own Google Drive rather than editing the original. [L1372.017]
  2. For each relevant page, record page title and page URL. [L1372.017]
  3. Mark whether it has duplicate content and whether it has thin content (under 300 words). [L1372.017]
  4. Mark Fix Content yes or no; if no, mark Delete yes or no. [L1372.017]
  5. If deleting, enter the redirect target URL. [L1372.017]
  6. If neither, mark No-Index. [L1372.017]
- SEO tools such as Ahrefs, Raven Tools and Semrush each include a site audit feature that crawls a site and flags duplicate and thin content. Raven's site audit was described as the most accurate for finding these issues, though all such crawlers can make errors, so a manual check is still recommended. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.011]
- Fixing a large site is a gradual process. A mapped spreadsheet can be handed to a VA or writer for deletions, redirects, noindex entries and content tasks, with clear instructions for each. Work steadily until all problems are fixed. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.019]
- Pages with zero views over the past 6 to 12 months that are not going to change should be deleted and 301-redirected to a relevant page with value. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.014]
- A site lost 40% of its search traffic overnight after a Google update targeting thin, low-value listings, which shows how a single algorithm update can remove rankings for many low-quality pages at once. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.023]

### Boilerplate Tabs and Shared Text

- Boilerplate content is static text that loads on many pages, such as shipping, returns or guarantee tabs. It can still lower the value of each page even if it appears on only five pages. Shared text that makes up a large share of a page's SEO-relevant text is the main source of duplication on many shops. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.009]
- Example from a large sauna shop: about 500+ product pages each carried the same three tabs, with a shipping tab of at least 200 words, a second block of roughly 150 words, and product texts that were mostly identical. About 50% of the SEO-relevant text on the page was duplicated, and about 90% of site pages carried the same tab content. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.010]
- Boilerplate should be minimised as far as possible, because it can crowd out the unique content of the page. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1191.017]
- When product descriptions are mostly images, the page has very little unique crawlable text, so shared tabs and boilerplate can make up most of the visible text. Even a few hundred words of shared content can then dominate the SEO value of the page. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.016]
- Tab replacement procedure (older advice, 2021-22):
  1. Open a product page and check whether tabs or sections with long text repeat across other product pages. [L0556.011]
  2. Count how much of the SEO-relevant text on the page is identical to text on other pages; aim for less than half duplicated. [L0556.011]
  3. Replace long shared blocks with a short statement of the main point, such as free shipping or a 30-day return policy. [L0556.011]
  4. Link the short statement to a full policy page that opens in a new window. [L0556.011]
  5. Review the site and, for each large duplicate chunk, shorten it while keeping a findable link to its own separate page. [L0556.011]
- Replace shared boilerplate with a two-line summary that answers the customer's main question, then add a call-to-action link to the full policy page. This keeps the important information visible and keeps the duplicated text to about 2% or less of the page text on a product page. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.012]
  - Shipping summary example: `Free shipping on all orders within the country, see our full shipping policy.` [L0556.013]
  - Returns summary example: `30-day free returns, read our full returns policy.` [L0556.013]
  - Both lines link to their policy pages, which open in a new window. [L0556.013]
- Moving a long shipping or policy section off the product page can help the page rank for high-value model searches. Customers who need the information must still find it easily from the product page. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.014]

### Shopify Duplicate URLs and Indexing

- Shopify can create several URLs for the same product, such as `/products/product-id` and `/collections/products/product-id`. Both show the same page and count as duplicate content. A canonical tag reduces the problem, but the two versions still exist. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.002]
- Shopify generates several URLs that display the same page, which creates duplicate content. Shopify's behaviour has some advantages, but it can cause an SEO problem that needs to be managed. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.011]
- The preferred base URL of a Shopify product is the root domain followed by `/products/` and the product handle, for example `domain/products/product-handle`. This is the URL that should always be indexed. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.012]
- A product reached through a collection page gets a different URL that includes `/collections/collection-name/products/product-handle`. Google treats this as a separate page even though the content is identical. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.013]
- On a Shopify store connected to Google Search Console, it is normal to see many pages listed as known but not indexed. Shopify sets this up by default so that product variants, collection-based product URLs and paginated pages do not get indexed as duplicates. [L0373.001]
- On Shopify stores, many pages show as not indexed, usually product URLs with collection paths or parameter variants, while the clean product URL is indexed. This is expected, because indexing the variants would split ranking across several URLs for the same product. (Older advice? No: 2025.) [L0807.033]
- The fix for duplicates caused by Shopify navigation variants is generally not required when the main collection URL is the indexed page. [L1283.096]
- A drop in indexed pages can be caused by duplicate versions of the same page, such as URLs with UTM parameters being counted as separate pages. To check, review which sitemap pages are actually indexed and look for errors on the ones that are not. [L0835.092]

### Canonical Tags, Noindex and Hidden Products

- A canonical tag is HTML code that marks one URL as the original version of a page and suggests that search engines do not index the other versions. Shopify uses canonical tags, but they do not guarantee which version Google indexes. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.014]
- Canonical tags for product variants on platforms other than Shopify are needed, because each colour or size variant can create its own URL with the same content. Shopify adds these canonical tags for product variants automatically. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.007]
- On platforms other than Shopify, a colour or size variant often gets its own URL with a modifier added to the end, while the description, measurements and price stay identical and only one or two words change. These variant URLs count as duplicate pages unless a canonical tag points them to the main product, so check platforms such as Magento, WooCommerce or BigCommerce for this setup. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.017]
- Use a noindex tag for pages that must exist but should not be indexed. Avoid serving the same content on dynamically generated URLs. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0556.008]
- If Google indexes a duplicate URL instead of the intended one, backlinks pointing to the non-indexed URL lose much of their value. The aim is to serve content consistently on a single URL. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.015]
- Shopify creates a hidden collection URL for every product tag, for example `/collections/collection-name/tag-name`. These pages cannot be edited in the Shopify admin, have very limited value, and can still be indexed, which creates a potential problem. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.016]
- The supplied code also sets noindex on tag-generated collection pages. It does not delete them, but it keeps them out of the index automatically. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.021]
- To organise products by type, create a collection page, add content to it and include it in the site structure, rather than relying on product tags. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.022]
- If a hidden product stays hidden long enough, Google can drop it from the index. When it is reactivated, it must be re-indexed from scratch, and any ranking and traffic it had can be lost. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.018]
- Canonical and noindex setup procedure (older advice, 2021-22):
  1. Insert the supplied canonical code into `theme.liquid` in the Shopify theme. [L1414.019]
  2. Avoid using product tags as much as possible. [L1414.019]
  3. Instead of hiding products, set them to zero stock or delete them and redirect the URL. [L1414.019]

### Multi-Storefront and Localised Setups

- Check canonicals and hreflang only if the store runs localised storefronts, such as language or market subfolders. If localised URLs compete with the main pages in search results, that can suppress rankings across the site. If the store has no such subfolders, skip this check. [L1269.026]
- The canonical and hreflang check for multi-storefront setups is a one-time audit of roughly 15 minutes. It should confirm that localised URLs do not compete with the main pages, and it should not turn into an ongoing cleanup project. [L1269.050]

### URL Handles: Format and Setup

- The product URL handle should follow the pattern brand name, product name, then SKU or GTIN, written in lowercase with dashes between words and no spaces. Add the SKU or GTIN after a final dash if the supplier provides one. [L1190.035]
- Add a SKU or GTIN to the end of the product URL handle only when the supplier actually provides one. Check the product details or specifications first. If no identifier exists, use just the brand name and product name. [L1190.048]
- Put the target keyword into the URL handle, written in lowercase with hyphens between words and no spaces, for example `customizing-your-standing-desk`. The URL handle is the first on-page element to check for the keyword. [L1321.016]
- In Shopify, the product URL follows the pattern `domain/products/[handle]`, and the handle is edited in the URL and handle field of the Search engine listing preview. Changing the handle changes the live URL, so the focus keyword belongs in the handle only when the page is new or the change is deliberate. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1026.020]
- Setting path: Product page > Search engine listing > URL handle. After editing the URL, a line appears below the field offering to create a URL redirect from the old URL to the new one. Keep that option checked, then click Save so old links forward to the new page. [L1190.037]
- Shopify generates the product URL by copying the full product title by default, so long titles with barcodes or extra words produce long URLs. Shorten the URL slug manually before publishing. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.004]

### URL Handle Rules for New and Existing Pages
