- Keep the URL short. Google prefers short URLs, so product URLs should not be overly descriptive. There is no fixed word count; keep it as short as possible and avoid stacking descriptive words. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.003, L1414.005]
- The page URL should contain the focus keyword and be short, because long URLs get truncated in search results. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1191.021]
- Include the page's focus keyword in the URL. For product pages this is usually the product title, and for blog posts it is the target keyword. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.006]
- A URL does not need to match the page title exactly. It needs to be short and contain the focus keyword. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.010]
- A URL is the unique identifier Google uses to recognise a web page and its content as one entity, so each page should have a correctly formatted URL tied to content that is as unique as possible. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.001]
- Set the URL with the focus keyword when creating a new page. For pages already ranking well, do not change the URL, since a change can cause temporary traffic losses without a clear gain. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1191.022]
- Change URL handles only for new products or stores with weak organic traffic. Stores that already rank well organically should leave their URLs unchanged. [L1190.036]
- Do not rewrite existing URLs just to add keywords. Changing URLs has consequences, so decide carefully before changing them. Apply the keyword-in-URL rule primarily to new pages. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1026.011]
- Do not change the URL and handle of a page after it is created, because changing it damages SEO. Leave these fields alone once the page is saved. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1264.005]
- New page creation procedure (older advice, 2021-22):
  1. When creating a new page, edit the URL before publishing it. [L1414.007]
  2. Check that the URL is short and contains the focus keyword. [L1414.007]
  3. Only then publish the page. [L1414.007]

### Changing URLs and Redirects

- After a page is published, changing its URL creates a redirect. Google treats the result as a new page that is not indexed immediately, even if only one letter changed. URLs should therefore be right before publishing. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.008]
- Changing a URL handle requires a redirect from the old URL to the new one, so existing links and search results keep working. Shopify's URL handle change can create this redirect automatically; confirm it was created. [L1271.018]
- A 301 redirect passes SEO value to the new URL. A 302 redirect is temporary and is not meant as a permanent move. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.009]
- Redirects keep visitors who bookmarked or linked to an old URL on the site, which can salvage a sale. Without a redirect they land on an error page and usually leave. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.025]
- Deleting a page does not immediately remove it from Google's index, and the old URL can keep returning a 404 for a while, which also hurts bookmarked or linked visits. A 301 redirect, which is permanent, to a page with value prevents this. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.005]
- Deleting pages without setting a 301 redirect leaves visitors and Google with error pages. Deleting is safe when paired with a redirect to a relevant page. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.024]
- Avoid redirects unless they are absolutely necessary. Large numbers of redirects use up crawl budget and make Google's crawling of the site inefficient. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.027]
- Google does not value links that point to redirects as highly, so a redirected backlink loses some of its value. The better practice is to ask the sites linking to the old URL to update the link to the new URL. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.030]
- Before changing or deleting a URL, check the following (older advice, 2021-22):
  1. Check the page's index status. [L1414.028]
  2. Check its organic traffic, since a change and redirect will probably reduce it at least temporarily. [L1414.028]
  3. Check its backlinks in a tool such as Ubersuggest or Ahrefs before deleting or changing the URL. [L1414.028, L1414.031]
  4. If the page is deleted or changed, set a 301 redirect to another relevant page. [L1414.028]

### Discontinued and Backordered Products

- Two approaches apply to discontinued products, depending on whether the product will come back and whether the page ranks:
  - **Product will not be sold again, page has little ranking value:** delete the product and set a 301 redirect so visitors and Google do not hit a 404 error. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.024]
  - **Product page still ranks in Google:** keep the page live and redirect visitors to products you can sell, because deleting it wastes the ranking it earned. [L0942.012]
- Redirect a discontinued product URL to its upgraded or updated replacement product rather than to a generic page. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.026]
- Keeping old product pages live and pointing them to a replacement preserves the search rankings they earned. People keep searching old model names for years. Competitors who delete their pages lose those rankings, so your replacement page can capture the traffic. [L1287.020]
- Deleting the product page of a discontinued or backordered product wastes the ranking it earned, because Google eventually de-indexes the page and the search traffic is lost. Keep the page and redirect the visitor to products you can sell. [L0942.012]
- Popular product pages that rank well organically can keep bringing traffic without paid ads, so they can serve as sales pages for other products. This only works when the page is no longer orderable and points to available alternatives. [L0942.013]
- Handling a discontinued or long-backordered product that still ranks in Google:
  1. Keep the product page live but make the product not orderable, so no Add to Cart is possible. [L0942.011]
  2. Stop running paid traffic to that page; organic rankings keep sending visitors. [L0942.011]
  3. State that the product has been discontinued by its manufacturer. [L0942.011]
  4. List the top three similar products that are available and explain why you recommend each one. [L0942.011]
  5. Link directly from the page to the product pages of those alternatives. [L0942.011]
- When a discontinued product's page still ranks in Google and a newer variation of it exists, keep the old page and point visitors to the new variation. Make the old page non-orderable, state that the older version has been discontinued by its manufacturer, and link to the newer variation. [L0942.031]
- Zero-view pages that are not going to change should be deleted and 301-redirected, as described under Thin and Duplicate Content Audit. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1372.014]
- A product that is removed from sale should not be hidden for long periods, because Google can drop hidden products from the index. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.018, L1414.019]

### Redirect Economy and Backlinks

- Before deleting a page with backlinks, check them in Ubersuggest or Ahrefs, since both tools show which pages have backlinks. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.031]
- Ubersuggest and Ahrefs can show which pages have backlinks, and this should be checked before deleting a page or changing its URL. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1414.031]

### Reading the Google Search Console Coverage Report

- Use the Coverage report to identify duplicate pages. The fix for Shopify navigation variant duplicates is generally not required when the main collection URL is the indexed page. [L1283.096]
- The Coverage report sorts URLs into four statuses:
  - **Error:** not indexed because of a problem.
  - **Valid with warnings:** indexed but with issues to check.
  - **Valid:** indexed and eligible to appear in search.
  - **Excluded:** not indexed, though Google has found the URL.
  
  Each status can be opened to see its reasons and affected URLs. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.019]
- Status Valid means the page is in Google's index and can appear in organic search results. Status Excluded means the page is not indexed, though Google knows it exists. Excluded pages are the priority for SEO fixes. Counts can exceed the real number of store pages because a page can appear under several exclusion reasons at once. In the demo example, 704 pages were valid and 5,480 were excluded for a store with roughly 500 products. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.008]
- A Coverage report can show Excluded pages for a store with far fewer real pages, because the same page can be counted under several reasons. Use the per-reason breakdown and the Examples table to see the actual affected URLs rather than relying on the total. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.013]
- The Coverage report has a Primary crawler setting, which is Smartphone by default, so the statuses show how Google's smartphone crawler sees the site. The report displays a Last updated date, so check that date before acting on the counts. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.018]
- In the Coverage detail view, each example URL row shows a Last crawled date. Use it to judge whether Google has visited a page recently before deciding that the page needs fixing. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.021]
- Search Console indexing reports often list excluded pages that were never meant to be indexed, such as utility or duplicate pages. Check which URLs show issues and confirm that product pages and main pages are indexed. If only utility-type pages are excluded, it is usually not a problem. [L1115.099]
- Check the Google Search Console Coverage report to identify duplicate pages. [L1283.096]
- A drop in indexed pages can be caused by duplicates, so review which sitemap pages are indexed before acting. [L0835.092]
- Alternate pages with a canonical tag on a young site are not necessarily errors. They are expected when the store has duplicate variations. Check whether real content is missing from the index before acting on them. [L0835.093]

### Excluded Reason: Alternate Page with Proper Canonical Tag

- Excluded pages with the reason Alternate page with proper canonical tag are normal and not an error. Shopify creates several URLs for the same product, via collections, tags and similar, and Google indexes one canonical version while excluding the others. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.009]
- The canonical choice should still be the intended one, for example the short `/products/` URL rather than long collection-path URLs. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.009]

### Excluded Reason: Crawled, Currently Not Indexed

- Crawled, currently not indexed is the reason to investigate. Google has crawled the page but chosen not to index it, which can indicate duplicate content or thin content. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.010]
- Check the page for duplicated content, strong similarity to another page, or very little unique content, and improve it. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.010]
- Such pages do not need to be resubmitted for crawling, since they may be indexed later. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.010]

### Diagnosing a Problem URL in Coverage

1. Open the reason, for example Crawled, currently not indexed or Alternate page with proper canonical tag, and read the Examples table of affected URLs. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.011]
2. Click the page in the Examples table to open the side panel, or use the URL Inspection tool on it. [L1333.011]
3. For robots.txt blocks, run the robots.txt tester, find the blocking rule, and update robots.txt only if the block is unwanted. [L1333.011]
4. For a noindex tag, remove the tag from the live page if indexing is desired. Then use Test live URL and, if noindex is gone, Request Indexing. [L1333.011]
5. For 404 errors on pages that were deleted, add a redirect to a relevant page if needed. [L1333.011]
6. For soft 404s, redirect chains or 5xx server errors, fix the page or server behaviour, then re-check. [L1333.011]
7. Export the URL list when many URLs are affected for bulk analysis. [L1333.011]

### Coverage Exclusion and Error Reasons: Standard Fixes

(Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1333.012]

| Reason | Status | Meaning / action |
|---|---|---|
| Server error (5xx) | Error | Server returned a 500-level error; fix server |
| Redirect error | Error | Redirect chain too long, redirect loop, too-long URL, or bad/empty URL in chain; debug with Lighthouse |
| Submitted URL blocked by robots.txt | Error | Remove or alter the robots.txt rule if the block is unwanted |
| Submitted URL marked 'noindex' | Error | Remove the noindex meta tag or HTTP header if indexing is wanted; verify with URL Inspection |
| Submitted URL seems to be a Soft 404 | Error | Server returned a soft 404; fix the page |
| Submitted URL returns unauthorized request (401) | Error | Remove authorization requirement or allow Googlebot access; check in incognito mode |
| Submitted URL not found (404) | Error | Non-existent URL submitted; fix or remove it from the sitemap |
| Alternate page with proper canonical tag | Excluded | Normal for duplicate product URLs; Google chose a canonical |
| Page with redirect | Excluded | Page redirects elsewhere; usually no action |
| Not found (404) | Excluded | Deleted page with no redirect; add a redirect if a relevant page exists |
| Crawled - currently not indexed | Excluded | Crawled but not indexed; check for duplicate or thin content |
| Blocked by robots.txt | Excluded | Blocked by robots.txt rule |
| Excluded by 'noindex' tag | Excluded | Noindex tag present on the page |
| Soft 404 | Excluded | Page looks empty or error-like to Google |
| Crawl anomaly | Excluded | Unspecified crawl problem; investigate |

## Search Listings: Titles, Meta Descriptions and Snippets

### Where the listing is set

- The search engine listing defines what Google shows for a page. The Page title is what Google displays, the meta description is the text shown in results, and the URL handle is the slug after your domain. These fields are optional for demo products and are covered in detail in later SEO modules. [L0036.025]
- On a product page, the Search engine listing section shows the product's URL and lets you edit how the product appears in search results. [L1167.036]
- The section sits at the bottom of the product edit page and is called Search engine listing preview. It contains the Page title, Description, and URL and handle fields. [L1026.007]
- The preview shows the title, URL and description as they appear in Google and Bing. If no meta description is set, Shopify uses the product description. [L1190.033]
- Collections have a Search engine listing section. The page title and meta description are what Google shows for the collection's keywords. They can be left empty at first and completed later as part of SEO work. [L0371.017]
- Pages use the Edit website SEO section, which contains a search engine listing preview and the fields Page title, Description, and URL and handle. Shopify shows a character counter under Page title (limit 70 characters) and Description (limit 320 characters). [L1264.003]
- The Page title and Description in Edit website SEO are what customers see in Google, so they should be accurate at launch and refined later. Once the business is running, these fields deserve proper SEO work. [L1264.004]

### Homepage and social sharing settings

- Homepage title and meta description are set in Shopify under Online Store > Preferences > Title and meta description (also reached via Sales channels > Online Store > Preferences). *(2021-22)* The homepage title allows up to 70 characters and the homepage meta description up to 320 characters. [L0370.009, L1145.021]
- *(2021-22)* The homepage meta description appears under the page title in Google. It describes what the store sells and why to buy from it, so it must contain real content rather than filler. [L0370.009]
- *(2021-22)* The homepage title is the clickable headline in Google results and the text in the browser tab. The homepage meta description is the snippet beneath it. Set both for the root domain, and make the description describe what is sold and why to buy there, since it is not a technical field. [L0370.023]
- *(2021-22)* The title and meta description define how the store appears in search results. [L1145.021]
- *(2021-22)* Social sharing image: set it under Online Store > Preferences > Social sharing image, sized 1200 x 628 px. Social platforms display it when someone shares a link to the store, and it is used if no other relevant image can be found. [L0370.022, L1145.021]

### Character limits and truncation

- Content longer than the limit is truncated in Google results, hiding important information. [L0278.008]
- Shopify shows a character counter in the preview. Keep the meta title at or below the displayed limit (the example shows 53 of 70 characters) and the description at or below 320 characters, since overlong text is cut off with an ellipsis. [L1026.008]

| Element | Limit or target | Where it applies | IDs |
|---|---|---|---|
| Page title, shown in Google | Stay within 70 characters | Search results | L1190.034 |
| Page title, writing target | 50-60 characters | When you write the title | L0226.023 |
| Page title field (homepage and pages) | Up to 70 characters | Shopify field and counter | L0370.009, L1026.008, L1264.003 |
| Meta description field | Up to 320 characters | Shopify field and counter | L0370.009, L1026.008, L1264.003 |
| Meta description, writing target | 150-160 characters | When you write the description | L0226.023 |
| Meta description, blog prompt | Under 160 characters | Blog post prompt | L1321.018 |
| Meta description, display | Search results may show only part of a long description | Search results | L1190.034 |

- Conflict between sources: the field limits (70 for titles, 320 for descriptions) are what Shopify accepts, while the 50-60 and 150-160 targets are what to write so the full text is displayed. Use the targets when writing and the field limits as the hard ceiling. [L0226.023, L0370.009, L1190.034, L1321.018]

### Auto-filled values and common mistakes

- *(2021-22)* When no custom meta data is set, Shopify copies the product title and product description into the meta title and meta description automatically. The description is often truncated mid-sentence and reads like a product spec dump, so it should always be customized. [L1026.005]
- *(2021-22)* The auto-filled meta title is often acceptable, especially for product pages, because it usually matches what shoppers search for. The auto-filled meta description is rarely usable and should always be rewritten. [L1026.019]
- *(2021-22)* On platforms that do not fill in meta data, Google chooses the title and description from any text on the page. This can produce an unattractive snippet, so meta data should be set manually. [L1026.006]
- *(2022)* Changing meta tags does not affect Google Ads product data as long as product names and descriptions stay the same. [L0803.087]
- Changing meta descriptions or page names does not break links as long as the URL stays the same. If they are in the wrong format, correct them. [L0964.133]
- *(2021-22)* Shopify shows a preview of the product title in the search title field. Check that the search title matches the product title and that the URL is clean, then replace the auto-filled description with a short text. [L1166.020]

### Writing page titles

**Product titles**

- Product page titles include brand name, product name and SKU. Remove the business name from product pages and keep it in the homepage meta title. If the title is too long, remove extras such as lowest price or free shipping first. [L0804.059]
- *(2022, older)* The page SEO title should include the product name, the SKU and the brand name, as this is what is added in most cases. [L0854.063]
- Brand-led formula: brand name, then the full product name, then a short click trigger such as free shipping, insurance, or a real offer, kept under 70 characters. [L1190.038]
  - Example for a bamboo cutting board set: `Northlane Bamboo Cutting Board Set | Free Shipping and Insurance`
  - Example for an oak bookshelf: `Hearth and Co. Oak Bookshelf | Order Today, Free Shipping`
- Benefit-led formula: [Target keyword] + [unique, verifiable claim such as largest, fastest, or most affordable] + [brand or short descriptor], kept within the character limit. It works because the keyword matches the search query and bolds in the snippet, while the claim gives a reason to click over competitors. [L1026.016]
  - Example for a standing desk: `Standing Desk for Small Offices | Quietest Electric Motor Under $400`
- Purchase-intent words such as buy and online in product titles help those pages rank for searches made by people ready to buy, who convert better than people running broad informational searches. [L0575.031]
- Find high-converting keywords in Google Ads and add them to product meta titles and descriptions, so the organic listing matches what buyers already search for and convert on. [L0920.027]
- Long-tail phrases: if your best revenue comes from a long-tail phrase such as `handmade German chef knife`, add that exact phrase with its qualifier to the meta title and meta description. This matches search intent more closely and can improve both clicks and conversion for that query. [L1357.051]

**Matching the product title versus rewriting it**

- *(2021-22)* Match the meta title to the product title. Make sure the meta title matches the product title. [L1163.023, L1166.020]
- The meta title does not need to match the product page title word for word. It can be rewritten to name a specific benefit or unique selling point, for example being the largest product of its type, while still including the target search term. [L1026.012]
- Use the matching rule when the product title already names the product clearly. Use the rewrite when a verifiable claim gives a reason to click. [L1026.012, L1163.023]

**Keywords and headlines**

- Include the focus keyword in the meta title, meta description and URL. [L1026.009]
- *(2021-22)* Headlines can include keywords for organic ranking without losing their function. The headline should also hint at or state a benefit that follows from the primary promise. [L0416.007]
- *(2021-22)* The meta title and meta description should each be unique for the page and contain the focus keyword. Write them using the copywriting principles for meta descriptions. [L1191.023]
- The page title and meta description are read together in search results, so they work as one unit. The title names the product with the target keyword, and the description gives a reason to click, such as a shipping detail or a real offer. [L1190.051]

**Case example**

- *(2021-22)* Before-and-after case for an RV fridge product on Shopify: the unedited snippet used the auto-pulled product description, which was cut off mid-sentence at the character limit. The edited version used a benefit-led title with the target keyword `RV fridge` and a copy-style description that ends with a call to action, and it displays in full with no truncation. [L1026.015]

### Writing meta descriptions

**Principles**

- Write for the click, not only for the ranking. Treat the description as a short sales letter. [L0575.012]
- *(2021-22)* A search snippet is a sales message only in the sense of getting the click. The actual selling happens on the product page. Treat the meta description like ad copy whose only goal is the click. [L1026.013]
- *(2021-22)* Write the meta description as short copy addressed directly to the searcher, using a question or a direct statement, instead of listing specifications. End with a clear call to action, for example `Shop online today`, because people do not reliably decide to act on their own. [L1026.014]
- Use the brand name, product name and SKU number in the meta description. The business name is optional and can be left out to keep the title short. [L1094.098]

**Formulas and patterns**

- Capital-letter formula: capitalise a few key words (for example FREE, NEW or LIMITED) so the snippet stands out among 10 to 15 competing results. Remove repeated brand names and shorten words where possible, for example replacing "and" with "&" or "+" if the platform allows. [L0575.012]
  - Example: `Handmade Oak Desks | FREE Delivery in 2 Days, 10-Year WARRANTY, Shop Solid Wood Now.`
  - Example: `Organic Cold-Pressed Olive Oil, EXTRA Virgin, Bottled in Italy | FREE Shipping on Orders Over $50.`
- Sensory or benefit-led hook: these beat plain product-fact lists. Hooks can use sounds-fancy wording, ingredient origin, FREE in caps, or same-day dispatch. Generic phrases such as `order online today` add nothing. [L0575.016]
  - Example: `Ceremonial Grade Matcha from Uji, Japan | 30g Tin, FREE Delivery, Same-Day Dispatch Before 2PM.`
  - Example: `Hand-Roasted Single-Origin Coffee Beans | FRESH Roasted Weekly, FREE Shipping Over $40, Order Today.`
- Copy-style formula: [direct question or address to the searcher] + [one specific product benefit] + [short proof or feature] + [call to action]. It works because it speaks to a single searcher intent and ends with an action. [L1026.017]
  - Example for a pet water fountain: `Want a quieter, cleaner water bowl for your cat? Our 2-litre filtered fountain runs whisper-quiet and needs cleaning only once a month. Order today and get free shipping.`
- Short description pattern: one sentence naming the product's main benefit, followed by one short call to action telling the shopper what to do now. Formula: [main benefit sentence]. [action verb] [product] online today. It gives the result a reason to click and a clear next step. [L1166.021]
  - Own examples: `Quiet cordless vacuum with 60 minutes of runtime and a washable filter. Shop online today.`
  - Own examples: `Hand-stitched leather wallet in three colours. Browse the full range today.`
- Benefit-and-trust formula: call to action, product name, brand name, one real trust or shipping detail, and an optional real offer, written like a short ad. [L1190.040]
  - Example for a bamboo cutting board set: `Shop the Northlane Bamboo Cutting Board Set today and get free shipping and insurance on your order.`
  - Example for an oak bookshelf: `Order the Hearth and Co. Oak Bookshelf today with free shipping and a 30-day exchange window.`
- Keyword-first formula: start with the target keyword (for example `Order the [keyword]`), then add the brand name, one short benefit, and a real shipping or store offer such as free shipping, and close with the official store. The keyword at the front helps the page rank and gives searchers a faster match, which can raise CTR. [L1271.016]
  - Example 1: `Shop the [keyword] from the official [brand] store. Soft, durable and in stock, with free shipping on all orders.`
  - Example 2: `Order the [keyword] online from [brand]. Fast delivery and easy returns, with free shipping over [amount].`

**Template for product descriptions**

```
Order the [PRODUCT NAME] today from [BRAND NAME]. [BRAND NAME] offers free shipping and insurance on all orders. [ONLY IF THIS PRODUCT HAS A REAL BONUS: Order today and receive a free [BONUS NAME].] Delete the bracketed bonus sentence if this product has no bonus.
```
[L1190.039]

**Benefit-focused template**

```
To improve your search result click-through rate, construct Meta descriptions that identify the page topic and your brand, then emphasize convertible benefits like free shipping, insurance protection, and a complimentary bonus item. This benefit-focused approach performs better than standard product descriptions.
```
[L0176.008]

**Calls to action and checks**

- *(2021-22)* End the meta description with a call to action. [L1026.009]
- *(2021-22)* The meta description must not be cut off and should end with a call to action such as `Shop Online Now`. The meta title, URL and product title should all match. [L1163.024]
- *(2021-22)* Metadata checks for each product, in order: make sure the meta title matches the product title; make sure the URL reflects the product title (updated title); make sure the meta description is not truncated, and add a call to action such as `Shop Online Now`. [L1163.023]

**Phone numbers**

- Show a phone number in the meta description only where it makes sense. Higher-priced products with a long sales cycle benefit from call-us messaging, because buyers want to speak to someone first. A phone number is a differentiator when competitors do not show one. [L0575.014]

### Snippet elements and what drives clicks

- Search results show the page URL, meta title, meta description and structured data (such as reviews, price and stock status), plus sitelinks that Google generates on its own. Each of these is a place where a searcher decides whether to click. [L1026.002]
- *(2021-22)* Google bolds search terms that match the query exactly in the snippet. Use the focus keyword in three places: meta title, meta description and URL, so each keyword occurrence adds a bolded word that draws the eye and signals relevance to the searcher. [L1026.010]
- Meta data is not a direct Google ranking signal, but it strongly affects organic click-through rate (CTR), which is a ranking signal. Optimize meta data mainly to earn clicks from search results. [L1026.001, L0881.019]
- The meta description has not been a direct ranking factor since 2003. Its value is mainly the CTR it earns, so spending much time on it for low-traffic pages is usually wasteful. [L1269.004]
- CTR is the share of people who see a listing in organic search results and click it. A 100% CTR is not realistic, but a higher CTR signals to Google that the listing matches the search, which can help rankings over time. [L1190.032]
- *(2022, older)* Page titles and meta descriptions should be written for CTR, since more organic clicks lead to higher organic rankings. [L0803.087]
- *(2021-22)* Misleading or incomplete snippets, such as showing a product as available when it is out of stock, lead to immediate bounces. High bounce rates hurt SEO because user interaction is also a ranking signal. Accurate snippets filter for visitors who really want the product. [L1026.004]
- *(2021-22)* On Shopify, Google generates organic search sitelinks completely on its own, and merchants cannot set them in the product or page editor. You can only prepare pages for sitelinks where possible, and Google has said it may add manual control later. [L1026.018]
- Optimized product page titles and keywords help both organic search traffic and Google Shopping results. Updating them is low priority when product pages already follow the standard structure, but it is unlikely to hurt. [L0748.014]

### Claims, bonuses and accuracy

- Mention bonuses or promotions in the meta description only if they really apply to that product. A promotion such as `$10 off orders over $50` should not appear on a single product priced under $50, because it cannot be claimed there. [L1190.041]
- Do not keep claims for bonuses or offers the store does not have. Remove any promotion that is not active before saving the page. [L1271.017]
- AI tools, including Smart SEO and others, can invent product details. Always check generated meta text against the actual product before saving. [L1190.045]

### Blog posts and pages

- Set the URL and listing fields for a Shopify blog post or page:
  1. Save the post first.
  2. In the search engine listing preview, check the URL and handle field.
  3. Replace the handle with the target keyword in lowercase, words separated by hyphens.
  4. Set the page title. Keep the product name, or use a clearer phrase such as `A guide to customizing your standing desk` or `The ultimate guide to customizing your standing desk`.
  5. Delete the description that Shopify pulls automatically from the content.
  6. Enter the meta description (see the meta description steps) and save.
  [L1321.017]
- Ask ChatGPT for a meta description with this prompt:

```
Please write a meta description for a blog post called '[post title]'. The post targets the keyword '[target keyword]'. Keep it accurate to the actual post content, include the keyword naturally, and keep it under 160 characters.
```
[L1321.018]

- Add a meta description to a blog post:
  1. Ask ChatGPT for a meta description using the post title and target keyword (prompt above).
  2. Read the result and check that it matches the actual post content.
  3. In the search engine listing description field in Shopify, delete the automatically pulled text.
  4. Paste the checked meta description and save.
  [L1321.019]

### Prioritising pages and testing CTR

- Audit the top 10 pages by page views in Google Analytics first (the 80/20 rule), and improve their meta descriptions and titles before the rest. [L0575.018]
- Test and improve each page's listing:
  1. Write compelling, keyword-relevant titles (50-60 characters).
  2. Write descriptive meta descriptions (150-160 characters) that encourage clicks.
  3. Ensure both are unique per page.
  4. Test for click-through rate in Google Webmaster Tools (now Search Console) and improve if CTR is below niche average.
  [L0226.023]
- Start with title and meta rewrites on pages that already get many impressions at positions just outside the top results, roughly positions 8 to 11. A CTR lift of 1 to 2 percentage points on 30,000 or more impressions is meaningful traffic without new content. [L1269.017]
- CTR check example: 90 clicks from 33,858 impressions at an average position of about 8 is a CTR of about 0.27%. A page near the bottom of page one commonly earns 1 to 3%, and reaching just 1% on those impressions would mean about 340 clicks. [L1269.018]
- Brand search check: if your own brand ranks lower than third-party aggregators in Search Console, for example around position 10 with hundreds of impressions, act on it. Make the homepage title lead with the brand name, set up Organization schema and sitelinks, and confirm the brand's own pages are the ones shown. [L1269.025]

### Smart SEO and AI-assisted drafts

- Smart SEO gives each product an SEO score from 0 to 100 for its title and meta description. Use low scores to find products to improve first, but do not treat a 100 as proof there is nothing left to improve. [L1190.042]
- Case: a product named only `100K Club` had a Smart SEO score around 50. The meta title was changed to include the brand name and the full product type, `Brand 100K Club shirt`, and the score rose to about 75. The change was made by editing the meta title field in the product's Smart SEO record. [L1190.043]
- Generate meta title and description with Smart SEO AI:
  1. Open the product in Smart SEO and click edit on the meta title and description.
  2. Delete any description text you do not want to keep.
  3. Enter keywords for the generation, such as the brand name, product type, and product name.
  4. Choose a tone of voice that fits the brand: expert for a serious brand, playful for a fun one.
  5. Click Generate.
  6. Choose the titles and descriptions you like; you can keep both options.
  7. Check every generated claim against the real product details.
  8. Click Save.
  [L1190.044]
- Generate meta tags for blog posts: Smart SEO > Meta tags > Articles > Edit > Generate. Pages and special pages can be handled the same way in their own sections. [L1321.022]
- Generate and choose meta tags in Smart SEO:
  1. Open Smart SEO > Meta tags > Articles and click Edit on the post.
  2. Click Generate.
  3. Enter the target keyword(s) in the keyword field, for example `customizing your standing desk` plus `ultimate guide`.
  4. To change the tone, select a different tone and click Generate again. Each run gives a new meta title option and a new meta description option.
  5. Compare the generated options with the ChatGPT meta description and keep the stronger one.
  6. Enter the chosen meta title and meta description into the post's search engine listing in Shopify and save.
  [L1321.023]
- The Smart SEO meta tag view may still show old values right after a post has changed. Reload the view or check the saved values in Shopify before relying on them. [L1321.024]

### Bulk rewrites from Search Console data

- Bulk SEO update workflow with an AI tool:
  1. Pull query-level Search Console data for the chosen pages over about 90 days, including impressions, position and clicks for each query.
  2. Scrape the current title, meta title, H1 and intro of each page so the AI can compare them with the new targets.
  3. Ask the AI to write rewrites for each page, anchored to its highest-opportunity query, and to output a CSV with one row per page.
  4. Export the CSV to a spreadsheet, edit it, and check it critically for topic changes, duplicated text and weak targets.
  5. Verify the CSV before import: every row should have the same number of columns, and quoting should be correct.
  6. Apply the approved changes to Shopify in bulk, either through a bulk import app or a Shopify MCP connection.
  7. Run the internal link rules through the same process: review outside Shopify first, then apply.
  [L1269.035]
- Bulk rewrite prompt:

```
You are working on an online store. For each page URL in the list below, use its Search Console query data from the last 90 days. Pick the single highest-opportunity query for each page, preferring queries with many impressions and positions just outside the top results. Then write a new H1, a new meta title, a new meta description and a new first paragraph that stay on the page's existing subject, lead with the target query, and capture the secondary queries naturally in the intro. Do not change the subject of a page, and do not optimize a page around just one item from a set of similar topics. Output a CSV with the columns URL, section, clicks (90d), primary target, impressions, average position, current H1, new H1, current meta title, new meta title, new meta description and new intro. Flag any page where the best target query does not fit the page's topic.
```
[L1269.036]

- The same CSV rewrite pipeline used for blog posts can be extended to product meta titles, product intros, collection meta titles, collection intros and collection H1s. Build each group from its own query data, and rewrite product or collection pages only where search data shows real demand. [L1269.051]
- Rewriting the title, meta title and first paragraph of a ranking page is among the fastest ways to improve it. Target the highest-opportunity query or the striking-distance query, and capture secondary query variants in the intro. [L1269.019]
- When a query has many impressions but zero clicks, the snippet is probably not answering what the searcher wants. Rewrite the title and intro to answer that exact question directly, for example by stating the name of the thing being asked about. [L1269.020]
- Check that an AI rewrite keeps the same subject as the existing page. If the new H1 and meta title describe a different entity than the one ranking now, the page can lose its current rankings. [L1269.022]
- Click-prompting title framings such as myth-busting can raise CTR. Use them only when the content is accurate and delivers on the promise of the title. [L1269.023]
- Example: a fear-of-sharks article had about 617 impressions for `phobia of sharks` and about 604 for `fear of sharks`, both near position 8, yet zero clicks. The snippet never named the medical term for the fear that searchers were looking for. Putting that term into the title and opening lines lets the result answer the question directly. [L1269.046]
- Example: a great-white-shark facts article earned 80 clicks in the last 90 days for the query `facts about great white sharks`, at about position 10.5 with about 814 impressions. The rewrite kept the page on its broad subject and captured secondary queries such as `10 fun facts about great white sharks` and `interesting facts about great white sharks` in the intro. A myth-busting meta title was proposed to raise clicks, but only if the content delivers on that promise. [L1269.047]

### Pre-publish checks

- Check the listing before publishing: confirm the search title matches the product title, the URL is clean, and the description is a short text rather than the auto-pulled main description. [L1166.020]
- Run this checklist for every new page, and go back and do it for existing pages that lack custom meta data:
  1. Pick the focus keyword for the page.
  2. Put the focus keyword in the meta title.
  3. Put the focus keyword in the meta description.
  4. Put the focus keyword in the URL.
  5. End the meta description with a call to action.
  6. Make sure structured data is installed and up to date.
  7. Optimize the page for sitelinks where possible.
  8. Repeat this checklist for every new page, and go back and do it for existing pages that lack custom meta data.
  [L1026.009]
- The Page title and Description in Edit website SEO must be accurate at launch. [L1264.004]

## Structured Data and Rich Results

### Why structured data matters for CTR

- Structured data, schema markup and rich snippets all refer to the same thing: code added to a website that gives search engines extra data about the business, products and stock, which search results can display. [L1349.001]
- Schema markup can pull review ratings, product price and stock status into search results, and search engines show this data alongside the normal listing. [L1349.002]
- Structured data is installed separately from the meta title and description. [L1026.003]
- Product listings can show star ratings from reviews, the product price and the category. These details make a listing stand out and can raise click-through rate. [L1190.031]
- Structured data passes store information to Google in the format Google expects, so product listings can show more details. Shown details can include the product title, URL, star ratings, price and stock status. [L1337.008]
- Structured data helps a result look more trustworthy and informative, which tends to lift CTR. [L1026.003]
- Rich results raise CTR, which signals to Google that the page should rank higher. [L1227.028]
- CTR from search results is a relevance signal for Google, so more clicks on a listing with strong reviews can also support organic rankings. Review markup is one way to make a listing stand out. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1012.005]
- A result with rich snippets draws more attention and clicks than competing results without markup, so it can indirectly help rankings. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1349.007]
- Add rich snippets, meaning Schema.org structured data markup, to product pages so that price, availability and ratings appear directly in search results, which increases click-through rate. [L0176.007]
- Use rich snippet data (reviews, price and stock) in search results. [L0575.013]
- CTR improves most when the URL, the title, the meta description and the structured data work together. JSON-LD lets organic listings and blog posts show rich snippets, which can raise CTR on pages and blog posts. [L1321.021]
- The more business information you enter in the Organization schema, such as email, description, address, social profiles and logo, the more Google can pass along to search results. Complete entries help raise CTR. [L1337.014]

### Ranking effect: sources disagree

| Source | Claim | When it applies | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | Structured data is not a direct Google ranking factor. Adding it does not raise rankings by itself. Its benefit comes indirectly through more clicks. | Always, per this source | [L1349.006] |
| Source A | Higher CTR from rich results signals that the page should rank higher. | Where rich results are shown | [L1227.028] |
| Source A | CTR from richer results can support rankings. | Where rich results are shown | [L1337.008] |

Use the indirect view as the baseline. Expect ranking support only through clicks. [L1349.006, L1227.028, L1337.008]

### What the rich snippet looks like

- A product search result can show a star rating with review count, the price and an "In stock" status. The stars come from Review schema, the price and stock status from Product schema. The review data may come from a review app, while the markup code is separate. [L1349.004]
- Review schema displays yellow star ratings that stand out strongly from the rest of a search listing and attract the eye. This only helps when the product's rating is good. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1349.008]
- Product schema lets Google show price, stock status, review stars, delivery and return information for product pages in organic results. Smart SEO includes schema, and judge.me adds review stars. [L1116.052]
- The handling days, transit days, return policy and shipping details entered in structured data should match what is shown on the store's own pages. Different values can create inconsistent information for Google. [L1337.019]

### Schema types to enable

| Type | Use | Condition | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Website | Enable as the first step of the setup | Always | [L1337.013] |
| Organization | Enable with business name, URL, contact and logo | Always | [L1337.013, L1337.014] |
| Product | Main type for product pages, enabled after Organization | Always for product pages | [L1337.013, L1349.003] |
| Review | Star ratings in results (yellow stars) | Only when the product has good ratings | [L1349.003, L1349.004, L1349.008] |
| Local business | Location data | Only when the business has a physical local presence. Online-only stores keep it off | [L1337.015, L1349.003] |
| Collection | Lets Google recognise the page as a collection | Turn on after Product schema | [L368.018, L1337.020] |
| Breadcrumbs | Navigation path in results | Turn on after Product schema | [L1337.020] |
| Article | Blog posts | Turn on after Product schema | [L1337.020, L1321.020] |
| FAQ | Questions and answers in results | Only once the store has a growing set of questions and answers, for example from a Q&A app. Not needed at launch | [L1337.021] |
| Recipe and How-to | Recipe and instructional content | Keep off unless the store publishes that kind of content | [L1337.022] |

- Only enable structured data types that match real content on the store. [L1337.022]
- For most e-commerce stores, Review schema and Product schema are the main types. Local business schema is mainly relevant to businesses with a physical location, and only some of their pages need it. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1349.003]

### Choosing the implementation: app or theme code

- Some Shopify themes include schema markup in the Liquid code for product pages. Check the theme for existing markup before adding anything, to avoid duplicating or conflicting with it. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1349.009]
- Most Shopify themes have rich snippet data on by default. Verify that it is turned on. [L0575.013]
- JSON-LD structured data is added to a Shopify store with an app, not by theme code alone. The app originally recommended for it was rebranded in 2023. Stores that already use JSON-LD can keep it in place. [L1114.075]
- Writing schema markup by hand and keeping it updated is a recurring burden, because schema formats change and outdated markup stops showing in search results. Most store owners who are not coders would need to pay a developer for every update. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1349.010]
- On Shopify, the recommended way to implement schema markup is a dedicated schema app, not a manual developer job. The app's developers update the markup automatically, so the store owner does not need to request updates. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1349.011]
- Even if a Shopify theme already includes some structured data, install a schema app. Theme developers often do not maintain that markup, so a managed app is more reliable. Schema apps cost money, but the course presents them as a worthwhile investment. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1349.012]

### Smart SEO setup

Enable JSON-LD in Smart SEO and check each part:

1. Go to Apps > Smart SEO > JSON-LD. [L1321.020]
2. Check that structured data is valid for blogs and articles and covers every place where rich snippets are needed. [L1321.020]
3. In the Collections setting, tick the Collections checkbox so the collection structured data is added. [L368.018]

Follow this procedure for the Pro plan:

1. Open the JSON-LD section of the app. [L1337.013]
2. Turn on Website schema. [L1337.013]
3. Turn on Organization schema. [L1337.013]
4. Enter the business name written as words, not as a domain. For example, enter the brand name, not a.com address. [L1337.013]
5. Enter the store URL. [L1337.013]
6. Fill in email, description, address and social profiles. These fields are optional but should be filled out as completely as possible. [L1337.013]
7. Upload the store logo. [L1337.013]
8. Click Save. [L1337.013]
9. Keep Local business turned off for a non-local online store. [L1337.013, L1337.015]
10. Turn on Product schema. [L1337.013]
11. Click Configure and link your Judge.me product reviews. This requires Judge.me installed on the store. [L1337.013]
12. Click Save. [L1337.013]

Then turn on the additional types:

1. Turn on Collection schema. [L1337.020]
2. Turn on Breadcrumbs. [L1337.020]
3. Turn on Article schema for the blog. [L1337.020]

Case: after one setup in Smart SEO, the store has its logo, social URLs, price range, phone number display and Judge.me review connection. Product listings can then show the star rating, price and category directly in Google results, which makes them more attractive than competitor listings. [L1190.046]

### Reviews in rich results

- In Judge.me, the review widget title can be set to load with the product title through a setting in the app's settings area. In Stamp.io, this requires adding a small piece of code to the site, which the vendor provides. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1191.018]
- A review widget title containing the product name, for example "product reviews", can help the product page rank for searches for that product's reviews. This only works when the product actually has reviews to show. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1191.019]
- Reviews from supplier websites can be used even if they are 2 to 3 years old. Hiding review dates is not a known policy violation. [L964.135]
- In Google Search Console under Review snippets, all product pages with reviews should be valid (green). Invalid review snippets usually mean a code problem on the site. When reviews come from an app such as Judge.me, valid snippets allow review stars to show with product pages in search results. [L1271.008]

### Testing and verification

- Google's free Structured Data Testing Tool checks whether Google can read the structured data on a given page URL. It is the reliable way to verify schema installation, instead of judging by search results. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1349.014]
- Procedure to check a page (older: Source B, 2021-22):
  1. Open Google's free Structured Data Testing Tool. [L1349.013]
  2. Enter the URL of the page you want to check. [L1349.013]
  3. Read whether Google reports that it can see structured data on that page. [L1349.013]
  4. If Google detects the structured data, the implementation is working, even if the rich snippet does not show in search results. [L1349.013]
  5. If Google does not detect it, the schema is not installed correctly and needs to be fixed. [L1349.013]
- The Structured Data Testing Tool named in the lesson has been replaced by newer Google tools, such as the Rich Results Test. Before following the steps above, confirm the current tool Google offers for checking structured data in 2026. [L1349.018]
- After a JSON-LD app is installed, resubmit and recrawl the sitemap. If Search Console still shows few snippets after that, contact the app's own support, who can check the settings. [L1285.044]

### Limits and common mistakes

- Google does not always show structured data in search results, even when it is installed correctly. Results can differ by query and by searcher. Searching your own product and not seeing the rich snippet does not prove that the schema is broken. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1349.005]
- Only turn on Local business for product schema if the business has a physical local presence. Online-only stores keep it off. [L1337.015]
- Recipe and How-to schema should stay off unless the store publishes that kind of content. [L1337.022]
- FAQ schema is not needed at launch. Turn it on only when the store has a growing set of questions and answers. [L1337.021]

#### Further details

- Smart SEO app > JSON-LD > Enable structured data: make sure the Collections checkbox is ticked. This adds collection structured data so Google can recognise the page as a collection. [L0368.018]

- Use the reviews from supplier websites, even if they are 2 to 3 years old. Hiding review dates is not a known policy violation. [L0964.135]

## Product Page SEO

### Timing and Priority

- Product pages are the pages that convert into sales, so SEO effort goes there before other content. [L0469.099]
- Apply product page SEO to every product page, not just one or two, and use the same keyword and product information standards across the whole catalog so every product matches its relevant searches. [L1254.016]
- Product page SEO makes products appear for the exact product names shoppers search for and for closely related niche terms. Relevance between the search and the product page decides whether a product shows up at all. [L1254.015]
- Keyword research for product descriptions is done after launch, once ads are live and traffic is coming in. It is not required before uploading products. [L0970.125]
- Launch with supplier-provided descriptions. After two to four weeks, check which products already receive impressions, clicks and sales, then rewrite and enhance those descriptions first. [L0471.045]
- Start with the top 20% of products by traffic. You do not need to rewrite every description at once. [L1190.017]
- For a large catalog, writing unique descriptions for every product is too much work for too little return. Focus the effort on pages that already receive traffic. (Older: 2019.) [L1275.037]
- Products you want to rank for need unique, longer descriptions. Give your writing time to those products first and decide which descriptions get the most effort. (Older: 2018.) [L1274.005]

#### Finding the Highest-Traffic Pages

**Procedure (older: 2021)**

1. Pull a monthly report in Google Analytics.
2. Open the Content section.
3. Sort pages by traffic.
4. Add unique copy to the highest-traffic pages first, then work down the list. [L0671.133]

**Procedure (older: 2019)**

1. Upload all products with the supplier's specifications as a starting description.
2. Run Google Shopping ads to drive traffic.
3. In Google Analytics, open Behavior > Site Content to see which product URLs visitors view most.
4. Write unique product descriptions for the top 20 percent of those pages, following the 80-20 rule. [L1275.035]

- Unique descriptions on the most visited product pages raise conversion, because shoppers get more information. The same pages also tend to rank better organically. (Older: 2019.) [L1275.036]
- For a monthly view of traffic, use the Content report. For a view of which product URLs visitors view most, use Behavior > Site Content. [L0671.133, L1275.035]
- Rewrite the pages that receive the most traffic in Google Analytics first. (2023.) [L0470.032]

### Supplier Descriptions: Launch Versus Rewrite

Sources agree that the target is a unique description. They differ on whether copied text is acceptable at launch. Both positions are listed below, with the conditions for each.

**Target state**

- Write unique product descriptions for every product. Copied manufacturer or competitor descriptions do not rank in search results. [L0337.040]
- Product descriptions must be at least 300 words long and must be unique, not copied from other sources. [L1467.015]
- Use reverse image search and competitor product pages to find more information for descriptions. Write descriptions that differ from the supplier's copy to avoid duplicate content. (Older: 2021.) [L0801.091]
- Use an SEO-based method for product pages rather than generic AI-generated text. (2025.) [L0966.099]

**Acceptable at launch, rewritten later**

- Copied supplier descriptions do not cause a blacklist, but original descriptions tend to rank better over time, so rewrite them as traffic grows. [L0970.126]
- Supplier product descriptions copied at launch are generally acceptable, because duplicate manufacturer copy is common across many sites. Later, find the highest-traffic product pages and rewrite them to be unique and more useful. (2023.) [L0470.032]
- When a new supplier is approved, its default catalog descriptions can be uploaded on day one and rewritten later. Leaving them unchanged at launch does not hurt the product page. [L1190.049]
- Supplier stock descriptions are used by many stores, and duplicate content is not a reason to avoid them, because product content can still rank well. Rewriting them gives better results, so do it when time allows. [L1190.015]
- Stock supplier descriptions are the base of product descriptions, then built out in the SEO lesson of module six. [L0964.140]
- In the early launch phase, supplier descriptions and specifications may be copied and pasted into the store, because duplicate content matters little for paid ads and speed to the first sale matters more. Rewrite them later during the growth phase. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1163.019]

**Older position**

- Copying a competitor's product descriptions or supplier default text weakens your chances of ranking for those products, because search engines see duplicate content. (Older: 2018.) [L1274.006]

### Product Titles

**Principles**

- The product title is the most reliable keyword target because it is the H1 heading and the most relevant term for that page. [L0881.007]
- Broad product type terms are better left to collection or content pages, unless the site is small and the product is the only one of its type. [L0881.007]
- Use a unique custom title instead of the generic supplier listing name. Unique names usually face less competition on Google when shoppers search for them. [L1207.001]
- Titles and descriptions should target the brand name, the supplier's name and product identifiers, rather than many extra keywords. [L0964.141]
- Match product titles and URL slugs to how top future competitors write them. Include SKU or variant numbers only if those competitors include them. (2024.) [L0471.036]
- Titles should match what buyers actually search for, such as brand, color and product type, rather than the long technical name from the supplier. Full technical details and variations belong in the description. (Older: 2018.) [L1085.042]

**Title order**

Sources give different orders. Each is listed with its conditions.

| Order | Conditions | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Brand name, product name, SKU number | Standard order. Variants may occasionally be included. (2023.) | [L0470.093] |
| Brand name, product name, SKU number | Format in Smart SEO. (2025.) | [L1097.037] |
| Brand, model name, product type, optional descriptor | Pattern for brands with sibling products. Separates the item from similar products of the same brand. (Older: 2021-22.) | [L1163.017, L1163.025] |
| Brand, product name, SKU, supplier identifier | Built into the product name field for long-tail targeting. (Older: 2022.) | [L0854.062] |
| Search term in the exact order people type it | Used when optimizing from search data. | [L1271.015] |

**Formula (older: 2021-22)**

Structure each product title as:

Brand > Model name > Product type > Optional descriptive words that separate the item from similar products of the same brand

Example pattern: Brand Model Product type in Colour

Search engines use the title as a primary signal to match products to shoppers' searches, so the product type (for example electric skateboard) should be included even if the supplier omits it. [L1163.017]

Examples in the same pattern:

- Trailride Lumen 2 carbon road helmet in matte white
- Harbor Loft 4 ceramic pour-over coffee dripper in sand

It works because shoppers search by product type and search engines treat the title as a main signal for matching a product to a query. [L1163.025]

**Common mistake (older: 2021-22)**

- Supplier product titles often lack the brand name or the product type, and that gap hurts search matching. Edit titles when uploading. A title with only a brand and model number should have the product type and colour added. [L1163.018]

**Search-term order**

Example: change 'Source A zip up hoodie' to 'Source A hoodie zip up' when shoppers type the second form. [L1271.015]

**Keyword choice**

- On a product page, the focus keyword should be the product name, brand name and SKU or model number, not the broader niche keyword. Keyword cannibalization becomes a real problem mainly when many blog posts cover related topics, or when a collection page targets the same keyword as the product pages. (2024.) [L0666.049]
- For the target keyword in a rewrite, use the short product name that starts with the product type, not the brand name, size or finish. Shorter names match what shoppers actually search for. [L1190.022]

Sources differ on the focus keyword. L0666.049 uses the product name with brand and SKU or model. L1190.022 uses the short product name starting with the product type. Use L0666.049 for the page's focus keyword and L1190.022 when choosing the target keyword for a description rewrite.

**Keyword in the title**

- The focus keyword should be placed as early as possible in the page title, since the title is the H1 of the product page. [L1191.005]
- Place the focus keyword at the front of the page title. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.001]
- Smart SEO rule: do not put targeted keywords in the URL or the title. (2025.) [L1097.037]

Sources differ: L1097.037 forbids keywords in the title, while L1191.005 and L1191.001 place them at the front. L1097.037 applies to the Smart SEO workflow. Outside it, place the focus keyword at the front of the title.

**Feed title**

- Use the SEO-optimized product title as the Shopify page title. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1170.023]

### URLs and Product Handles

**Decision: whether to include a SKU in the URL (older: 2021)**

1. Search for the product on Google without the SKU.
2. Look at the top organic results, not the ads.
3. If the top results show one product page with all variants selectable, leave the SKU out of the URL and keep all variants on one page.
4. If the top results are split by SKU, include the SKU and create a separate page for each SKU. [L0469.098]

**Handle changes**

- Do not change the product handles, and do not put targeted keywords in the URL. (Smart SEO, 2025.) [L1097.037]
- Change the URL handle to the short keyword form (for example Source A-hoodie) and let Shopify create the automatic redirect from the old URL. [L1271.015]
- Use a short page URL containing the focus keyword. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.001]
- When optimizing a product, keep the URL the same. Avoid changing the URL unless needed, and use redirects when URLs must change. (2023.) [L0964.139]

Sources differ. L1097.037 says do not change handles. L1271.015 changes the handle when optimizing from search data, and Shopify creates the redirect. L0964.139 says keep the URL unless a change is needed, and use redirects for any change. Use L1097.037 under Smart SEO. Otherwise change a handle only when the search data justifies it, and create a redirect as L1271.015 and L0964.139 describe.

### Variants Versus Separate Listings

**Decision procedure**

1. Search Google for the product name without any color or variant word.
2. Check how the top results list their variants.
3. If the top results list variants together on one page, do the same.
4. If they break variants apart, break them apart too.
5. Test the choice over time, because it varies by supplier and product. [L0359.077]

**Competitor check (2025)**

- When choosing product variants vs separate product listings, check how the top 2-3 competitors handle the same products, and replicate their structure. [L0255.062]

**Competitor check (older: 2022)**

- Check the top two to three competitors in the niche. If they use separate product pages for variants, use separate pages. If they use product options, use options. [L0969.081]

**Shopify handling**

- Shopify product variant handling should follow what the top search results do for the same product name, because variant pages affect search and Google Shopping results. [L0359.078]
- Use the niche keyword in the store name and in product variant pages. Breaking variants into separate pages usually creates more direct traffic for specific keywords, but test it. [L0359.076]

Sources differ in emphasis. L0359.076 notes that separate variant pages usually bring more direct traffic for specific keywords. L0359.077 and L0969.081 say to follow the top results. Use the competitor check by default, and test separate pages where you expect direct traffic for specific keywords. [L0359.076, L0359.077, L0969.081]

**Size and color tables**

- Unique product content such as size tables, color tables and variant charts helps both SEO and the customer's buying decision. Presenting your own version of this information also makes your listings different from competitors. [L1357.030]

### Keyword Placement

**Where keywords belong**

- Place keywords throughout the product page: in the title, bullet points, description, and the Seller Central keyword field. Write naturally for human readers, not search bots, but ensure keywords appear organically. [L0228.022]
- A week-long oversight omitting keywords from Seller Central significantly reduced visibility and conversions until corrected. [L0228.022]
- Use the niche keyword in the store name. [L0359.076]

**Long-tail keyword procedure (older: 2022)**

1. Build the keyword from the brand (supplier name), the product name, the SKU and any unique supplier identifier.
2. Put the full string in the product name field.
3. Repeat it in the product description and the SEO description section of the product page.
4. Do not stuff the same phrase repeatedly. [L0854.062]

**Focus keyword and synonyms**

- Choose a focus keyword plus relevant synonyms, based on the keyword research already done for that product. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.001]
- Keywords from the product's own search data are used first. Keyword research for product descriptions is done after launch. [L0970.125]

**Revenue-based keywords (Google Ads)**

Procedure:

1. Identify the high-converting Google Ads keywords by revenue.
2. Place them in the product title.
3. Place them in the product description.
4. Place them in the meta title and meta description.

This improves organic conversions and helps your ads show for those keywords more often. [L1357.031]

**Keyword counts**

Sources give different counts. Each applies to a different measure.

| Measure | Value | When it applies | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Occurrences in the product description | 3 to 5 times | Effective optimization without keyword-stuffing penalties | [L0176.004] |
| Occurrences on top-visited pages | 3 to 5 times | The product name counts as one occurrence, so two mentions in the rewritten description plus the product name on the page gives three | [L1190.024] |
| Occurrences across the whole page | 5 to 10 times | Typical placements that reach 5: page title, one H2 heading, the first 100 words of body copy, one product image file name, one product image alt text (older: 2021-22) | [L1191.010] |
| Number of distinct keywords on the page | 3 to 5 keywords | Include the brand name, product name, and product ID such as a SKU or GTIN when one exists (2023) | [L1094.097] |

- Placing the target keyword in nearly every line of the description causes keyword stuffing, which reads awkwardly and hurts the page. Use the keyword naturally. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.011]
- Include the focus keyword at least once in the first 100 words of plain text, most often the product description. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.009]

**Google and the feed**

- Google reads the product description first and the features second when it reads the Merchant Center feed. [L0964.136]

### Writing the Description

**Procedure**

1. Write at least 300 words that are unique to the product.
2. Describe the important features of the product.
3. Explain how each feature benefits the customer.
4. End with a call to action that encourages the customer to buy the product.
5. Add a list of 4 dot points that highlight the main features.
6. Add a list of technical details such as dimensions, weight and similar specifications. [L1467.016]

**Length and readability**

- Product descriptions should contain at least 300 unique words. Below this, Google may treat the page as thin content, which is why the threshold matters for indexing. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.013]
- Product descriptions should be set in at least 18pt font and use short sentences and short paragraphs to keep the text readable. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.012]
- Long, technical or expensive products should be chunked with subheadings, each describing the few paragraphs beneath it. Short product descriptions do not need this. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1099.014]
- Putting too much information on a product page is said to hurt SEO. Keep product page tabs to basic information at launch and expand them later. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1300.024]

**Content formula (older: 2021)**

Describe what the product is, who it is for, and what problem it solves, with specifics about use and fit. Such content works because it answers the questions a buyer types into search, which helps the product rank and convert. [L0801.089]

Example 1: Solid oak dining table that seats six, with a finish that resists scratches, for families who host weekly dinners.

Example 2: Compact standing desk with a bamboo top and a 60 to 120 cm height range, for home offices with limited space.

### Headings

- Each product page should have exactly one H1 tag, which is the page or product title. No other H1 tags should appear on the product page. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.004]
- Headings in the description area should be H2 tags. No heading tagged H1 to H6 should ever contain a link. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.006]
- Links inside heading tags are a negative signal for Google, so they should never be used on any page type. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.007]
- The focus keyword should appear in at least one H2 heading, preferably the first one. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.008]
- Tag every heading in the description area as H2, with no link attached. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.001]

### Images and Video

**Feed-optimized page procedure (older: 2021-22)**

1. Use the SEO-optimized product title as the Shopify page title.
2. Give images descriptive file names.
3. Write a unique alt tag for each image.
4. Write a solid product description. [L1170.023]

**Image rules (older: 2021-22)**

- Make product images unique where possible, each with a unique descriptive file name. At least one image, ideally the first, carries the focus keyword in its file name. [L1191.001]
- Compress product images as far as possible without visible quality loss. [L1191.001]
- Give images in the description area unique file names and alt tags. [L1191.001]
- Typical placements for the focus keyword include one product image file name and one product image alt text. [L1191.010]

**Video rules (older: 2021-22)**

- Load videos in the description area with a thin (lazy) load. [L1191.001]

### Question and Answer Sections

- Add a question and answer section to product pages. It serves shoppers and supports SEO. [L0894.030]
- Add a short question-and-answer section near the end of each product description, using an H2 or H3 heading for each question. Only include questions and answers that do not already appear on the FAQ page or in other content on the site. (Older: 2021-22.) [L0658.009]
- Product-page Q&A sections can improve conversion rates by answering buyer questions on the spot. They also add unique content to product pages and give search engines extra question-and-answer material to display. (Older: 2021-22.) [L0658.010]

### Meta Title and Meta Description

- Give the meta title and meta description a unique text that includes the focus keyword. (Older: 2021-22.) [L1191.001]
- Set the page title to the keyword, followed by the brand name. Rewrite the meta description with the keyword at the front. [L1271.015]
- Meta titles and descriptions can be improved with keywords when the URL stays the same. [L0964.139]
- Place the revenue-based keywords in the meta title and meta description. [L1357.031]
- Check the meta description on each listing as part of the checklist. [L0337.043]

### Shopping Feed and Search Listing

- Customize product page copy for SEO. Google Merchant Center pulls information from product pages, so better-optimized pages can rank higher in free listings. (Older: 2021.) [L0671.134, L0671.269]
- Aim to rank in the top 1 to 3 organic Google results for your brand name plus product name by improving your product pages. This gives you two spots on the first page when you also run Google Shopping ads. (2023.) [L0804.060]

**Search engine listing check (Source E, 2025-26)**

1. Open the Search engine listing section in the product.
2. Read the title and description and make sure they are readable and make sense.
3. Shopify pulls them automatically from the product title and description, so fix any gibberish before saving. [L1331.015]

### Using Search Data After Launch

**Optimization procedure**

1. Open the product page on the live store to see how it currently looks.
2. Open the same product in the Shopify admin and edit it.
3. Change the product title so it contains the search term in the exact order people type it (e.g. 'Source A hoodie zip up' instead of 'Source A zip up hoodie').
4. In the SEO section, set the page title to the keyword, followed by the brand name.
5. Rewrite the meta description with the keyword at the front.
6. Change the URL handle to the short keyword form (e.g. 'Source A-hoodie') and let Shopify create the automatic redirect from the old URL.
7. Save the page.
8. Check Google Search Console again after two weeks for more impressions, a better average position and a higher CTR. [L1271.015]

### AI-Assisted Writing

**Principles**

- Use ChatGPT for outlines and bullet ideas for product or collection content, then expand and rewrite the text in a human voice. Fully AI-generated text is easy to spot and may be penalized. (2024.) [L0471.044]
- AI writing tools such as Copy.ai can produce ideas and drafts for product descriptions, but they do not replace human copywriting. Use them to break writer's block or generate variations, then edit the result. (Older: 2021.) [L0801.090]
- The free version of ChatGPT is enough to rewrite product descriptions. A paid plan is not required. Manual rewriting is also possible if you prefer not to use AI. [L1190.021]
- AI-written product descriptions can include details that are not true for the product. Compare every claim with the real product specifications and remove anything unverifiable before publishing. [L1190.023]

**Rewrite procedure with ChatGPT**

1. Open the top-visited product page in Shopify and copy the supplier-provided description.
2. Open ChatGPT and paste the prompt from the pattern below, replacing the placeholder with the supplier description in quotes.
3. Choose the target keyword: use the short product name that starts with the product type, not the brand name, size, or finish.
4. Paste the product name into the prompt in place of the placeholder and send it.
5. Review the output options and take the parts you like; you do not have to use everything.
6. Check that the text matches the actual product and contains no claims that are not true.
7. Paste the approved text into the Shopify product description, replacing the old text.
8. Proofread it, verify the data, and edit anything you want to change. [L1190.019]

**Prompts**

Direct response structure for product pages (2024 prompt, full text):

```
Direct response copywriting for product pages uses a proven structure: open with a compelling headline, provide 2-4 lines describing the offering, itemize distinctive features in bullet format, then explain the customer benefits of each feature in bullets. Incorporate your primary keyword at least twice throughout the copy.
```

[L0176.009]

Product description prompt (2024, full text):

```
Take on the role of a direct-response copywriter. Draft a product description for [product name and/or URL] in this layout: a headline, one to three sentences of description, a features-and-benefits section, and a group of bullet points. Draw only on facts found in the product data you were given, make up no specifications, and before the text goes live, recast the finished copy in a natural, human tone.
```

[L0471.043]

Rewrite prompt (full text):

```
Please act as a direct response copywriter. Rewrite this product description: '[PASTE SUPPLIER DESCRIPTION]'. Use this format: Headline, a two to four sentence description, features and benefits, and bullet points. Please include the target keyword, which is the product name [PRODUCT NAME], at least two times. Use only facts that appear in the original description and do not add any claim that could be untrue.
```

[L1190.020]

**Quality and rewrite targets**

- Use an SEO-based method for product pages rather than generic AI-generated text. (2025.) [L0966.099]
- Aim for the target keyword 3 to 5 times on top-visited product pages. The product name counts as an occurrence. [L1190.024]

### On-Page SEO Checklist

**Listing checklist for each new product (2021)**

1. Write a unique product description rather than copying the manufacturer's or a competitor's text, since copied text does not rank.
2. Add product tags.
3. Add a meta description.

A listing missing any of these items means the listing is not finished. [L0337.043]

**On-page checklist (older: 2021-22)**

Run the product page on-page SEO checklist in this order:

1. Choose a focus keyword plus relevant synonyms, based on the keyword research already done for that product.
2. Make sure visible breadcrumb navigation is present on the page.
3. Tag the page title (usually the product title) as H1 and place the focus keyword at the front of it.
4. Make sure product images are unique where possible, each with a unique descriptive file name, and that at least one image (ideally the first) carries the focus keyword in its file name.
5. Compress product images as far as possible without visible quality loss.
6. Make sure the product image and the title are both visible above the fold on mobile, ideally with price and add-to-cart button too.
7. Tag every heading in the description area as H2, with no link attached.
8. Place the focus keyword in at least one H2 heading, preferably the first.
9. Place the focus keyword at least once in the first 100 words of plain text.
10. Use the focus keyword 5 to 10 times on the whole page.
11. Write the product description in at least 18pt font, with short sentences and short paragraphs.
12. Write at least 300 unique words in the product description.
13. Give images in the description area unique file names and alt tags.
14. Load videos in the description area with a thin (lazy) load.
15. Minimise boilerplate content across the page.
16. Load the review widget title dynamically with the product title included.
17. Use a short page URL containing the focus keyword.
18. Give the meta title and meta description a unique text that includes the focus keyword. [L1191.001]

## Collection Page SEO
