### Order of Work for Collection Pages

Collection pages are the most important pages for e-commerce SEO and should be treated as money pages. More category pages rank than product pages, and category pages usually convert better than individual product pages. [L1389.010]

Collection pages can rank organically in Google for free, so they deserve the same SEO work as product pages. Their images, descriptions, URLs and meta tags should be set up early, because collections are usually fewer than products and the work is quick. [L0368.001]

Shopify collections have no description by default, so adding one gives shoppers and search engines extra information from the start. This is a fast gain for stores that have only a few collections. [L0368.021]

Collection pages can rank for specific phrases, such as custom concrete countertops. Adding a custom section or category to a store can bring in buyers who already want to spend more. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0801.087]

Each collection must go through the same sequence. Completing it for only some collections leaves the rest without the benefit. [L0368.019]

1. Image optimization
2. Descriptions with keywords
3. Template layout
4. URL and handle
5. SEO title and meta description
6. JSON-LD [L0368.019]

### Indexing and Site Structure

Build one collection page per category and make it the content-rich, SEO-optimized version. Do not build two versions with and without content, because one strong page concentrates ranking signals. [L0667.033]

Before publishing a collection page, decide whether it should be indexed and where it fits in the site navigation. If it does not fit into the site structure, set it to noindex. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.001]

Collection pages built only to receive paid traffic are often orphan pages with no internal links. Since such a page is not meant to rank organically, noindexing it avoids diluting the SEO value of the rest of the site. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.002]

If a collection page should rank organically, it must be linked into the site structure, and it needs the same on-page optimization as any other indexed page. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.003]

Apply these collection page optimizations to new pages. Do not rewrite collection pages that already rank well. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.020]

### Collection Names and Search Intent

Collection pages target searches for a product type rather than a specific product, which is middle-of-funnel search intent. The focus keyword should match that intent. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.004]

Build category collection pages with buying-intent names, such as a generic collection for the product type and a for-sale collection. Turn each collection into a landing page so it ranks, and place your product at the top as the recommended choice. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0768.007]

### Keyword Research for Collections

To set the collection name and keyword, follow this procedure. [L0251.035]

1. Decide on your collection name based on the exact product type (e.g., 'Bamboo Standup Desks').
2. Go to Google and type your collection keyword.
3. Review the top organic search results to understand content expectations.
4. Scroll to the bottom of Google results and check 'Related searches' for long-tail keyword variations.
5. Incorporate these related keywords into your collection description and metadata. [L0251.035]

The keyword list comes from prior keyword research. The main keyword matches the collection name, and related keywords are added to it. [L0368.004]

To find related keywords for a collection, search the main keyword with modifiers such as 'best' and 'for beginners' (for example 'best hybrid surfboards' and 'hybrid surfboards for beginners') and note the variants that come up. Use these variants in the collection description and meta tags next to the main keyword. [L0368.020]

### Images and Media

Reduce image file size and dimensions on collection pages to improve page load speed. This benefits both search engine rankings and user experience. [L0177.001, L0178.001]

In a demo store, optimizing four collection images cut the total from 3.6 MB to just over 1000 KB, saving about 2.6 MB. Image compression is worth doing for collection pages, but a fast theme and few apps matter more for page speed. [L0368.003]

Compress any additional images on the collection page as far as possible. Give each one a unique file name and unique alt text. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.009]

Give every image in the description area a unique file name and unique alt text. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.005]

Videos in the description area should use a thin load, which keeps the page load time low. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.021]

### Descriptions and Content Length

Add descriptive text to collection pages as an SEO element. [L0178.002]

Collection descriptions should contain at least 300 unique words. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.014]

Each H3 section should carry about 100 words of copy. A collection page built on the full wireframe therefore needs several hundred words of unique text, not a few lines. [L0883.003]

AI-generated section copy often comes out at around 30 words, roughly a third of the target length. Expand each section to about 100 words, because shorter copy does not carry enough relevance to rank. [L0883.004]

Collection pages often have little meaningful content, so search engines can classify them as thin content. On a large site with many such pages, this can dilute overall SEO efforts, keep pages from being indexed, or leave indexed pages underperforming. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.015]

Thin collection pages made of a title, a product grid and only a few lines of text rarely rank. Only very large, well-known brands can rank such pages on brand authority alone, so smaller stores must add relevant on-page copy. [L0883.001]

Add some content to collection pages so they are not considered thin or duplicate content. Only a small number of collection pages should be made cornerstone pages, because if every page is treated as important, none stands out. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1465.037]

The fear that adding content to a collection page makes products harder to find is not supported by evidence, as long as the content is genuinely valuable to the user. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.016]

Valuable collection content helps the buyer decide which product in the category fits them. It can compare brands, explain price ranges and configurations, and show which option suits the buyer's usage conditions and experience level. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.017]

A collection page for one equipment category could explain which brands suit beginners versus advanced users, outline the price tiers, and describe which configuration works best for a given terrain or usage style. This guidance lets visitors reach the right product faster and gives search engines clear context about the page. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.018]

Helpful collection content gives search engines a clear signal of what the page is about. If competitors do not provide similar content for the same category, a page with it may be able to outrank them. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.019]

### Keyword Placement and Count

The focus keyword should appear 3 to 5 times in the collection description. [L0177.002, L0178.003, L0368.004]

The focus keyword should appear 5 times on the whole page. Headings, body text, image file names, image alt text and links to products on the page all count toward this. Collection pages often reach the count easily because product titles listed on the page can contain the keyword. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.012]

The focus keyword must appear at least once in the first 100 words of plain text on the collection page. Plain text on the page is recommended. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.011]

### Layout and Copy

Use this layout from top to bottom. [L0883.002]

1. H1 = the exact target keyword, with no variation.
2. Short description directly under the H1, about one to two sentences stating what the products do, placed above the product grid.
3. Product grid.
4. Descriptive copy below the grid, opening with an H2 that contains the target keyword in some form (an inverse phrasing such as 'Running Shorts for Men' for 'Men's Running Shorts' is acceptable).
5. Three H3 headings, each covering one unique selling point.
6. About 100 words of copy under each H3.
7. 3 to 5 internal links in total, acting as a mini navigation. [L0883.002]

Collection titles should be tagged as H1 and serve as the page title. Place the focus keyword at the front of the title where possible. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.006]

Headings in the collection description area should be H2 tags and must not carry a link. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.010]

Collection pages must display visible breadcrumb navigation. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.007]

The collection title must be visible above the fold on mobile, since it is the main signal that tells visitors they have reached a page matching their search. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.008]

Collection page content should use 18pt font, short sentences and short paragraphs. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.013]

### URLs and Handles

Collection URLs (the handle in Shopify's search engine listing) should match the collection name with dashes between words, for example /hybrid-surfboards, not run together. If an existing handle does not match the collection name, change it to match. [L0368.011]

The page URL should contain the focus keyword and be short. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.022]

Descriptive URLs that include the target keyword improve click-through rate from search results. [L0177.003]

### Meta Title and Meta Description

To set the SEO title, meta description and URL in Shopify, use this setting. [L0368.012]

In Shopify admin, open the collection, scroll to Search engine listing and click Edit. The fields are SEO title, SEO description and Handle (the URL). Use these fields to set the title, meta description and URL. [L0368.012]

SEO title formula for collections: main keyword + qualifying phrase for sale + a shipping benefit, e.g. `[Main keyword] for Sale | Free and Fast Shipping`. Adding these qualifiers turns the title into an ad-like snippet that gets more clicks. Example in the same pattern: `Standing Desks for Sale | Free Delivery in 3 Days`. Example in the same pattern: `Ergonomic Office Chairs for Sale | Free Returns and Fast Shipping`. [L0368.013]

The page meta title should be unique, include the focus keyword, and be kept short. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.023]

The page meta description should be unique and include the focus keyword. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.024]

Create custom meta descriptions for each collection page instead of using defaults to maximize click-through rate from search results. [L0177.004]

Rich snippets displayed on collection pages in search results increase click-through rate by showing additional product or collection information. [L0177.005]

Improve click-through rates on collection pages from search results by optimizing page URLs, writing persuasive meta descriptions, and implementing rich snippets that display additional information in search results. [L0178.004]

A generic meta description or title gives Google no reason to rank the page or show it to searchers. Adding the category, shipping and trust signals makes the search listing more attractive and raises click-through rate, which is the main reason to improve listings. [L0368.015]

The page title and meta description in a collection's search engine listing are what Google displays in search results for the keywords on your site. They can be left empty during the initial setup and filled in during the SEO phase. [L1312.012]

AI-generated meta tags are a starting point only. Review them and edit them before publishing, since a generic listing is worse than no listing at all. [L0368.017]

Filling in unique meta descriptions for all collections as a box-checking task will not bring visitors this week. Duplicate meta descriptions do not penalize pages, so this work should wait behind changes that affect clicks and rankings. [L1269.024]

Reusing the homepage social share description (OG description) across collections creates duplicate metadata. Give key collections their own descriptions as part of the targeted rewrite work, not as a blanket task. [L1269.027]

### Prompts and Templates for Collection Copy

Use this prompt to draft a collection description. [L0177.007]

```
Create a product collection description optimized for search engines. The copy should target [COLLECTION NAME] and strategically incorporate these search terms: [KEYWORD 1, KEYWORD 2, KEYWORD 3].
```

[L0177.007]

Use this prompt to draft the description from your keyword research. [L0368.005]

```
Act as an SEO specialist and draft an e-commerce collection description for [collection name], working in these keywords: [keyword 1], [keyword 2], [keyword 3], [keyword 4]. Take the primary keyword as the collection's name and swap the keyword placeholders for the related keyword list from your research. Review the result, keep the parts that work, and reword whatever feels stiff so it reads naturally and reflects what the collection really contains instead of using it as-is.
```

[L0368.005]

Use this template for the opening line of a collection description. [L0177.006]

```
Acquire [Collection Name] from [Brand] without delay. Complimentary shipping and protection apply to your entire [Products] order. Purchase immediately to unlock a complimentary [bonus] offer.
```

[L0177.006]

Use this template for a meta description draft. [L0368.014]

```
Buy [collection name] now from [brand] and enjoy free shipping plus insurance on every one of our [related keywords]. Place your order today to get a free bonus. (Optional: delete the bonus line if no bonus applies.) Fill the placeholders with the collection name, brand name and related keywords from your research, and use the result as a draft to revise rather than publishing it word for word.
```

[L0368.014]

Use this prompt to write the full collection page copy in the wireframe layout. [L0883.015]

```
You are an ecommerce SEO copywriter. Write search-optimized collection page copy for the brand [BRAND NAME], based only on the product catalog and brand facts below: [PASTE PRODUCT CATALOG AND BRAND FACTS]. Target keyword: [TARGET KEYWORD].

Follow this exact structure:
- H1: the target keyword, unchanged.
- Short description under the H1, 1 to 2 sentences, placed above the product grid.
- [PRODUCT GRID] placeholder.
- H2 that contains the target keyword or a natural inverse of it.
- Three H3 headings, each naming one unique selling point.
- About 100 words of copy under each H3.
- 3 to 5 internal links to these URLs: [LIST OF COLLECTION AND BLOG URLS].

Before writing, search Google US for the target keyword with the Firecrawl MCP, analyze the top 10 results, and list the entities they cover (related terms, features, use cases, activities). Work the relevant entities into the copy where they fit naturally.

Style rules: write in active voice, do not use em dashes, remove unnecessary fluff, and avoid these words: [BLACKLIST OF AI-TYPICAL WORDS].

Facts: use only facts from the catalog above. If a size, material, claim or certification is missing, write [PLACEHOLDER: missing fact] instead of inventing it.

Output: the copy in the order above, each section labeled with its heading level and word count, followed by a list of the internal links used and any section under 90 words.
```

[L0883.015]

To have AI write a collection page in your exact layout, attach a screenshot of the wireframe (H1, product grid, H2, three H3s with copy) to the prompt and tell the model to base the page on it. Submitting the image gives the model the structure directly, which works better than describing the layout in words. [L0883.030]

Before running a collection copy prompt, state what each H3 section must cover, such as inseam lengths, liner type, fabric, pockets or other real product features, and the roughly 100-word length per section. Without these details the model fills the sections with generic claims that need heavy rewriting. [L0883.031]

### On-Page Checklist

Run the full checklist for every new collection page, in this order. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.005]

1. Decide whether the page should be indexed and where it fits in the site structure; if it does not fit, set it to noindex. [L0367.001]
2. Choose a focus keyword and its relevant synonyms. [L0367.004]
3. Show visible breadcrumb navigation on the page. [L0367.007]
4. Tag the collection title as H1; it is also the page title. Place the focus keyword at the front of the title where possible. [L0367.006]
5. Make sure the collection title is visible above the fold on mobile. [L0367.008]
6. Compress any additional images as far as possible. [L0367.009]
7. Make headings in the description area H2 and do not attach a link to them. [L0367.010]
8. Use the focus keyword at least once in the first 100 words of plain text. [L0367.011]
9. Use the focus keyword 5 times on the page in total. [L0367.012]
10. Format collection page content in 18pt font with short sentences and short paragraphs. [L0367.013]
11. Write at least 300 unique words for the collection description. [L0367.014]
12. Give every image in the description area a unique file name and unique alt text. [L0367.009]
13. Give videos in the description area a thin load. [L0367.021]
14. Make the page URL contain the focus keyword and keep it short. [L0367.022]
15. Make the meta title unique and include the focus keyword. [L0367.023]
16. Make the meta description unique and include the focus keyword. [L0367.024]

Apply the checklist to every collection page as follows. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0367.025]

1. Run through the full collection page checklist every time a new collection page is created.
2. Tick off each item before the page goes live.
3. If someone else creates collection pages for you, give them the checklist and check that every item is ticked on each page.
4. If a page skips checklist items, ask why and have it corrected. [L0367.025]

### Where Sources Differ

Keyword count: the description should carry 3 to 5 uses of the focus keyword [L0177.002, L0178.003, L0368.004], while the whole page should carry 5 uses [L0367.012] (older, 2021-22). Both apply, because they measure different things: the description and the full page.

H1 wording: the H1 must be the exact target keyword with no variation [L0883.002], while the focus keyword should sit at the front of the title where possible [L0367.006] (older, 2021-22). Both fit when the title equals the keyword itself.

Meta descriptions: write unique ones for each collection [L0177.004, L0367.024] (older, 2021-22). Waiting is advised for filling them in across all collections as a box-checking task [L1269.024]. Apply the unique-description rule to new pages [L0367.020] (older, 2021-22) and to key collections in the targeted rewrite [L1269.027]. The search engine listing fields can stay empty during initial setup and be filled in during the SEO phase [L1312.012].

Image compression: compress images as a priority [L0177.001, L0178.001]. A fast theme and few apps matter more for page speed than compression alone [L0368.003].

Word count: at least 300 unique words for the description (older, 2021-22) [L0367.014] are consistent with about 100 words per H3 across three H3s, which gives several hundred words [L0883.003].

## Image SEO, Visual Search and Compression

### File names and image metadata

#### Filename rules

- Every image on a website must have a unique filename containing relevant keywords and a descriptive phrase, not generic defaults like "screenshot" or "image123". [L0301.009]
- Every image on a page should have a descriptive file name, and at least one image file name on the page should include the focus keyword. Google reads file names as one of several signals for what the page is about. [L0881.027]
- Every image on the site needs a short, descriptive file name, for example "black dog running" for a photo of a black dog running. Random strings of letters and numbers as file names are not allowed. [L1467.012]
- Image filenames help Google understand both what the image depicts and what the page is about, improving both image search and traditional search rankings. [L0301.007]
- Image file names should describe the product, such as the brand, model, product type and colour, instead of generic supplier names like "image 5467". A detail such as "side view" can be added for close-up shots. [L1163.013]

#### Filename patterns by page type

- **Product pages:** Name the first image with just the product name. Name subsequent images by combining the product name with what each image emphasizes. Example: "widget-blue-packaging" for the packaging detail, "widget-lifestyle-use" for the in-use shot. [L0301.010]
- **Products with several angles:** Use variations of the product name and include synonyms across different images. [L0301.011]
- **Target keyword:** Include the page's primary target keyword in at least one image filename. Use related synonyms and variations in the filenames of other images. [L0301.012]
- **Blog post images:** The filename should simply describe what the image shows (what it demonstrates, depicts or contains), not forced keywords. [L0301.013]
- **Older advice (2021-2022):** The source describes these patterns as current practice for Google Images. Treat the filename patterns as older advice and check them against current guidance. [L0301.010, L0301.011, L0301.012, L0301.013]

#### Images already published

1. Update the filename and alt tag of the existing image file.
2. Re-upload the modified image file to the site. Re-uploading applies the changes. [L0301.032]

#### Images in the product description

- Images placed in the product description area need unique file names and alt tags, just like the main product images. This is often skipped. [L1191.014]

### Alt text

#### Rules

- Write descriptive alt text for every product image on the product page. This improves SEO, supports accessibility, and provides context if images fail to load. [L0176.003]
- Each image on a page must have a unique alt tag describing that specific image. Using the same alt text for multiple images wastes the optimization opportunity and provides no additional ranking benefit. [L0301.014]
- Each image's alt tag must be unique and feature target keywords and synonyms related to that specific image, similar to how filenames should include keywords, to optimize both traditional search and visual search rankings. [L0301.029]
- Each product image needs a unique alt tag, a short descriptive phrase of what the image shows. Alt tags serve screen readers, display when an image fails to load, and help search engines including Google Images rank the page. [L1163.012]
- Give every product image its own alt text that names the product, its key feature and the view shown, kept short. [L1166.013]
- Add alt image tags to all images. [L1227.027]

#### What alt text does

- Alt text describes what an image shows. It helps screen readers for visually impaired users and gives Google more context. Accurate alt text can also help product images rank in Google image search. [L1190.012]
- Alt text displays when an image cannot load and helps screen reader users. It should describe the image and can include the focus keyword. [L0881.018]
- Google reads image file names and alt text to understand the page topic. [L0881.018]

#### Entering alt text in the editor

1. Click the image in the media section.
2. Enter the alt text.
3. Click out to save. [L1166.013]

### Image size and dimensions

- Product images should be 800x800 pixels. Never go below 600x600 pixels. [L0301.015]
- Cropping example: take a 3000x4000 supplier image and crop it to 800x800, focusing on a product detail. [L0301.023]

### File size and compression

#### Targets

- Compress images to reduce file size while maintaining quality. [L0226.022]
- Aim for images under 100 KB each. Images must not reach hundreds of megabytes. [L0226.022]
- Multi-megabyte product images make product pages load slower, which hurts page speed and organic rankings. Compress them; well-built compression keeps the visible quality unchanged. [L1190.004]
- Keep images small enough to load quickly. [L1227.027]

#### Compression principles

- Compress all images as far as possible without visibly degrading visual quality. Balance page speed against conversion-friendly image appearance. [L0301.016]
- The optimal compression point is when image quality begins to noticeably degrade. Most compression tools provide settings to identify this threshold. [L0301.017]
- Optimized images look the same to a visitor on a desktop but have smaller file sizes, so pages load faster. In one blog post, image optimization reduced the image file size by 64%. [L1321.004]

#### Formats and delivery

1. Compress images to reduce file size.
2. Use web-friendly formats: JPG, PNG, WebP. [L0226.022]
3. Keep images under 100 KB each. [L0226.022]
4. Use image CDNs or lazy loading to improve page speed. [L0226.022]

#### Tools

- **Image Optim:** A free desktop application for offline bulk image compression. Drag and drop images into it and adjust compression settings until quality is acceptable. [L0301.018]
- **Smart SEO (Shopify app):** Use it for bulk optimization of store files and blog posts (see procedure below). [L1321.003]

#### What not to use

- Do not use Shopify or other on-site image compression applications. Some inject JavaScript into the page code, which harms SEO. Compress images offline before uploading. [L0301.019]

#### Smart SEO procedure for pages and blog posts

1. Open the Smart SEO app.
2. Click Image optimization, then click Configure.
3. Open the Files section, which contains images used on the store, on pages and in other parts of the site.
4. Select all images and click Optimize file images. Let it run in the background.
5. Open the Articles section, which lists blog posts. Unoptimized posts are shown as not optimized.
6. Click optimize on each blog post. Posts added later appear in the Articles section and are optimized the same way. [L1321.003]

### Originality and image sourcing

#### Why unique images matter

- Use unique, original images rather than stock images when possible. Google's algorithm gives preferential ranking treatment to unique images. [L0301.021]
- For resellers and dropshippers selling products also offered by competitors, the first person to publish an image is typically considered to have the original version. Unique variations are weighted more heavily than duplicated stock images. [L0301.022]
- Original product photography taken with a smartphone produces high-definition images and is more effective for search rankings than stock images already used by other competitors. [L0301.024]
- While Google shows preferential treatment to unique images, it is not a major ranking factor. Prioritize domain authority and quality content first, then pursue unique images where practical rather than treating it as a critical optimization. [L0301.030]

#### Creating unique images from supplier files

- Crop specific sections from high-resolution supplier images to highlight particular features or angles. Example: take a 3000x4000 image from a supplier and crop it to 800x800, focusing on a product detail. This avoids the generic images all competitors use. [L0301.023]

#### Permitted sources (company rule)

- Images for content come from the company's shared image library or its Adobe Stock account. Do not use images from other sources. [L1467.011]

### Google Images and visual search

#### Scale and adoption (figures as of 2020, from a 2021-2022 source; older)

| Metric | Value | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| Google Lens uses | Over 1 billion times as of 2020 | [L0301.001] |
| Pinterest Lens visual searches | Over 600 million per month | [L0301.002] |
| US consumers who had used visual search at least once, as of 2020 | 36% | [L0301.003] |
| Objects Google Lens can recognize | Over 1 billion | [L0301.004] |

#### How visual search affects product discovery

- Visual search through Google Lens and Pinterest Lens is now a primary search method alongside text-based search. Image optimization is essential for product discovery. [L0301.005]
- Customers can use Google Lens to scan physical products in their environment and immediately find purchasing options. This works through shopping ads and organic search results, without knowing the product name or retailer. [L0301.006]

#### Ranking signals for Google Images

- Google maintains a separate ranking algorithm for image search. It operates independently from text-based search, so image optimization benefits rankings separately from standard on-page SEO. [L0301.028]
- Search engines use information from images to determine which search terms an entire page should rank for in traditional results. [L0301.008]
- Domain authority is a more important ranking signal in Google Images results. Building site authority through quality content, backlinks and technical SEO improves image search rankings automatically. [L0301.025]
- Google Images prioritizes fresher content. Recently updated pages rank higher for image searches than unchanged older pages. [L0301.026]
- Position images centrally on the page and place them higher up. Google Images prioritizes product pages where images are central to the content over category pages displaying several products. [L0301.027]

## Blog and Content Page Writing Standards

### Choosing the Page Type and Focus Keyword

- Decide per content piece whether it becomes a page or a blog post. Use a page for evergreen tools and static resources, such as a height chart calculator. Use a blog post for evergreen guides and how-to articles, such as how long to stand at a standing desk or how to customize one. Blog or page makes little difference to rankings. [L1321.006]
- Select a focus keyword and identify the relevant search terms before writing. [L0311.002]
- Choose an informational topic rather than a product page when the keyword is informational, because a product page would not rank for it. Example: the keyword *lined vs unlined running shorts* was handled as an informational article. [L0883.018]
- Take the topic and target keyword from your keyword research worksheet and from popular threads on community sites in your niche. [L1321.029]
- Study a strong reference page for its structure (sections, headings, spacing, link placement) rather than copying its design. The structural elements matter, not custom graphics. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.040]
- There is no one-size-fits-all blog post. The best content depends on the market. A structure checklist defines how the page is built, not what it must say. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.025]

### Researching the Top Results Before Writing

**Setup**

- Keyword research setup in Firecrawl: search engine Google, country US, analyze the top 10 organic results, and extract page copy and entities from each result. [L0883.013]
- Firecrawl MCP connected to Claude searches Google, scrapes the top-ranking pages, and returns their copy, headings and entities for Claude to use in the writing. Reddit may block direct scraping. In that case, search snippets and debate articles on the same topic can supply the pain points. [L0883.012]
- When generating page copy with a web research tool, have the model list the entities it extracted from the top 10 Google US results before it writes. Check that list first, then accept or remove topics, so the draft is built on terms you verified. [L0883.032]
- Always make the research step part of the prompt. Without an explicit instruction to research the top 10 results, the AI often skips the crawl and writes a generic article that blends all sources. [L0883.008]

**Pattern analysis**

- Build a pattern analysis table from the top 10 results before writing. Record the median word count and the target range (median plus or minus 100 to 200 words) below the table. [L0883.017]

| Rank | URL | Word count | H2 count | H3 count | Bullet points | Table | FAQ | Conclusion | Main entities |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [URL] | [number] | [number] | [number] | yes/no | yes/no | yes/no | yes/no | [list] |
|... |... |... |... |... |... |... |... |... |... |
| 10 | [URL] | [number] | [number] | [number] | yes/no | yes/no | yes/no | yes/no | [list] |

[L0883.017]

- Match the heading structure of the top 10: count H2s and H3s and note whether competitors use bullet points, tables, FAQs or a conclusion. If all competitors use bullets and you use H2 or H3 headings or a table where they do not, the page can lose ground. [L0883.009]
- Copy the structure of top results, not their text. Aim for the average of the top 10 on length and format, then add a small amount of unique material to pull ahead. [L0883.010]
- Entities are the related terms and topics that top-ranking pages for a keyword cover, such as features, use cases, activities and measurements. For men's running shorts they included liners, anti-chafing, sprinting, long distance, marathons and mileage. Work these into the copy naturally. [L0883.014]

**Pain points and originality**

- Forums and Q&A threads (Reddit, Quora, Facebook groups) are a source of real customer concerns and pain points only, never a format to imitate. Use the pain points as substance without mirroring the forum's style. [L0883.011]
- Put your own angle and research into the content. Rewriting a competitor's reviews with a few words changed counts as plagiarism and is not an acceptable way to compete. (older, 2020) [L0961.076]
- Blog and product content must be written in the store's own words and based on its own ideas, even when the topic overlaps with competitors. Guest posts from other writers are acceptable. A comparison article such as a top 10 list should use the store's own selection and copy. (older, 2019) [L1069.094]

**Worked case: lined vs unlined running shorts** [L0883.018]

- Keyword chosen as an informational topic, because a product page would not rank for it.
- Firecrawl pulled the top 10 Google US results. The top informational results ran about 1,200 to 1,800 words: one at about 1,800 words (9 min read), one at about 1,600 words (7 min read), and one at about 1,000 words.
- Median target set at about 1,500 words.
- Reddit blocked direct scraping, so pain points came from search snippets and a long forum-style debate article.
- Shared heading skeleton: short intro, what lined and unlined shorts are, a comparison table, when to choose each by activity, distance and climate, care and durability (competitors cited a liner lifespan of 6 to 12 months versus 2 to 3 years), an FAQ, and a conclusion with a soft call to action.
- Pain points used as substance:
  - Short runs favor lined shorts, while ultras of 50K and more favor unlined shorts with your own underwear.
  - Liners exist mainly for support and anti-chafing.
  - A brief liner edge can chafe.
  - A seam in the wrong place can ruin a run for days.
  - Changing in and out of lined shorts in public is a hassle.
  - Some runners prefer their own boxer briefs to an unknown liner.
  - Liners wick moisture and resist odor better than ordinary underwear.
  - In winter, runners wear tights under unlined shorts.
  - Lined shorts need washing every time and dry more slowly.
- One extra angle beyond the top 10 was added.
- The run took about two minutes and produced an article ready for editing.

### Length

**Length by source**

| Source | Figure | Applies when | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source A (undated) | Standard blog article 500 words; about 1,000 words for high-search-volume keywords | Standard keywords: 500. High volume: about 1,000 | [L0380.013] |
| Source A (undated) | Blog posts at least 1000 words wherever possible | Style guide baseline | [L1467.006] |
| Source A (undated) | Articles of 1,000 to 2,000 words rank best. Shorter articles tend to rank poorly. | The right length depends on the topic. A short article is acceptable when the subject can be fully covered in fewer words. | [L0382.013] |
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | At least 1200 words; no upper limit | Minimum for blog posts | [L0311.014] |
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | 1800 words or longer performs best in search | Best-performing content | [L0311.015] |
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | At least 1500 words | Minimum for the checklist | [L0462.001] |
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | 300 or 500 word posts are almost always a waste of time; written content should generally exceed 1,500 words | Search results increasingly favor deep content | [L1461.003, L1461.004] |
| Source E 2025-26 | Median of the top 10 results plus or minus 100 to 200 words | Preferred method for each keyword | [L0883.006] |

**Working rules**

- Calculate the median word count of the top 10 ranking pages and aim for the median plus or minus about 100 to 200 words. Example: if the median is 1,700 words, write roughly 1,500 to 1,900 words. [L0883.006]
- Overshooting the median is a mistake. A 4,000-word article when the median is 1,700 merges every competitor's points into one long piece that does not match what ranks, so it will not rank. Undershooting is equally wasteful, so keep the length within the median range. [L0883.007]
- Blog posts are an excellent place to show depth, but the sources differ on the minimum. Apply the median method first. Use the Source B floors (1200 or 1500 words) and the Source A 1,000-word figure as checks, not as separate targets. [L0883.006, L0311.014, L0462.001, L1467.006]
- Top results average about 1,443 words on the page for the number one position. This is an average and a trend, not a fixed target. (older, 2021-22) [L1461.005, L1461.023]
- Pages ranking in positions 1 to 4 on Google had high word counts, and average length fell steadily for lower positions down to page ten. (older, 2021-22) [L1461.023]
- Length does not hurt a post, since readers scroll. What matters is that the structure is easy to scan. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.029]
- Long posts (for example around 5,000 words) are fine if they make the content the most complete on the topic. (older, 2021-22) [L1461.004]
- Avoid thin content and duplicate content. With 1200 or more words of original content, these are not typically concerns, and longer posts tend to be the most comprehensive resource for that search term. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.016]
- Social shares rose with article length. Articles of 3,000 to 10,000 words earned far more shares than those under 1,000 words, although short articles still received some shares. (older, 2021-22) [L1461.022]
- In an Ahrefs and Backlinko analysis, a higher content grade from ClearScope, an online content-quality scoring tool, correlated with higher Google rankings on both desktop and mobile. Check content against a grading tool to see whether its quality is likely to rank. (older, 2021-22) [L1461.025]
- Ranking signals depend primarily on content quality, depth and inbound links, but on-page optimization is still necessary to maximize results. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.001]
- Also "a blog post is any page with more than 500 words of content." (older, 2021-22) [L0311.001]

**Length follows the purchase**

- For considered, expensive purchases, a long and detailed explanation sells better than short copy, because buyers want the facts before deciding. Readers who take the time to read long copy are already interested. [L1261.131]
- Short copy works only for simple, low-cost purchases. For complex products, length should cover every important claim. The rule is to make the story reasonably complete, whatever its length. [L1261.132]

### Outline and Structure

**Prompts for the outline**

Prompt for the outline [L0178.005]:

```
Develop a structured outline organizing the main topics and sections for a blog post about [Blog Post Name].
```

Prompt for a standing desk outline [L1321.010]:

```
Prepare an outline for a blog post on tailoring your standing desk. Provide a proposed headline, a short introduction, and the primary sections in their proper sequence. For each section, supply bullet points that a writer can develop using their own phrasing. Leave out the complete article text.
```

Prompt for subsection strategies [L1321.011]:

```
Please list strategies for [subtopic from the outline]. Give short bullet points that can be used as subsection headings in the blog post.
```

**Outline procedure with ChatGPT** [L1321.009]

1. Put the exact target keyword into the outline request, for example *customize your standing desk*.
2. Send the prompt and review the proposed title, introduction and main sections.
3. Check that each section comes with bullet points that can be expanded into paragraphs.
4. For a bullet that needs more depth, send a follow-up request for a list of ideas on that subtopic. One bullet can yield around 14 ideas.
5. Use the ideas as input only and write the final text yourself, in the Shopify blog editor or in a document editor.

**Research and outline sequence** [L0972.077]

1. Ask ChatGPT for an outline of what the post should include.
2. For each subsection, search Google for other content covering the same topic.
3. Collect statistics and data from those sources, and cite the source where it fits.
4. Write your own version of each subsection in your own words, rather than copying any single article.

**Topical authority sequence** [L0971.096]

1. Use AI to build a topical authority map for the niche.
2. Use AI to brainstorm topic ideas from that map.
3. Use AI to create an outline for each piece of content.
4. Have a human write the actual article based on the outline.
5. Customize the text before publishing it.

**Structure rules**

- After the headline, add a short overview of what the reader will learn. This keeps attention on a long post before the detailed sections begin. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.027]
- Split the body at logical points: in a how-to, each step becomes a sub-headline, and in a list, each item becomes one. Many readers scan a post for the section that matters to them. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.008]
- The best structure is the one the market rewards. Use the checklist and the top-10 pattern analysis to decide the skeleton. [L0462.025, L0883.017]

**Example: how-to guide page** [L1099.022]

A how-to guide page that works well shows a clear headline, a date and a short intro of two paragraphs that states what the reader will learn, all above the fold on desktop and mobile. The hero photo is kept small or omitted, because a photo of generic subject matter tells the reader nothing about the content. The body text sits in a narrow reading column even on a wide screen. The steps move from a numbered list to bullets and then to subheadings for each section. [L1099.022]

### Headings

**Heading levels**

- Use exactly one H1 per page, and make it the headline of the blog post. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.002]
- Use H1 only once per piece of content, for the title. Use H2 tags for secondary headings that separate sections, as many as needed. [L1467.008]
- H1 tags contain the main heading of a page. H2 tags organize subcategories or subtopics within H1. H3 tags further subdivide topics within H2 sections. [L0278.009]
- Use H2 tags for all headings after the post title, not H1. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.009]
- Use H2 tags for the main sub-headlines of the body and H3 tags for sub-headlines nested under an H2. Never leave the body as one large block of text. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.007]
- Use H3 tags only as subheadings inside a section marked by an H2. H4, H5 and lower heading levels should not be needed. [L1467.009]

**Single H2 group (conflict with many H2s)**

- Use a single H2 subheading group per page to break content into chunks, since subheadings help SEO. Only at least one H2 needs the focus keyword. [L0881.017]
- Many H2 subheadings are also recommended to chunk long text. Read L0881.017 as one H2 set for the body, and use many H2s for chunking as in the rule below. (older, 2021-22) [L1099.008]

**Shopify heading setup** (older, 2022) [L0429.032]

- Shopify product pages automatically wrap the product name in H1, so no changes are needed for product pages.
- For content pages, select the main title in the WYSIWYG editor and choose H1 from the text dropdown (options: body text, H1, H2, H3, H4). Then use H2 to H4 for subheadings.

**Heading content**

- Put keyword variations, synonyms and related search terms from keyword research into sub-headlines. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.009]
- Use synonyms and related keywords in other H2 headings to avoid repetition of the exact focus keyword in every heading. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.010]
- Format some H2 headings as questions to capture question-and-answer featured snippets. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.009]
- Do not place the primary keyword in every sub-headline. Stuffing it into every sub-headline looks unnatural and is seen as overuse. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.009, L0462.031]
- Include the focus keyword in at least one H2 heading. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.009]
- AI output can add headings you did not ask for, such as a fourth H3 under a three-H3 structure. Check each output's heading count against your template and remove or merge extra headings before publishing. [L0883.022]

### Title, Headline and URL

**Title rules**

- Use the post title as an H1 heading and place the focus keyword at the front of the title. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.003]
- The H1 should state clearly what the content covers or what the reader will learn, and include the primary keyword if possible. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.002]
- Avoid hype, power words, over-the-top claims and click-bait headlines. Platforms such as Google and Facebook have anti-click-bait policies, and a headline that promises more than the article delivers works against the page. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.003]
- Include the publication year or an update reference in the title (for example *2020 update* or *updated for 2020*), because Google prioritizes fresh content and this signals recency. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.004]
- Append the word updated to the title only after the post has been genuinely revised, and do this at least once a year. (older, 2021-22) [L0658.017]

**Title conflict:** L0311.004 asks for a year or update reference in every title. L0658.017 allows the word updated only after a real revision. Use the year on new posts. Add updated only after a genuine revision.

**Guide headline formula** [L0462.004]

- Formula: [primary keyword] + [content type such as Guide, Checklist or How-to] + optional [reader qualifier such as who it is for], with no filler words.
- Examples: *Complete Guide to Standing Desks* and *Standing Desk Checklist for Small Offices*.
- It works because the reader sees the topic, the format and the target audience at once and can judge fit immediately. (older, 2021-22)

**URL**

- Create the post URL to include the focus keyword and keep it short. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.020]
- Set the URL handle to the target keyword in lowercase with hyphens. [L1321.029]
- Check the URL and handle, title and description fields before saving. [L1321.014]

### Keyword Placement

- The focus keyword must appear within the first 100 words of body text. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.011]
- Stuffing the primary keyword into every sub-headline looks unnatural and is seen as overuse. Use related terms and variations instead. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.031]
- Work the entities from the research step into the copy where they fit, so the page covers the terms top results cover. [L0883.014, L0883.016]

### Paragraphs, Font and Readability

**Paragraph length (sources differ)**

| Source | Rule | Applies when | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | Use short sentences and short paragraphs | General readability | [L0311.013] |
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | Maximum of four lines, shorter where possible | Checklist limit | [L0462.016] |
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | Short paragraphs of two to three lines | Content areas and readability | [L1099.008] |
| Source A (undated) | About 3 lines, never more than 4 | Style guide | [L1467.007] |

- Use a short sentence and paragraph structure throughout, with paragraphs of about 3 lines and never more than 4. [L0311.013, L1099.008, L1467.007]
- Avoid dense blocks of text. Dense blocks make reading and comprehension harder. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.016, L0462.030]

**Font size**

- Set primary body text to a font size of 18 point. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.012]
- Use a minimum font size of 18 pt for body text, because smaller text becomes hard to read, especially on mobile devices. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.017]
- Set body text to font size 18 so the page has more white space and is easier to read. (older, 2021-22) [L1099.008]
- Small text makes readers give up. The same problem appears on mobile when the font is smaller than 18 pt. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.030]

**White space and column width**

- White space around text helps the eye process a page. Generous line spacing is part of the same readability rule. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.018]
- Narrow long text blocks with a CSS max-width so paragraphs do not span a full wide desktop screen. Narrower columns are easier to read because the eyes travel less. (older, 2021-22) [L1099.010]

### Lists

- Use bulleted lists and numbered lists in the post content. Google favors these formats and they improve user engagement. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.017]
- Use bulleted or numbered lists wherever you make a series of short points. Google often pulls numbered lists and bullet points into featured snippets on the results page. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.019]
- Use plenty of bullet points, numbered lists, images and videos in content areas. Embedded videos in content keep visitors on the page longer and help dwell time. (older, 2021-22) [L1099.009]

### Images

**Image frequency (sources differ)**

| Source | Rule | Applies when | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | Insert images or video approximately every 300 words. Do not let difficulty sourcing images prevent publication. | Default for long posts | [L0311.018] |
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | At least one image every 500 words (five in a 1,500-word post). Many publishers prefer one every 300 words. No maximum. | Minimum floor | [L0462.005] |
| Source B (older, 2021-22) | An image every 100 words is acceptable. A listicle can place one image per list item followed by a short text block. | Listicles and image-heavy content | [L0462.006] |
| Source A (undated) | Blog posts start with a main image where possible, followed by at least one image in every section marked by an H2 | Style guide baseline | [L1467.010] |

- Images break up long text, keep readers on the page, and help visual learners understand the content faster. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.006]

**Image production and style**

- Blog images should share one size and style so the blog looks consistent. Keep a fixed image set in Canva and add new photos from it each time a blog post is written. [L0342.028]
- Blog header formula: a short headline that names the reader's decision or problem, a one-line benefit subline in smaller text, and a background photo that matches the topic. Examples: *Pick Your Perfect Tent* with *Find the right size for your group and season* over a campsite photo, and *Choose the Right Running Shoe* with *Get support that fits your stride* over a trail photo. [L0342.029]
- Use unique images whenever possible. Infographics created by freelancers add significant value to blog posts. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.019]
- Images that you personally edit, retouch or modify count as unique assets, even if based on stock photos or other sources. Self-edited images are as valuable as original photography for visual variety. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.030]
- Use a royalty-free image or an AI-generated image for the header. [L1321.014]
- On content pages, a large hero image is less important than a clear headline and introductory copy. (older, 2021-22) [L1099.004]

**Image technical rules**

- Compress all images to minimize file size. All images require unique file names and alt text. Place the focus keyword in the file name and alt tag of the first image. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.008]
- Compress existing images in Smart SEO: open Image optimization, click Configure, and optimize the images in the Files and Articles sections. [L1321.029]

### Links

**Internal links**

- Blog posts are excellent opportunities for internal linking because of their length. Include internal links to collection pages, product pages and other content pages wherever relevant. This helps Google crawl and understand the site structure and is beneficial for users. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.025]
- Aim for approximately one internal link per 300-word section. For each 300-word segment, include at least one relevant internal link to a product page, collection page or other content page. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.028]
- Add internal links wherever they are relevant, with no fixed number. Link a product type to its collection page, a specific product to its product page, and a topic covered in another post to that post. Cornerstone content follows different linking rules than standard posts. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.013]
- Use the one-per-300-words rule as a working guide and the no-fixed-number rule as the principle. Cornerstone content is excepted. [L0311.028, L0462.013, L0462.023]

**Outbound links (sources differ)**

- For a 1200-word blog post, up to 3 outbound links is appropriate. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.026]
- For a 1500-word post, include at least three external links to high-quality sources that extend the topic, including resources you drew inspiration from. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.010]
- The two rules agree at three links, but L0311.026 sets a ceiling and L0462.010 sets a floor. Use three as the working number for a 1,200 to 1,500-word post.
- Always check the domain authority of the target site. Linking to higher-authority sites is acceptable. Common uses for outbound links include crediting image sources, citing statistics and referencing videos. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.026]
- Link out to other reputable sources in articles. It looks natural and shows search engines that the content is part of the wider web. An article without any external links can look suspicious. [L0382.028]
- External links give little direct link value, but linking to credible sources is a minor ranking factor that Google rewards. Sending readers to another site adds value for them. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.011]

**Link behavior**

- Configure external links to open in a new window or tab. This keeps the reader's place on your site and their time on site. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.029, L0462.012]

### Above the Fold and Layout

- Place the most important information above the fold, especially on mobile. On product pages this means the product image and product title. On blog posts it means the headline, possibly an image, and roughly the first paragraph of text. (older, 2021-22) [L1099.003]
- On mobile screens, ensure the post title, featured image and opening paragraphs appear above the fold. Avoid large images at the top that push text down. Consider placing explanatory text first, followed by visuals, so readers immediately understand the topic. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.006]
- Oversized images at the top that push text down should be avoided because they do not explain what the reader will learn. (older, 2021-22) [L1099.004]
- Add user interaction points where possible: comment sections on blog posts, Q&A sections on product pages and review widgets, especially with video or image reviews. These add content and social proof that keep visitors on the page longer. (older, 2021-22) [L1099.012]

### Tables of Contents and Long-Post Navigation

**Threshold (sources differ)**

- For posts of about 3,000 words or more, add anchor links at the top so readers can jump to each section. For a 1,500-word post this navigation is not necessary. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.028]
- Long blog posts (for example around 5,000 words) should get a clickable table of contents at the top that links each heading to its position on the page. A 1,500-word post does not need one. (older, 2021-22) [L1099.013]
- Both sources agree that a 1,500-word post needs no table of contents. The threshold is about 3,000 words in L0462.028 and about 5,000 words in L1099.013. Use the table at about 3,000 words or more, and at least from 5,000 words.

**Build method** (older, 2021-22) [L0462.041]

- On a long post of about 3,000 words or more, add a chapter list at the top where each entry links to a heading on the same page.
- Each heading gets an anchor id, and the link takes the form page-URL#heading-id, so a click scrolls straight to that section.
- On a 1,500-word post this navigation is not needed.

### Comments, Interaction and Calls to Action

- Enable an active comment section on the blog post. Encourage comments by posing a question or inviting reader thoughts. Reply to every comment, even briefly, to demonstrate that the business is real and cares about engagement. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.023]
- Allow comments on blog posts, but moderate them to block spam and abusive posts. Visible replies from the business show responsiveness and build trust and social proof. Comments do not harm rankings. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.020]
- Turning off comments on blog posts misses a chance to show responsiveness and build trust. Enable comments with moderation instead of disabling them. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.032]
- End the blog post with a call to action using phrases such as *read more* or *learn more*. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.022]
- Offer readers an opportunity to join the audience, such as an email subscription. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.023]
- Use inline opt-in forms or static opt-in forms to capture leads or distribute lead magnets. Do not use pop-ups or interstitials that load within the first 30 seconds of a page visit, because pop-ups are disliked by both users and Google. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.024]
- A conclusion with a soft call to action fits the standard skeleton for informational articles. [L0883.018]

### Blog Post Checklist

Blog post checklist, in this order [L0462.023]:

1. Write 1500 or more words. [L0462.023, L0462.001]
2. Write an engaging H1 headline that clearly explains the content and includes the primary keyword if possible, without hype. [L0462.023, L0462.002, L0462.003]
3. Add images at a ratio of at least 1 image per 500 words. [L0462.023, L0462.005]
4. Add plenty of sub-headlines, with H2 for main sections and H3 for sub-sections. [L0462.023, L0462.007]
5. Include at least 3 external links. [L0462.023, L0462.010]
6. Add internal links where relevant (cornerstone content is excepted). [L0462.023, L0462.013]
7. Keep paragraphs to no more than 4 lines. [L0462.023, L0462.016]
8. Use a minimum font size of 18. [L0462.023, L0462.017]
9. Use bulleted or numbered lists wherever you make a series of short points. [L0462.023, L0462.019]
10. Allow moderated comments. [L0462.023, L0462.020]
11. Offer readers an opportunity to join the audience, such as an email subscription. [L0462.023]
12. Run through the whole list again before publishing and before promoting the post. It takes only a few minutes. [L0462.023]

- Give the blog post checklist to freelance writers and content producers so they get the structure right from the start. This reduces the guidance you need to give on each piece. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.024]

### Meta Title and Meta Description

- The meta title must be unique and include the focus keyword. The meta description must be unique. (older, 2021-22) [L0311.021]
- Prompt for the meta description [L0178.006]:

```
Compose a meta description that summarizes the [Blog Post Name] blog post for search engine display.
```

- In Smart SEO, generate meta titles and descriptions, compare them with your own, keep the stronger version and save. [L1321.029]

### Keeping Posts Live and Updating Them

- Keep every blog post live rather than rotating them, because rotation hides pages from search results and loses traffic. Display the top three posts on the homepage and list the rest in the blog section. (older, 2020) [L0468.129]
- Publish at a starting pace of one piece per month, later move to one every two weeks, and then focus on improving the pages and posts that already perform well. [L1321.029]

### Publishing in Shopify

**Built-in engine and location**

- Shopify accounts come with a built-in blogging engine. Publishing blog posts with valuable content generates organic traffic from Google and Bing. [L0310.001, L0311.001]
- Create a blog post in Shopify: Online store > Blog posts > Add blog post. For a page, use Online store > Pages and the same process applies. [L1321.013]

**Publishing procedure** [L1321.014]

1. Go to Online store > Blog posts > Add blog post (or Online store > Pages for a page).
2. Enter the title. An AI-suggested title can be pasted here if it fits.
3. Write the body in the editor based on the outline, either directly in the Shopify editor or in a document editor such as Microsoft Word or Google Docs, then paste the finished version into the post.
4. Add an image using a royalty-free image or an AI-generated image.
5. Scroll to the search engine listing preview section at the bottom of the editor.
6. Check the URL and handle, title and description fields (see the URL and handle steps).
7. Save the post.

**Test content**

- Do not publish test or unedited content to a live store. Leave such posts unpublished, where they stay hidden from visitors. [L1321.015]

**Full on-page SEO sequence for a Shopify page or blog post** [L1321.029]

1. Compress existing images in Smart SEO: open Image optimization, click Configure, and optimize the images in the Files and Articles sections.
2. Decide whether the content is a page (evergreen tool or calculator) or a blog post (evergreen guide or how-to).
3. Take the topic and target keyword from your keyword research worksheet and from popular threads on community sites in your niche.
4. Ask a free AI chat tool for an outline with bullet points, expand the bullets with ideas as needed, and write the final text yourself in the Shopify editor or a document editor.
5. Add a royalty-free or AI-generated image.
6. Save the post, then set the URL handle to the target keyword in lowercase with hyphens, set the page title, and delete the description Shopify pulls automatically.
7. Generate a meta description with an AI tool, check it against the actual content, paste it into the search engine listing, and save.
8. In Smart SEO, confirm that JSON-LD is enabled and valid for blogs and articles.
9. Generate meta titles and descriptions in Smart SEO, compare them with your own, keep the stronger version, and save.
10. Publish at a starting pace of one piece per month, later move to one every two weeks, and then focus on improving the pages and posts that already perform well.

**Quality first**

- Quality content is the base of every page or blog post. Once the content is good, technical steps such as optimized images, a keyword URL, a clear title, a meta description and JSON-LD give an extra edge over competitors. [L1321.025]

### Style Guide

- The website style guide applies to every piece of content produced or edited for the site, including product descriptions, policy pages, images and blog posts. Anyone working on site content must follow it, not only the designers. [L1467.005]
- Write AI copy in active voice, without em dashes and without filler. Keep a blacklist of words typical of AI writing, remove them from every output, and apply a written style guide where one exists. [L0883.019]
- Blog posts should be at least 1000 words wherever possible. [L1467.006]
- Paragraphs should be about 3 lines long and never more than 4 lines, to improve readability. [L1467.007]
- Use the H1 tag only once per piece of content, for the title. Use H2 tags for secondary headings that separate sections, as many as needed. [L1467.008]
- Use H3 tags only as subheadings inside a section marked by an H2. H4, H5 and lower heading levels should not be needed. [L1467.009]
- Blog posts should start with a main image where possible, followed by at least one image in every section marked by an H2 tag. [L1467.010]

### Using AI and Its Limits

**Role of AI**

- Use AI for content outlines and assistance, not copy-paste. Take AI-generated text, refine it with human writing to make it unique. Bulk AI-generated articles no longer rank. Quality weekly content beats 1,000 AI articles. (2023) [L0274.037]
- Do not publish AI-generated text unedited. Search engines may recognize fully AI-written content and rank it lower, so a human should review, rewrite and enrich AI output before publishing. The same editing applies to AI-written product and collection descriptions. [L1321.012]
- AI-generated content carries long-term uncertainty, since search engines may change how they handle it. Using AI drafts as a starting point and rewriting them by hand keeps the content more reliable. (2023) [L0970.128]
- AI tools can create a first draft of a buyer's guide, but all numbers, data and statistics must be double-checked because AI can be wrong. (2023) [L0855.153]
- AI copy is only as accurate as the brand data you give it. Supply the full product catalog with sizes, materials and claims. Without it, the model invents specifications that do not exist. Use placeholders for any fact you have not supplied. [L0883.020]

**Prompt for a full research-based article** [L0883.016]

```
You are an ecommerce SEO content writer. Write a blog article for the target keyword [TARGET KEYWORD], pattern-matched to the search results.

Step 1: Search Google US for the target keyword with the Firecrawl MCP and analyze the top 10 ranking results. For each result, record word count, H2 count, H3 count, use of bullet points, tables, FAQ section and conclusion, and the main entities covered.

Step 2: Calculate the median word count of the 10 results. Set the target length to the median plus or minus 100 to 200 words, and state the target length before writing.

Step 3: Match the common heading skeleton and format elements of the top results, but do not copy any text and do not imitate forum formatting.

Step 4: From forum and Q&A results (Reddit, Quora, Facebook groups, debate articles), collect real customer concerns and pain points. Use them as substance only.

Step 5: Write the article in active voice, without em dashes and without unnecessary fluff, avoiding these words: [BLACKLIST OF AI-TYPICAL WORDS]. Work in the entities from Step 1 where they fit. Include [NUMBER] internal links to these URLs: [LIST OF COLLECTION AND BLOG URLS]. Add one unique angle that none of the top 10 results covers, and label it.

Facts: use only facts from [PASTE BRAND AND PRODUCT FACTS]. Write [PLACEHOLDER: missing fact] where a fact is missing.

Output: first the pattern analysis table from Step 1 with the median and target length, then the article, then a list of the pain points used and the unique angle added.
```

**Prompt for competitor style matching** [L0972.078]

```
I'm attaching a blog post I recently wrote for my store at [store URL]. I looked at my competitors, including [competitor link 1], [competitor link 2] and [competitor link 3]. This is a rough draft, and I like their writing style better. Please modify and improve the attached draft so it matches the style of those competitors, while keeping all of my facts, product details and the structure of the post.
```

- Use the competitor style prompt only with your own facts. Check the output against the research and the style guide before publishing. [L0972.078, L0883.019]

## Content Strategy, Formats and Planning

### Role of Content in the Funnel

- Without content, a store depends on paid channels such as Google ads, paid social and affiliate marketing for traffic. Content adds organic traffic that does not depend on ad spend alone. [L0500.012]
- Search engine optimisation cannot be done effectively without content, because product pages give search engines too little material to rank for informational searches. [L0500.007]
- Content has four jobs: answering customer questions and solving problems, gaining attention and engagement, generating traffic from search engines and YouTube, and building backlinks to the site. Plan each piece for at least one of these jobs. [L0386.002]
- Product pages are commercial, while content is educational and entertaining. Product pages fit the product-aware and ninja-aware stages. Buyers at the problem-aware and solution-aware stages need educational content, such as a guide that explains what to look for and recommends specific products. [L1117.014]
- A store made only of collection pages and product pages can answer questions only at the product-aware and ninja-aware stages. Content lets a store serve the whole awareness pyramid more easily than any other marketing mechanism. [L1117.013]
- Competitors who reach people in the early awareness stages first gain attention, engagement and trust, and lock those buyers into their own sales funnel. A brand that appears only at the product-aware stage must win attention away from sources the customer already trusts. [L1117.012]
- To be present everywhere in the buyer's journey, a brand must engage customers with both content and products. Product pages belong where the buyer already knows what they want, but they should not be the entire journey. [L1117.016]
- Educational content builds know, like and trust and helps buyers decide intelligently. Show both the positives and the negatives of a product, because not every visitor is a potential customer. [L0382.004]
- Pain-point content can rank in search engines and bring traffic, and it guides people through their customer journey. This supports conversion and authority, so it is a strong starting point. [L0386.004]
- A buyer's guide raises the authority of a store, educates customers and links to products that fit what the reader wants. It can be offered as a downloadable PDF with a clear format and relevant product links. [L0837.063]
- Buyer's guides, content on the store, and content co-created with suppliers help organic traffic and build the store's content base. (older, 2021) [L0801.088]
- Publish unique long-form content as video on YouTube or as articles on a blog, and use social platforms to link people there. These platforms are consumption-focused, so viewers who stay are highly qualified. [L0487.059]
- Written blog content is a worthwhile format on its own, because readers like to read and search engines favour written content. It can also be combined with video. (older, 2021-22) [L0462.026]
- Content is one part of a connected marketing system, not a standalone task. The same pieces feed email flows, SEO, link building and retargeting. Automated email sequences can use site content in their first emails. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.002]
- Content pieces published on the store's own website are later reused for link building and SEO. Plan each piece with a place on the site, not only for social channels. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.018]
- Produce content from a researched, documented plan made in advance, not from topics picked at random. Random content usually performs poorly, and the failure is then wrongly blamed on content marketing. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.001]
- Content delivers its value when it is produced and maintained on an ongoing basis. Treating it as a one-off project limits the results it can bring. [L0500.013]
- Most e-commerce stores have no regular content production system when they start. A store without one should build it from the beginning. A store already publishing content should first check whether the content fits the market, then promote it. [L0500.011]

### When to Start Content

- Start blog content marketing once the store has traffic, so you know which topics attract visitors. Content made for the blog can also be reused on social media. [L0835.096]
- Buyer's guides and content are most useful after the store has traffic, because the data then shows which products visitors actually want. [L0855.154]
- Start a done-for-you content package only after the store is complete and has traffic data, so you know which products visitors view. Spending on content before there is data is wasted. [L0804.062]
- Done-for-you content packages add long-term value through unique content and organic traffic, but they do not produce early sales. If a store still has open site bugs or unprofitable ads, fix the site and make the paid traffic profitable first. (older, 2021) [L0801.184]
- On a new affiliate site, publish three to five blog posts before buying traffic. The posts show visitors that you know the topic and give you something to link to when you share the site elsewhere. [L0514.008]
- Sending traffic to a new affiliate site with no posts leaves you nothing to link to when you use free methods such as forum replies or YouTube comments. Publish a few posts before you send people there. [L0514.039]
- If there are no competitor blogs in your exact niche, use the closest related content, such as blog posts from related products or from the buyer's own interests, as a starting reference for ideas. [L0804.063]
- Do not spend time deciding the perfect order of content ideas. Pick any idea and start producing, because the cost of waiting is higher than the cost of a suboptimal first choice. Research can start immediately and the plan can be adjusted later. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.008]
- Trying to fully prioritise content ideas before starting wastes time that could be spent producing content. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.012]

### Content Types and Formats

#### Choosing the format

- Use one of three content formats: written only, video only, or a combination of both. Pick the format before planning or producing anything. (older, 2021-22) [L0383.005]
- Suitable content formats for keyword topics include buyer's guides, how-to articles, entertainment or list posts, industry announcements and new product type alerts. Choose the format to match the search intent of the keyword. [L1120.002]
- A search term does not depend on the format of the page that targets it. The same keyword can be targeted with a video, a blog post or another written format, so the format choice does not change the keyword assignment. (older, 2021-22) [L0877.034]
- Mix the content themes in your plan. Problem-solving pieces include how-tos, listicles, ultimate guides and product comparisons. Entertaining pieces include best-of lists, breaking news and controversial takes. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.006]
- Mixing text, images and video suits readers who prefer different formats. Some want the reasons in text, some want photos of real customers with the products, and some want a video that repeats the text. (older, 2019) [L0467.016]
- Every blog post should include three elements: text, images and a video, with the images and video placed within the text. Videos should come from the suppliers or the store owner. (older, 2019) [L0467.017]
- Extensive content libraries such as comparisons, how-tos and buying guides are a strong SEO strategy and can drive a large part of a competitor's traffic. Note the content types and count them. (older, 2021-22) [L0893.032]

#### Type reference

Recommended length and purpose by content type:

| Content Type | Recommended Length | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| Product release (new item announcement with photos) | ~500 words | Announce inventory and drive immediate interest |
| How-to guides, repairs, tutorials, tips, user guides | 1,000-2,000 words | Build authority and rank for informational keywords |
| Competitor comparisons | 1,000+ words | Capture 'best of' searches |
| Product reviews | 1,000+ words | Rank for specific product queries |
| News and announcements | 500+ words | Show fresh inventory and promotions |
| Testimonials/customer stories | 500-1,000 words | Build trust and provide social proof |

[L0226.011]

- A listicle can work as top-of-funnel content for a shop that sells related products. A surf shop publishing a top 10 list of local surf spots attracts searchers who are not ready to buy. Product pages remain the commercial targets, and the listicle serves to attract the audience. (older, 2021-22) [L0662.018]
- Best-of pages and listicles place your own product first, so they only work if the product is genuinely the best option in its category. Build the product quality first, then the pages. (older, 2021-22) [L0768.010]
- Brand versus brand comparison pages can either compare your lesser-known brand with a well-known brand, or compare two well-known brands and recommend yours at the bottom of the page. (older, 2021-22) [L0768.011]
- News-jacking content from a source article, such as a medical article:
  1. Find the article.
  2. Quote the relevant parts.
  3. Link to the original source and mention it.
  4. Add your business commentary.
  Do not use content behind a paywall. [L0804.061]

### Length and Depth

- Use the type-based lengths in the table above as the starting point for each piece. [L0226.011]
- Blog post length is 300 to 1,000 words depending on the topic. Do not inflate with filler; write what is necessary to explain the topic fully. Blog posting every other week is effective. (older, 2022) [L0272.039]
- Aim for quality over quantity in each article, because that is what currently ranks. Google increasingly favours authoritative, long pages that cover several related keywords rather than thin pages for one keyword. [L0382.014]
- To rank for a competitive search term, a new piece should cover its subject in more depth than the pages already on page one. Google tends to favour longer-form content, so a thin article is unlikely to outrank existing pages. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.022]
- When judging a competitor's content, check its volume and quality separately. Long, in-depth pages with images and links to products suggest higher quality. Short pages that answer simple questions suggest lower quality. Quality gaps reveal opportunities. (older, 2021-22) [L0893.031]

### Idea Sourcing

#### Customers and their questions

- To find content ideas, work through four questions in this order:
  1. Which questions, problems and frustrations do my buyers run into that connect to what I sell?
  2. Which content subjects related to my products are already being shared on social platforms and through email?
  3. Which content already ranks in search engines for the keywords I am targeting?
  4. Which product-related content topics are other websites already linking to?
  (older, 2021-22) [L0386.003]
- Customer service records and customer surveys show real pain points, the questions customers ask before buying, and what they struggle with or wish they knew more about. Ask existing customers directly and treat their answers as sources for early content topics. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.013]
- Choose which content to create based on keyword research, using the questions real customers ask in the niche. [L1227.024]
- Keyword-based content should match the questions customers ask, so the question itself can become the post's target keyword. [L1227.024]

#### Communities and social signals

- Content research starts with what already performs in your market. Check what popular creators publish and which topics their audiences watch. Use this to model and adapt topics rather than copying content. (older, 2021-22) [L0478.017]
