- Content gaps are topics that large audiences in your market do not cover well. Finding them gives you content opportunities where you can serve buyers with less competition. (older, 2021-22) [L0478.020]
- Shareable content can be found by looking for existing topic pieces with high social engagement or shares. Create something similar but more in-depth, so it has a good chance of getting the same engagement. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.005]
- Shareable topics can be found with BuzzSumo, which shows which topics in your niche are trending by displaying Facebook engagement counts, Twitter mentions, share frequency and related articles. Use this research to align content strategy with audience interest. [L0286.028]
- Topics with many upvotes, likes or comments show what the niche audience engages with and make good content candidates. Reddit threads with question lists can also provide related keywords for a blog post. [L1120.032]
- Content idea research on Reddit:
  1. Search your niche keyword plus Reddit on Google.
  2. Open the niche subreddit (for example the standing desks community with about 27,000 members).
  3. Look at the recent posts sorted by the number of upvotes, and note topics that could become a blog post.
  4. Click the top sort option and change the time filter to all time to see the most upvoted posts that can serve as evergreen topics.
  [L1120.029]
- Example: a Reddit question about whether paint on the legs of a standing desk wears off led to a blog topic on customizing a standing desk, including which paint lasts. The question idea was added to the worksheet as a blog post keyword. [L1120.030]
- Content idea research on Facebook groups:
  1. Go to Facebook and type your niche into the search bar.
  2. Click Groups.
  3. Join relevant groups and look for posts with many likes and comments.
  4. Add those topics and keywords to the worksheet.
  [L1120.031]
- Example: question-style titles from competitor blogs can be used directly as blog topics in a niche such as standing desks. Examples are *how long should you stand at a standing desk* and *tips for buying an adjustable standing desk converter*. Each one should be added to the keyword worksheet as a blog post keyword. [L1120.041]
- Log content ideas from communities:
  1. Join Facebook groups in your niche and note the questions people ask.
  2. Join relevant Reddit communities and collect the discussion topics that come up repeatedly.
  3. Search Google for niche topics to see what people are asking about.
  4. Log each content idea in a Google Sheet, with one column for the idea and one column for the link to the source it came from.
  [L0472.075]
- Example of an AI-assisted idea chain, applied to a food brand:
  1. Every Monday, scrape a relevant Reddit forum for the niche, for example r/cooking for a food brand.
  2. Use Chat2T to summarise all new posts and extract key insights.
  3. Send those insights to Claude to generate new marketing angles the brand has not yet tested.
  4. Send each angle back to Claude to write a complete UGC video script.
  5. Add all generated scripts to ClickUp for the production team.
  This chain turns raw community conversations into ready-to-produce creative briefs automatically. [L0287.020]

#### Brainstorming the first ideas

- Plan content topics six months ahead by collecting 12 content ideas as the starting point. Include any ideas already in your head, and brainstorm the rest across different content types for your market or niche. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.005]

### Keyword and SERP Research

#### Keyword discovery

- Keyword and traffic tools such as Ubersuggest, Ahrefs, Semrush and Moz show, for a search term, what content already exists, its social share counts, its organic traffic and its backlink counts. Use them to judge what already exists for a topic. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.011]
- Keyword research for the content plan can be done yourself or outsourced. If it has not been done yet, do it before entering the first content ideas. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.001]
- Keyword pass output: the minimum output of a first keyword pass is a list of terms to target with content. (older, 2021-22) [L0877.034]
- Build a keyword list from informational queries before writing. [L0380.014]
- Style-based keyword research for homewares, using a style name rather than a product term:
  1. Search for style-based keywords related to your products, such as an interior style name, and note monthly volume and keyword difficulty.
  2. Choose styles with usable volume and low difficulty, and estimate the number of backlinks needed to rank.
  3. Write a style guide that explains the defining characteristics of the look and how to recreate it at home.
  4. Create product-type guides for the style, such as beds, dining tables or wardrobes.
  5. Link from the guides to lists of your top products for a themed room, so the content leads down to products.
  [L0719.049]
- Example of a style-based entry point: a keyword such as *Hamptons furniture* had 2,000 to 3,000 monthly searches, keyword difficulty of 20, and an estimated need for about 20 backlinks to rank. [L0719.039]
- Content aimed at style searches sits higher in the funnel and suits homewares, because buyers start with a look or style rather than a specific product. The content can then lead down to product lists. [L0719.040]
- Keyword research workflow step 1 uses the Ahrefs Content Gap method to choose keywords, and RAW is used to vet them. [L0382.039]
- Evergreen keywords are optimised once on existing pages such as collections, product pages or permanent pages. Trending keywords can justify one or two new blog posts per month. Plan the two groups separately. [L1120.023]
- Holiday and promotion keywords are covered in the holiday section below. [L1222.011]

#### Checking what ranks

- To check what already ranks for a target keyword:
  1. Search the target keyword in Google.
  2. Note the URLs of the content pages in the top five organic positions on page one.
  3. Record those URLs in the content research document.
  4. Have the person producing the content review those pages before writing and identify where they can go further with a better piece.
  (older, 2021-22) [L0386.006]
- If no content pages rank on page one for a keyword, do not drop the keyword. It means ranking should be easier with reasonable effort. If three or four content pages already rank, they are the competition to beat. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.007]
- Finding no similar online content is not a bad sign and does not mean the content will fail. It only means the topic may be new ground with less existing competition. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.020]
- Research does not have to be finished for every planned idea, but some research should always be done in advance so that part of the planned content is already researched. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.016]
- Content research for a niche keyword:
  1. Search Google for your main niche keyword, for example ring light.
  2. Analyse all organic results on the first page and ignore paid ads.
  3. Note the format Google returns for the query, such as top 10 lists, best-of pages or buying guides.
  4. Build your guides, videos and reviews to match those formats and topics, instead of producing large volumes of content blindly.
  5. Over a few months, this content that mirrors what already ranks lifts a new site against established competitors.
  (2018, older) [L1274.004]
- Publishing more content on a fixed weekly schedule does not automatically improve rankings. SEO gains for a new site usually build slowly, often over several months. (2018, older) [L1274.003]
- A niche that is new in a country can have high-volume keywords with little competition, so ranking can be possible with content alone. Content should target those keywords before adding backlinks. [L1153.062]
- Case: the seller found keywords with high search volume and low competition in the adult coloring book niche, which is new in the US. The seller wrote seven blog articles of about 500 words each, targeting 10 keywords, with no backlinks, press releases or other link building. The result was page 2 rankings for nine of the 10 keywords and a page 3 ranking for one, which the seller saw as the basis for future free organic traffic. [L1153.061]

### Competitor Research

- Check competitor content for every new content piece: have competitors covered this topic? If they have, note it and aim to do it better. If no competitor does content marketing in the niche, this part of the research can be skipped. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.009]
- The competitor research document made early in the programme asks whether each competitor does content marketing. Check that answer before searching for competitor content. Competitors with no content marketing can be skipped in content research. Those with content get noted and must be beaten. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.023]
- Do not reinvent the wheel. In markets with existing content, study what works and do everything better. In markets with little content, you are breaking new ground, and organic competition is usually low. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.008]
- The attention matrix lists the people, pages and organisations the audience already follows. Use it to find out whether those sources have already produced similar content. It can be shared with any writer or producer. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.010]
- The attention matrix is a document that maps where a store's audience pays attention, and it can be delegated to a virtual assistant. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.010]
- BuzzSumo and Social Animal work like a search engine for content that was shared on social networks. Enter a topic to see shared pieces and how others approached it. Use this for inspiration rather than copying. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.012]
- BuzzSumo has a free sign-up that lists the most popular pages and articles for a niche keyword. Results are sorted by social shares and engagement on Facebook and Twitter, which shows what the audience cares about. (older, 2019) [L0467.011]
- Steps to pick content topics from a competitor's blog:
  1. Use the free version of BuzzSumo to search your niche keywords.
  2. Find which of the competitor's posts have the most shares or comments.
  3. Identify the theme of the best-performing content.
  4. Create your own content on a similar but different angle, rather than rewording theirs.
  (older, 2020) [L0961.075]
- Competitor collection pages, step by step:
  1. Search the competitor category on Google (for example *L-shaped standing desks*).
  2. Note the content types ranking (product pages, collection pages, top 10 lists).
  3. If lists rank, create your own top 10 article on the collection page.
  4. Review the top results and determine the features and topics they cover.
  5. Write your own content covering the same topics, but better.
  6. Add it below the products on the collection page.
  [L0274.038]
- Source C research procedure for a product you sell:
  1. Select a product you are selling.
  2. Search Google for articles on that product.
  3. Read the top three to five ranking articles on the first page of results.
  4. Note the repetitive patterns, topics and information covered across those articles.
  5. Combine the best information from all of them.
  6. Write a new article in your own words covering those same topics (you can use AI to assist).
  7. Post it to your blog to acquire Google SEO ranking.
  [L0280.021]

### Pattern Matching

#### Matching formats that already work

- Research-based content process that avoids copying competitors:
  1. Find the most popular content of competitors, such as blog posts with the most comments or product pages with strong descriptions.
  2. Describe the intro, the body and the conclusion of that piece in one sentence each.
  3. Have the writer start from scratch with that framework, using their own words.
  (older, 2019) [L0467.009]
- Match the format Google returns for your keyword. Build guides, videos and reviews that mirror the top formats and topics. (2018, older) [L1274.004]
- Pattern for content aimed at solution-aware and problem-aware buyers: a broad educational guide titled as the ultimate guide to a product category for a specific buyer type. It explains the buyer's needs, lists the characteristics to look for and recommends a short set of specific products that meet them. Two new examples: *The Complete Guide to Choosing a First Road Bike for Commuters* and *The Ultimate Guide to Picking a Beginner Espresso Machine for Small Kitchens*. [L1117.015]
- Page types to build on the store for SEO, in this order:
  1. Category collection page with buying-intent keywords, turned into a landing page with your product at the top.
  2. Best-of page for the category with the current year in the title.
  3. Listicle, for example a top seven list for the current year.
  4. Generic category page.
  5. For-sale page.
  6. Top seven page.
  7. Brand versus brand comparison pages.
  (older, 2021-22) [L0768.008]
- Brand versus brand comparison pages are covered under Content Types and Formats above. [L0768.011]
- Style guides lead into product lists, so the top products for a themed room sit after the guide. [L0719.040]

### The Content Planner

#### Planner structure

- The content planner is the central sheet where content ideas, research status and promotion channels are recorded for each content piece. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.009]
- The BSGR Content Planner is a spreadsheet with one row per content piece and one column per planning field. Keep the full multi-month list in this single sheet so the status and owner of every piece is visible in one place. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.019]
- Columns of the BSGR Content Planner and what each one records:

| Column | What to record |
|---|---|
| Content Topic/Title | The working title of the planned piece |
| Overview | What the piece should teach or do for the reader |
| Target Keyword + Similar Terms | The main search phrase plus related search terms |
| Similar Online Content | Existing pieces that already cover the topic |
| Assets Required | Images, graphs, PDFs and other materials the piece needs |
| Sources, Stats & Links | Where statistics, images and external links come from |
| Promotion Plan | The channels the piece will be promoted through |
| Who Is Producing This Content? | The writer, freelancer, VA or self responsible for the piece |
| Additional Comments | Any other notes the producer or you need to track |

(older, 2021-22) [L0384.010]

- Content planner layout: a spreadsheet such as Excel or Google Sheets, or a project tool such as Trello. One row per content idea.

| Content Topic/Title | Overview | Target Keyword + Similar Terms | Similar Online Content | Assets Required | Promotion Plan | Who Is Producing This Content? | Additional Comments |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Working title, may be broad at first | Purpose of the piece, what it should do, its goal | Primary keyword from keyword research plus synonyms, variations and similar terms | URLs of ranking pages and high-sharing pieces found in research | Materials the piece needs | How the piece will be promoted | Name of the writer, recorder or producer | Any other notes |

(older, 2021-22) [L0386.014]

- Example of one filled planner row for a home espresso equipment shop: working title *How to choose a grinder as a first-time espresso buyer*, overview of the features the reader needs to judge, main keyword *espresso grinder for beginners* plus close variants, and existing buyer guides listed as similar content. Assets could be a comparison graphic and a checklist PDF. Sources could be manufacturer spec sheets and a credited stock photo. Promotion runs through social posts, the email list and search text ads. The producer is a freelance writer, with a Fiverr designer for the graphic. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.018]
- Example of a filled content planner row for a fictional fishing gear shop: the title is *Best Starter Fishing Rods Guide*. The overview says it should help first-time anglers choose a rod and understand key specs before buying. The target keyword is *starter fishing rod*, with similar terms such as *beginner fishing rod* and *best rod for beginners*. The similar online content column holds the URLs of the top five ranking pages plus any high-sharing pieces found in BuzzSumo. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.024]
- Content plan size formula: months of planning multiplied by content pieces per month equals the number of ideas to fill in. Example: six months at two pieces per month gives twelve content ideas, which can be filled in at any time in advance.

  Ideas = months of planning × pieces per month

  (older, 2021-22) [L0386.015]

#### Filling in the planner

- A content title can start as a broad topic and be refined later. The overview should state who the content is for, what it should teach or achieve, and the goal of the piece. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.017]
- Target keyword and similar online content are recorded in this order:
  1. Take the primary target keyword from keyword research and record it.
  2. Add the similar terms, close variations and synonyms of that keyword, and plan to include them in the content.
  3. Search the keyword in Google and record the URLs of ranking content pieces.
  4. Check social share data for the topic with BuzzSumo or a similar tool and record strong pieces.
  5. Check competitor websites for content aimed at the same audience and record it.
  (older, 2021-22) [L0386.018]
- Research phase output, in order:
  1. Content topic and title.
  2. Overview with the content's purpose and goal.
  3. Target search terms the piece might rank for.
  4. Similar online content that already exists for those terms.
  (older, 2021-22) [L0386.021]
- Anyone who produces content must review all recorded similar online content before writing, recording or planning it, and their version must be better than that content. Share the planner with every writer or producer along with this instruction. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.019]
- Record every asset a piece needs before production starts, such as images, graphs and PDFs, together with where each asset will come from. This lets whoever produces the piece find everything without asking. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.011]
- Stock photo platforms such as Unsplash and Adobe Stock can supply images for content. Any third-party image used in a piece needs its source recorded and attribution given in the piece itself. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.012]
- Record the statistics you plan to cite and the existing content you plan to link to in the planner, backed by enough research to support the idea. Empty fields are acceptable early on if the sources are not yet known. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.013]
- For each planned content piece, choose three to four promotion channels and log them in the content planner. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.007]

### Setup Sequence

#### Gameplan

- Simple content marketing gameplan, in order:
  1. Decide the content format: written content, video content, or a combination of both.
  2. Plan your first 12 content ideas (topics) in your content planning document.
  3. Decide how and by whom the content is produced. For written content, find a content writer. For video, find an editor and, if someone else records, a person to do the recording.
  4. Produce two quality content pieces per month, continuously and without an end date.
  5. After 12 months of consistent work, either increase output above two pieces per month (for example, add one more per month) or hire another person.
  6. Promote each content piece for at least two weeks, ideally up to 30 days, and build backlinks to content pieces where it is relevant.
  (older, 2021-22) [L0383.004]
- Content marketing action plan, in order:
  1. Fill in the attention matrix document. It does not have to be finished in one month, but make a substantial dent.
  2. Do keyword research, or check whether it was already done. If not, do it yourself or outsource it.
  3. Enter the first 12 content ideas into the content planner, which covers about six months of content at two pieces per month.
  4. Complete research for the first four content ideas in the planner.
  5. Every two months, block up to 30 minutes in a calendar to research the next four ideas and add more content ideas, so about six months stay planned ahead.
  6. Post a job to find a content producer, such as a writer or video editor. Give candidates a test, hire one, and produce two quality pieces of content per month.
  7. For each planned content piece, choose three to four promotion channels and record them in the content planner.
  (older, 2021-22) [L0381.001]
- Action steps to set up the content plan:
  1. Fill the planner with 12 content ideas that mix problem-solving and entertaining topics suited to your market or niche.
  2. Complete the research columns for the first four pieces.
  3. Add a calendar entry for two months from now to research the next four pieces.
  4. Make sure a producer is ready before content production starts. Keyword research is done alongside these steps.
  (older, 2021-22) [L0384.017]

#### Planning horizon

- Keep a rolling plan of about six months of content in the content planner at all times, so new ideas are always added before the existing schedule runs out. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.003]
- Plan 12 content ideas in the planner first. That equals about six months of content at two pieces per month. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.004]
- Complete keyword and content research for the first four content ideas right after planning them, before production starts. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.005]
- Plan topics six months ahead, and research them in batches of four. Do not research all six months in one sitting, because that takes too long. Schedule the next research session every two months, then research the following four pieces before their production window starts. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.008]
- Every two-month research review should be a fixed calendar entry that is actually kept. Failing to schedule content research and planning in a calendar or management tool leads to missed updates. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.011]
- Content should be produced from a researched, documented plan, so every planned piece should already have some research behind it. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.001]

### Production System

#### Keyword-led weekly system

- Content marketing system with seven steps, repeated for each keyword every week:
  1. Define the target market and write the customer profile.
  2. Set up an email opt-in on the store.
  3. Build a keyword list from informational queries.
  4. Write a blog article for one keyword.
  5. Create a custom image for that article that contains the keyword and matches the brand.
  6. Write a plain text email that drives traffic to the article.
  7. Upload the article to the blog, then send the email to the list.
  [L0380.014]
- Create a separate blog for the store and publish each article as its own page. The email then links directly to that article page instead of the homepage. [L0380.015]
- Keyword-based content is produced in this order:
  1. Research the keywords yourself.
  2. Create the content. Most beginners should write it themselves. Later, collaborate with niche influencers who can contribute pieces, or hire an expert.
  3. Make basic graphics yourself in Canva, or outsource images on Fiverr.
  [L1120.003]

#### Article workflow and quality control

- Content production workflow from start to finish:
  1. Choose keywords with the Ahrefs Content Gap method and vet them with RAW.
  2. Create the article in the Google Docs template and brief the writer with the brand guideline.
  3. Have the writer complete the article with an editor.
  4. Edit the article with a native English editor.
  5. Write the headline and meta description, often with a sales copywriter.
  6. Create a custom image with the keyword in its alt tag.
  7. Upload the article and check its SEO with a plugin such as Yoast.
  8. Publish the article.
  9. Track the article's email signups each month and use the best performers to find similar keywords.
  [L0382.039]
- Templates for customer surveys, blog post creation guidelines, podcast keyword research and podcast content processes systematise outsourcing and maintain quality at scale. [L0326.002]
- Writer brand guideline rules are covered under Writers and Outsourcing below. [L0382.057]

### Visual Assets and AI Assistance

- Stock photo platforms such as Unsplash and Adobe Stock can supply images for content. Record the source and give attribution in the piece. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.012]
- Content created from a blog post can be turned into an infographic with ChatGPT. Steps:
  1. Copy the URL of the blog post from the store's blog.
  2. In ChatGPT, send the first prompt for an infographic outline together with the blog URL.
  3. Review the outline ChatGPT creates and tell it what to add or remove.
  4. Send the second prompt to turn the approved outline into an infographic image.
  5. Ask for revisions or a different colour scheme, such as the store's brand colours, if needed.
  6. Download the finished image by clicking it.
  [L1075.016]
- Prompt for the infographic workflow:

```
Step 1: Please create an info graphic based on this blog post: [paste the blog post URL here]. Create an outline of the text that will appear on the infographic first.
Step 2 (after reviewing and approving the outline): Please turn this into an info graphic image. Keep all the points in the approved outline, make the main headings bold, and use the color scheme of [brand name / website colors].
```

[L1075.017]

- Mixing text, images and video on each blog post suits readers who prefer different formats. (older, 2019) [L0467.016]
- Every blog post should include text, images and a video, with the images and video placed within the text. Videos should come from the suppliers or the store owner. (older, 2019) [L0467.017]
- Use a custom image for each article, with the keyword in the alt tag. [L0382.039]

### Writers and Outsourcing

#### Who can write

- Professional writing ability, marketing expertise or native English fluency are not requirements for content creation. These skills are learnable over time or outsourceable to freelancers, which removes a psychological barrier that stops many from starting a content strategy. [L0226.046]
- Keep research and planning in-house: decide which article titles, About Us format and product page elements the site needs. Outsource only the writing, because the owner must control the content direction. (older, 2019) [L0467.012]
- Start content writer search on Upwork and specialised platforms, but recognise that great general writing does not produce great dropshipping content. Pivot to Facebook groups for niche enthusiasts who understand your category. They write with insider passion and knowledge that generic writers lack. (older, 2022) [L0249.102]
- When outsourcing content writing, find niche enthusiasts in Facebook groups rather than professional writers. Niche passion produces better content for dropshipping stores than generic writing skill. (older, 2022) [L0249.065]
- If the writer has no niche knowledge, find an experienced writer in niche communities such as Reddit or Facebook groups and post a paid gig request there. (2023) [L0855.152]
- ProBlogger has a jobs board where content writers can be hired for blog and written content. Its community is mostly full-time freelance bloggers, usually native English speakers, which gives better applicants than general freelance platforms. (older, 2021-22) [L0379.008]

#### Finding writers step by step

- Finding writers for a broad, non-technical niche:
  1. Post a content writer job on Upwork with the outline of the pieces you want.
  2. Send the same piece brief to at least three writers.
  3. Compare the three samples and pick the writer whose voice fits best.
  4. Send the remaining work to that writer.
  (older, 2019) [L0467.013]
- Finding writers for a technical or specialised niche:
  1. Search the niche term on Facebook and open the Groups results.
  2. Join all relevant groups.
  3. Identify members who post often and whose posts get many likes and comments.
  4. Send them a private message with an offer to write articles for your site.
  (older, 2019) [L0467.014]
- Outreach template for a niche group member:

```
Hi [Name], my name is [Your name] from [your store domain]. I saw your posts in the [Facebook group name] group, and the feedback they get from the members is great. We are creating new content for our website, and I would like to know whether you would be interested in writing a few articles for us or [other content you need]. If you are interested, I can send you the outline and the list of article titles we need, and we can take it from there.
```

[L0467.015] (older, 2019)

#### Pricing and cost benchmarks

- Freelance blog writers on Upwork were priced at $20-40 per article. (older, 2020) [L0268.052]
- Hire a content writer on Upwork at $20-40 per article, or use voice-to-text with a virtual assistant to write articles. (older, 2020) [L0268.052]
- A professionally written and Google-ranked blog article budget is about $100. [L0280.022]
- A blog post priced at $500 is an example of a fair freelance price for quality written content. A comparable piece can be obtained for $250 if the writer's quality is proven. (older, 2021-22) [L0379.020]

#### Standards for outsourced writing

- Do not use PLR (Private Label Rights) content. It is rewritten and republished everywhere, and you need unique content for your business. (older, 2020) [L0268.053]
- For a US supplement store, article writers must not claim that a product cures a disease, because readers and the FDA will not accept such claims. Put this prohibition in the ground rules of the brand guideline given to writers. [L0382.057]
- A writer brand guideline can set key factors per article, such as a minimum of 1,000 words, three links to external sites, and the target keyword used a fixed number of times, for example three. Editors use the same list to check each draft. [L0382.058]
- Have the writer complete the article, then have a native English editor edit it. [L0382.039]
- Have the person producing the content review all similar online content before writing, and make their version better. Share the planner with them. (older, 2021-22) [L0386.019]
- A virtual assistant can build the attention matrix and receive the planner. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.010]
- Hire a content producer such as a writer or video editor by posting a job, giving candidates a test, hiring one, and producing two quality pieces of content per month. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.001]
- For written content, find a content writer. For video, find an editor and, if someone else records, a person to do the recording. Make sure a producer is ready before production starts. (older, 2021-22) [L0383.004]
- Beginners should write their own content first. Later, collaborate with niche influencers who can contribute pieces, or hire an expert. [L1120.003]
- Example of a designer partner: a Fiverr designer makes the graphic for the freelance writer's piece. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.018]
- For a beginner, a realistic minimum is two high-quality content pieces per month. Publishing one or two pieces per week is not realistic, and it also makes it hard to promote each piece properly. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.003]
- Increase publishing frequency above two pieces per month only when you already have experience producing content and can keep it genuinely valuable. Without that experience, do not overreach. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.004]

### Cadence and Volume

- Content marketing only builds results when it is published consistently. Irregular output loses momentum, while steady output compounds over time. (older, 2021-22) [L0384.001]
- Produce two high-quality content pieces per month as an ongoing target, not as a one-off launch goal. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.006]
- Target a minimum of two high-quality content pieces per month as an ongoing baseline, not as a campaign with an end date. (older, 2021-22) [L0383.007]
- Publish about two high-quality content pieces per month. Daily publishing is not needed for a high-ticket dropshipping business, and posting more low-quality content adds no value. Quality matters more than quantity. (older, 2021-22) [L0414.024]
- A content marketing plan of two high-quality pieces of content per month is a workable publishing rhythm. (older, 2021-22) [L0943.002]
- Start publishing blog posts once every two to three weeks: publish 2-4 articles per week consistently rather than bulk publishing. [L0226.012]
- A blog needs about one quality post per week or every other week that is valuable to the reader. More posts do not help if the quality is low. (2025) [L0857.046]
- There is no fixed minimum or maximum for content articles per month. One high-quality piece per month can be a workable start, provided it is better than the existing results for its target search terms. (2023) [L0970.127]
- Blog posting frequency: every other week is effective. (older, 2022) [L0272.039]
- Publishing more content on a fixed weekly schedule does not automatically improve rankings. SEO gains for a new site usually build slowly, often over several months. (2018, older) [L1274.003]
- Evergreen keywords are optimised once on existing pages. Trending keywords can justify one or two new blog posts per month. [L1120.023]
- Move from two pieces per month to more after 12 months of consistent work, for example by adding one more per month, or hire another person. (older, 2021-22) [L0383.004]

### Distribution and Promotion

- Promote each content piece for at least two weeks, ideally up to 30 days, and build backlinks to the content pieces where it is relevant. (older, 2021-22) [L0383.004]
- For each content piece, choose three to four promotion channels and log them in the planner. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.007]
- Content is one part of a connected marketing system. The same pieces feed email, SEO, link building and retargeting, so each piece should be promoted through those channels. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.002]
- Each piece should have a place on the store's website, so it can later be reused for link building and SEO. (older, 2021-22) [L0381.018]
- Publish three to five blog posts on a new affiliate site before buying traffic, so you have something to link to when you share the site elsewhere. [L0514.008]
- Content on social media can come from the blog, so the blog provides material for social posts. [L0835.096]

### Holiday and Seasonal Content

- Start publishing blog content for the promotion periods now. Target keywords in the form *niche* black friday deals and *niche* cyber monday deals, with the niche name in place of niche. This content should be live before the sale dates. (2025) [L1222.011]
- Publish keyword-targeted blog posts for each holiday offer in the coming month, using the pattern [niche] + holiday deal, for example Black Friday deals or Cyber Monday deals. Publish early so Google can index and rank them before the holiday searches peak. [L1224.032]
- Each holiday blog post should explain how to get the best deals for that holiday, which offers are available, which products they apply to, and how to take advantage of them. [L1224.033]
- A page built for a time-limited event, such as a homebrewing trade show, can rank for the event name in search. After the promotion ends, keep the page as evergreen content and consider an affiliate arrangement with the organiser for ticket sales, or a note that the store attends each year. [L1204.072]

### Where Sources Disagree

| Topic | Source value | Applies when |
|---|---|---|
| Posting cadence | 2-4 articles per week, published consistently (undated) [L0226.012] | Aggressive publishing plan, not the beginner baseline |
| Posting cadence | One quality post per week or every other week (2025) [L0857.046] | Quality is high enough to justify it |
| Posting cadence | Every other week (2022, older) [L0272.039] | Blog-led store with limited production capacity |
| Posting cadence | Two high-quality pieces per month, minimum baseline (2021-22, older) [L0384.003, L0381.006, L0383.007, L0414.024, L0943.002] | Beginner baseline with a planner and two-month research cycle |
| Posting cadence | One high-quality piece per month can be a workable start if it beats existing results (2023) [L0970.127] | Starting out, with a clear target search term |
| Posting cadence | More posts on a fixed weekly schedule do not automatically rank (2018, older) [L1274.003] | Any stage, as a warning against volume alone |
| Posting cadence | Increase above two per month only with experience (2021-22, older) [L0384.004] | Producers without content experience should hold at two |
| Article length | Product release about 500 words, how-to 1,000-2,000, comparisons and reviews 1,000+, news 500+, testimonials 500-1,000 (undated) [L0226.011] | Choose by content type |
| Article length | 300 to 1,000 words depending on topic, no filler (2022, older) [L0272.039] | Topics that can be explained fully in that range |
| Article length | Minimum 1,000 words per article in a writer guideline (supplement example, undated) [L0382.058] | Set by the brand guideline for that store |
| Freelance writer price | $20-40 per article on Upwork (2020, older) [L0268.052] | Simple articles from a generalist |
| Freelance writer price | About $100 for a professionally written, Google-ranked article (undated) [L0280.022] | Ranking-focused article |
| Freelance writer price | $500 as a fair price for quality writing, $250 if quality is proven (2021-22, older) [L0379.020] | Experienced, proven writers |
| Writer sourcing | Upwork for a broad, non-technical niche (2019, older) [L0467.013] | Generalist niche with a clear outline |
| Writer sourcing | Facebook groups for technical or specialised niches (2019, older) [L0467.014] | Niche with knowledgeable enthusiasts |
| Writer sourcing | Start on Upwork, then pivot to niche Facebook groups (2022, older) [L0249.102] | Dropshipping content that needs insider knowledge |
| Writer sourcing | Niche enthusiasts in Facebook groups rather than professional writers (2022, older) [L0249.065] | Dropshipping content generally |
| Writer sourcing | Paid gig posted in Reddit or Facebook groups (2023) [L0855.152] | Writer without niche knowledge |
| Writer sourcing | ProBlogger jobs board for blog writers, usually native English speakers (2021-22, older) [L0379.008] | Blog writing needing better applicants than general freelance platforms |
| Format | Written only, video only, or both, chosen before planning (2021-22, older) [L0383.005] | Choosing the format at the start |
| Format | Every blog post should include text, images and video (2019, older) [L0467.017] | Stores that can produce video |
| Format | Written blog is worthwhile on its own (2021-22, older) [L0462.026] | Stores without video production |
| Start timing | Blog content once the store has traffic (2023) [L0835.096] | Store already has visitors to learn from |
| Start timing | Done-for-you content only after store completion and traffic data (2023) [L0804.062] | Paid content packages |
| Start timing | Build content from the beginning, not later (undated) [L0500.011] | New stores without a content system |
| Start timing | Three to five posts before buying traffic on a new affiliate site (undated) [L0514.008] | Affiliate sites |
| Start timing | Start producing now, adjust the plan later (2021-22, older) [L0381.008] | Stores that are ready to produce |

The timing conflict is about different things. Content inside the store's own blog is best started early, and a done-for-you package or paid content waits until traffic data exists [L0500.011, L0835.096, L0804.062]. Affiliate sites need a few posts before any paid traffic, so the posts come first [L0514.008].

### Role of Content in the Funnel

#### Content Compared with Sales Copy

- Educational writing that only informs, such as a general guide or blog post, is not sales copy and does not move a visitor toward a purchase. It can have a place on an e-commerce site, but product, collection and sales copy should each be written to earn one defined action. [L1460.013]
- Authoritative, educational content in text, image, video or social form raises conversion, especially for high-priced or technical products. It engages buyers earlier on the customer awareness path than product pages alone. You do not need hundreds of blog posts; you need more useful content than your competitors. [L1522.031]
- Content is a long-term investment that builds organic traffic and rankings over time. Unique product descriptions, collection page copy and blog posts should include the correct keywords and be formatted properly. [L1283.095]

#### What Makes Content Worth Producing

- Great content must be deep, relevant to what is happening now, shareable, well researched and worth linking to. Content that meets these criteria attracts attention, builds trust, shows relevance and authority, gives search engines what they reward, and makes a business look unique. [L1461.001]
- Original content is a competitive advantage because no competitor can copy it, which makes the business feel more unique to visitors. [L1461.009]
- Create unique general content such as blog posts, guest posts, and downloadable supplier catalogs, sales sheets and spec sheets. Unpublished text from supplier materials gives a large SEO advantage once you publish it on your store. [L1357.038]

#### Depth Before Width

- Shallow content is common online, so producing deep content is the way to stand out in search and social channels. Search engines and consumers can see large numbers of thin pages, so depth is the differentiator. [L1461.010]
- There are over one trillion individual web pages online, and most of them are shallow, with very little substance. Because thin pages are so common, deep and comprehensive pages stand out to both search engines and consumers. [L1461.026]
- Content should be deep before it is wide; only a small share of web content is deep, so depth is a differentiator that compounds over time. A practical test is whether the piece gives visitors more value than any competitor's version on the same topic. [L1461.021]

#### Mixing Content Types by Funnel Stage

- Problem-solving content is often the best starting point for a business that has no content yet, because the team already knows the customer problems from running the business. It ranks well in search and YouTube and also improves conversion by helping buyers move through the customer journey. [L1461.014]
- Entertainment content is the most widely engaging content and is the main tool for filling the top of the funnel, since it does not require the visitor to be problem aware. It should still be relevant to the target market and appeal mainly to that audience rather than everyone. [L1461.015]
- Process for using entertainment content to fill a sales funnel:
  1. Publish interest-based or best-of content that attracts relevant visitors to the website.
  2. Offer an opt-in on the page to add visitors to the email audience.
  3. Place a cookie for retargeting so visitors can be shown ads later.
  4. Serve further content to move them through the funnel toward the desired awareness stage.

  [L1461.017]
- Example: a beard-care brand's blog mixes content types on one landing page: a product-related review of nose hair trimmers (problem solving), a controversial opinion piece against neckties (entertainment), a personal-story piece about facial hair (entertainment), and a men's hat style guide (a mix of both). This combination of types is presented as a good model for a mixed blog strategy. [L1461.020]

#### Store Launch and Blog Timing

*Source: Source A, 2022 (older advice).*

- Blog posts are not required before supplier approval. If they are holding up the store launch, skip them and add them later. [L1281.116]

### Content Types

#### Priority Order

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- These six content types are the most shared and most linked to, and they lend themselves to depth, which makes them the strongest choice for SEO and engagement results. Backlinks from other sites are a key reason to favor them. [L1421.002]
- Focus first on how-to, listicles and ultimate guides, since they are the easiest to produce and the best starting point. Best of the interwebs and expert roundups come after these. [L1421.030]

#### Problem-Solving Content

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Problem-solving content is usually the easiest type to produce because most problems have known answers, and the product itself is typically the answer. It gives immediate value and can engage people at the problem-aware, solution-aware and product-aware stages of the customer awareness pyramid. [L1461.012]
- Types of problem-solving content: [L1461.013]

  | Type | Purpose |
  |---|---|
  | How-to guides | Step-by-step instructions to solve a specific problem |
  | Ultimate guides | Most comprehensive resource on a topic, helping choose products and answer purchase questions |
  | Product comparisons | Pros and cons of specific products for middle-of-funnel buyers deciding between options |
  | Listicles (solution lists) | Numbered lists of solutions or products to a problem, such as 'top 10 ways' or 'five ways to avoid' |
  | Explanatory content | Simple explanations of technical terms, industry lingo or product technology to help customers understand |
  | FAQ content | Answers to common questions, optionally combined with a listicle format, such as 'top 5 questions when shopping for X' |

#### How-To Guides

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- How-to content, built step by step:
  1. Pick a concrete problem or pain point the reader has and make the piece a direct answer to it.
  2. Break the solution into logical steps, one segment per step, presented in order (step one, step two, step three and so on).
  3. Explain each step as clearly as possible in jargon-free language, written for the reader with the most basic knowledge.
  4. Use as many images as possible: in written content, insert images; in video, show each step either with an edited static image or with a hands-on recording of the action.

  [L1421.003]
- Breaking a how-to into small, simple steps makes it feel easy for a beginner, which is what drives the best result for this content type. [L1421.004]

#### Listicles

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- For listicles, the title contains a number and the list covers different items. They suit both problem solving and entertainment, and they are among the most shared and linked content types. [L1421.005]
- Listicles have a low barrier to entry because most of the information already exists online or offline; the work is mainly collecting, organizing and rewriting it in a unique way instead of copying. [L1421.006]
- Listicles often work well with a short introduction, a table of contents with anchor links to each section, practical info such as required gear, and a short description per item covering location, who it suits and the conditions. [L1421.010]
- Listicle research before publishing:
  1. Search for the topic and see how many existing listicles already cover it.
  2. If a competitor has published a top N list, publish a longer or differently sized list instead, for example top 17, top 20 or top 21.

  [L1421.007]
- Listicle numbers that are not round (for example 7, 17, 23 or 33) tend to get higher engagement and more clicks than round numbers such as 5, 10, 15 or 20. [L1421.008]
- Title pattern and examples are in [Pattern Matching](#pattern-matching) below. [L1421.009]

#### Ultimate Guides

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Ultimate guides are the most valuable content and are highly linkable, which makes them strong for problem solving and link building. Their title must be earned: if a piece is called the ultimate guide, it must be the deepest and best resource on that topic. [L1421.011]
- Ultimate guides can be very long, from roughly 5,000 to 10,000 words. Even when little competing content exists, the piece should be written as if someone could publish a better one soon. [L1421.012]
- Ultimate guides suit high-volume, more competitive search terms, which are broader and harder to rank for. These pieces are easier to build backlinks to, and such terms generally need more backlinks to reach top positions on Google. [L1421.014]
- An ultimate guide can combine how-to and listicle styles, for example an ultimate guide to choosing a product that works as a how-to, or an unlimited-count list where each item is covered in detail. [L1421.015]
- Not every piece should be an ultimate guide because it takes more work, but some should be included in the mix. [L1421.016]

#### Best of the Interwebs

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Best of the interwebs pieces collect existing online content, which makes them quick to produce. Images can be taken from the original sources, so custom visuals are usually not needed. [L1421.017]
- Each item in a best of the interwebs piece needs commentary: explain why it is good, what it does particularly well or what you like about it. A simple link with no insight is not enough; personal opinion is acceptable. [L1421.019]
- Entertainment form: curated lists of the best blogs, YouTube channels or resources in the target niche, bringing relevant visitors to the site. [L1461.016]

#### Expert Roundups

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Expert roundup production:
  1. Choose a popular question or statement in your market.
  2. Identify experts with large audiences using the attention matrix, meaning people regarded as authorities who already have audiences through websites, social media or YouTube.
  3. Find their contact details on their website or social media, then email each one with the simple question.
  4. Contact more experts than you need, because not all will respond.
  5. Keep the question simple so that a few-line answer is enough; for example, ask for one favorite item.
  6. Build the piece around each answer, adding a short explanation and a relevant image sourced from the internet.
  7. When published, email the participants with a thank-you, a link and a request to share the piece.

  [L1421.020]
- Expert roundups use experts' names and trust to attract attention to your piece, which is useful when your own name is not yet trusted in the market. [L1421.021]
- An expert roundup should state in its headline that it is based on expert feedback; leaving that out weakens the piece. [L1421.022]
- Expert roundups can take longer, from a few days to a couple of weeks, because the work depends on experts replying. [L1421.023]
- Roundup content gains reach through reciprocity: after publishing, send the contributing experts the link and ask them to share it with their audience, which lets the business use their audience to gain exposure. [L1461.019]
- Roundup content in the entertainment table: expert quotes gathered by emailing experts for a short comment; the published roundup is shared with the experts, who often share it with their audience. [L1461.016]

#### Controversial Content

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Controversial content takes a position that goes against commonly held beliefs. It should be used sparingly, because it is highly engaging but polarizing. [L1421.024]
- A controversial position must be explained in depth with supporting evidence and a real attempt to convince the reader. A fake position cannot be defended in depth, so it should not be used. [L1421.025]
- Controversial content in the entertainment table: taking an opposing position on a commonly held belief in the market to attract attention. [L1461.016]

#### Entertainment Content Formats

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Types of entertainment content: [L1461.016]

  | Type | Purpose |
  |---|---|
  | Interest listicles | Lists about a topic of interest to the audience, such as top surf breaks or top destinations, without selling products |
  | Best-of-the-internet lists | Curated lists of the best blogs, YouTube channels or resources in the target niche, bringing relevant visitors to the site |
  | Roundup content | Expert quotes gathered by emailing experts for a short comment; the published roundup is shared with the experts, who often share it with their audience |
  | Breaking news content | Coverage of new developments in the market; high engagement at the time but not evergreen, so weak for long-term ranking |
  | Controversial content | Taking an opposing position on a commonly held belief in the market to attract attention |

### Idea Sourcing

#### Keyword Research as the Source

*Source: Source A (no year given).*

- Take topic ideas for pages and blog posts from the keyword research worksheet created during organic keyword research, not from guesses. [L1321.005]

#### Community and Audience Questions

*Source: Source A (no year given).*

- Blog post ideas can come from the most popular threads on community sites such as Reddit within a niche. Note which questions get the most engagement and turn them into content candidates. [L1321.007]

*Source: Source C.*

- A public container such as a blog or website listing content in reverse chronological order keeps you producing because the newest item is what visitors see first. Use it also as a place to develop ideas that are not finished yet. [L1343.019]

#### Platform Analytics and Search Questions

*Source: Source E, 2025-26.*

- Rising volume of AI-generated, recycled and syndicated content causes audience fatigue, and attention is split between creators, streaming services and daily life. Content needs a clear strategy to stop the scroll and hold viewers. [L1457.004]
- Idea-sourcing procedure:
  1. Open the analytics of the main platform, TikTok being the most detailed for audience insight, and Instagram analytics for audience behaviour.
  2. Read the search terms your audience uses and the videos they interact with.
  3. Note the questions behind those searches.
  4. Build videos that answer these questions before the viewer asks them.

  [L1457.007]

#### Problem-Based Ideas

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Before producing any piece of content, follow these steps:
  1. Research what already exists on the topic by searching for it and reviewing who has covered it.
  2. Identify which aspects the existing top content has not covered.
  3. Plan how to go deeper and provide more value than the existing pieces.
  4. Produce the content so it aims to be the most comprehensive resource online on that topic.

  [L1461.008]

### Competitor and SERP Research

#### Reviewing Existing Coverage

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Listicle research: search for the topic, count how many existing listicles cover it, and if a competitor has published a top N list, publish a longer or differently sized list instead (for example top 17, top 20 or top 21). [L1421.007]
- Ultimate guides target high-volume, competitive terms that need more backlinks to reach top positions on Google. [L1421.014]
- Shallow content is common, so a depth-first approach is the way to stand out against what already ranks. [L1461.010]
- Gap test before production: find what the top content misses, go deeper than it, and aim to be the most comprehensive resource. [L1461.008]

#### Competitor and Keyword Data

*Source: Source A, 2022 (older advice).*

- Six-month done-for-you content plan process: [L1281.115]
  1. Research the niche, the site, the competitors and the keywords.
  2. Build a content marketing plan from that research.
  3. Write blog posts and source images.
  4. Publish the content on a schedule over six months to build organic traffic from Google.

### Pattern Matching

#### Listicle Titles

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Listicle title pattern: [Number] + [best/top] + [item] + [location or qualifier] + [audience or experience level qualifier]. The qualifier at the end widens the appeal to a segment, such as beginners, advanced riders or first-time visitors, while the base title still captures the broad search. [L1421.009]
  - Travel niche example: "11 Best Hiking Trails in Colorado for Every Fitness Level"
  - Finance niche example: "19 Best Budget Apps for Freelancers Who Are Just Starting Out"
- Use non-round numbers such as 7, 17, 23 or 33, which tend to earn higher engagement and more clicks than 5, 10, 15 or 20. [L1421.008]
- Listicle structure: short introduction, table of contents with anchor links, practical info such as required gear, and a short description per item covering location, who it suits and the conditions. [L1421.010]

#### Trend Patterns

*Source: Source E, 2025-26.*

- Trend search example: a skincare brand checks the search terms in TikTok analytics and finds a viral technique, such as slugging, being searched for often. The brand then builds videos that answer the questions viewers are about to ask, before they search for them. [L1457.025]

### Content Planner

#### Starting Plan Structure

*Source: Source A, 2022 (older advice).*

- The six-month plan runs research, a content marketing plan, writing and sourcing images, then scheduled publishing. [L1281.115]

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Content planning method: for the first 12 ideas, think of a few different versions of each content type and include them in the plan. [L1421.026]

#### Content Pillars

*Source: Source E, 2025-26.*

- Each content pillar should align with the brand's expertise, its business goals and what the audience cares about. Pillars keep content balanced, prevent random posting, simplify monthly planning and show which formats to scale. [L1457.011]
- Content pillars are assigned to fixed weekdays. Two pillars per week is the minimum, five pillars fit daily posting Monday to Friday, and seven pillars are possible but usually too demanding. [L1457.010]
- Test a set of content pillars for two months, then review performance data. In the next month, increase the posting frequency of the pillars that perform well and reduce the frequency of those that do not. [L1457.012]
- Posting without pillars leads to disorganized content and makes batch production much harder. Without a system, the creator also loses motivation more easily. [L1457.013]

#### Flexibility in the Plan

*Source: Source E, 2025-26.*

- Keep some flexibility in the schedule, for example one fixed weekday for trend-based videos. Trend slots are harder to batch but allow a fast reaction to trends while the rest of the plan stays fixed. [L1457.017]

### Cadence

#### Publishing Pace

*Source: Source A (no year given).*

| Stage | Pace | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Starting | One new piece of content per month, enough to get started and to see how the content performs | [L1321.026] |
| Later, once the process runs smoothly | One new piece of content every two weeks | [L1321.027] |

- The sources set different pace figures for different situations: the monthly and fortnightly paces above apply to a single store's own content plan, while the per-writer rate below applies to a writer's weekly workload. [L1321.026, L1321.027, L1396.043]

#### Batching and Production Rhythm

*Source: Source E, 2025-26.*

- Batching means filming a whole series in one session. For example, two hours of filming can produce ten videos for one weekday slot, covering the next ten weeks. [L1457.015]

#### Writer Throughput

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Ask writers to focus on one product category per week, for example twelve collection pages for similar products, so they keep the same subject and write faster. A writer needing about 1.5 hours per 500-word page can usually finish about two pages per day. Plan about twelve content items per week per writer as a starting point. [L1396.043]
- Writing a 500-word collection page took a writer about 1.5 hours, so a typical day allows two or three pages. Set weekly targets based on this measured rate, then adjust after one week of real results. Do not expect a 500-word article in half an hour. [L1396.044]

### Writers and Outsourcing

#### Weekly Assignment Cards

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Assign weekly content work to a writer using this structure:
  1. Create a weekly project card, for example for a specific week in October.
  2. Add the target keywords in the card description.
  3. Set a length, such as 300 to 500 words for collection or product page content.
  4. Attach the link to each collection or product page to be written.
  5. Move each card to the done list when the writer finishes, so progress is visible.

  [L1396.042]
- Create blog or buying guide tasks using this structure:
  1. Write the blog name or topic.
  2. Add reference sources, such as manufacturer pages, reviews or related blogs.
  3. List the product links that must appear.
  4. Set the target keywords and the desired length.
  5. Move the card to done when published.

  [L1396.045]

#### Writer Capacity

*Source: Source B, 2021-22 (older advice).*

- Plan about twelve content items per week per writer as a starting point, one product category per week, adjusted after one week of measured results. [L1396.043, L1396.044]

### Holiday and Seasonal Content

*Source: Source A, 2025.*

- Publish seasonal blog posts about each holiday promotion at least a month before it starts, so Google can index and rank them in time. Example titles are [niche] Black Friday deals and [niche] Cyber Monday deals, each explaining the offer, when it runs, which products it covers and how to use it. [L1287.034]

#### Further details

- Customer testimonial request email: 'Hi [Name], I noticed you recently purchased [Product]. We'd love to feature your story. Would you mind sharing: (1) a few photos of you using the product, (2) how the product improved your [activity/situation], (3) any advice for other customers considering it, (4) where/how you use it. In return, we'll offer you [free accessories/coupon/discount] and feature your story on our blog and social media.' [L0226.013]

## Featured Snippets, FAQ and Voice Search

### Scope: Which Searches AI Answers Affect

- AI-generated overviews in Google search results affect rankings for research-based informational queries, but they do not affect e-commerce product page rankings. Product names, brand names, and SKU searches are not affected by AI. Continue applying standard SEO best practices to product pages unchanged, because they remain effective for e-commerce. [L0255.073]
- Most ecommerce traffic comes from buying-intent searches such as product names, brand names, and SKU numbers. AI answer features mainly affect question-style searches, so this traffic is largely not reduced by them. [L0807.034]

### Finding the Questions Customers Ask

- Product descriptions and blog content should answer the questions people ask AI tools about a product or niche, not only include keywords. Questions collected from forums, communities, and search results are the raw material for this content. [L0472.076]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* People usually type shortened search phrases, but they speak complete questions when using voice search. Keyword research for voice should therefore target full natural-language questions. [L0658.002]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* One short typed keyword, such as "best smart speaker", usually stands for several full spoken questions. Expand each target term into question variants, then cover those variants in the FAQ or page content. [L0658.018]

  **Pattern:**
  - Target term: "best smart speaker"
  - Expand to: "what is the best...", "where can I find the best...", "where can I buy the best..."
  - Example for a different product: "best running shoe for flat feet" expands to "what is the best running shoe for flat feet?" and "where can I buy running shoes for flat feet?" [L0658.018]

- *Older advice (2021-22):* Spoken searches often ask for nearby options in the first person, such as "what's the closest restaurant to me?". Businesses with a local presence should therefore include question-and-answer content that names their location or service area in the question form. [L0658.019]

### Validating a Question Before You Target It

- *Older advice (2021-22):* Before using a question as an H2 heading to target question-and-answer featured snippets, validate that it is an actual search query. Type the potential question into Google to see whether it produces results, or check your keyword research data to confirm that people search for that question. This ensures your featured snippet strategy targets real user searches. [L0311.027]

### Winning Featured Snippets

**Eligibility and frequency**

- *Older advice (2021-22):* Featured snippets appear for only a share of search queries (roughly 12 percent, as estimated at recording time). When one exists and you win it, the click-through rate is much higher than that of a normal top result. [L0662.001]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* A page can only be pulled into a featured snippet if it already ranks in the top four organic positions for that search term. A page ranking at position 10 has no chance, so ranking in the top four must come first. [L0662.002]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Only pages already ranking at positions 1 to 4 should be optimized for featured snippets, because pages at lower positions are too far from the top to benefit yet. Focus first on raising rankings. [L0662.006]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Featured snippets are mostly triggered by informational queries such as "what is" or "how to" questions, and they mainly show content pages such as blog posts. Product searches rarely show them. [L0662.007]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Even when product pages rank in positions 1 to 4, check their keywords for featured snippets, but expect the realistic wins to come from content pages. [L0662.008]

**How Google chooses the snippet**

- *Older advice (2021-22):* Google chooses the snippet by content structure, not by ranking alone. A page in position 1 is more likely to be used than one in position 4, but if the top result lacks a clean answer block, Google moves down to the next result and pulls from there. [L0662.003]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Google pulls only the short answer or list from the top of a page, not the full article. A long piece must therefore still show its concise answer block at the beginning to be eligible. [L0662.012]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Leapfrogging: a page ranking at position 3 can win the featured snippet and appear above the position 1 result, even if it cannot outrank the position 1 page directly. This works only when the sites above lack a good snippet-ready answer, and not for every keyword. [L0662.013]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Google may choose a short, clear definition from a third-party blog over its own documentation for a featured snippet. A precise definition placed at the top of your own page can therefore win the snippet even against authoritative sources. [L0662.015]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* A featured snippet sits above the organic results and pushes them further down. Google often shows a set of related question-and-answer boxes beneath it. This placement is why its click-through rate is much higher than that of a normal first-place result. [L0662.016]

**Snippet types**

- *Older advice (2021-22):* The two common snippet types are definition (a direct answer to "what is X") and list (a numbered list, such as "top 10 ways"). Match your content to the type shown in the search results. [L0662.009]

**Placing content for a snippet**

- *Older advice (2021-22):* Add a clear definition at the very start of the post, as the first element of the page, to capture definition-type featured snippets, provided the page already ranks in the top 4 search results. [L0311.007]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Procedure for placing content for a featured snippet:
  1. Add a clear definition or a numbered list to the page that already ranks for the keyword.
  2. Put the definition or list at the very start of the post, above the detailed content.
  3. For a list post, give each list item its own section with an H2 heading, then add the detailed explanation below the list. [L0662.010]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Listicle structure for list snippets: a numbered list of short answers sits at the top of the post, with each item linked to an H2 section containing the full details below. Google extracts only the short numbered list with its headings, not the whole article.

  **Pattern:** A "Top 7 ways to reduce cart abandonment" post opens with a numbered list of seven short items, then gives each item an H2 section with an explanation. [L0662.011]

- *Older advice (2021-22):* In a list-type post, each list item's H2 section can include its own picture. The short numbered summary at the top stays clean for Google, while the detailed sections below add visual depth for readers. [L0662.017]

**Reviewing opportunities**

- *Older advice (2021-22):* Review the list of featured snippet opportunities every few months, roughly every six months, because new keywords will enter the top four positions as SEO work progresses. [L0662.014]

### Building Question-and-Answer Content on Blog and Content Pages

- *Older advice (2021-22):* In blog posts, use common customer questions on the topic as H2 headings, with the answer as a paragraph directly below each heading. Search engines give H2 headings more weight, so this makes the answer easier to find. [L0658.008]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* A page that poses a question and answers it directly on the page has a better chance of being returned as the spoken answer to that question. The same question-and-answer format can also help a page win featured snippets. [L0658.003]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* An FAQ page can match a specific question-style informational keyword, though a content page is the usual default for informational queries. Collection and product pages should not be used to answer those questions. [L0881.026]

### Building the FAQ Page

**Setup and structure**

- *Older advice (2021-22):* Build a standalone FAQ page and link it in the main site navigation. Also link to it from other relevant content wherever possible. [L0658.004]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* The page title of an FAQ page is formatted as the H1 heading, and every individual question on the page is formatted as an H2 heading. Questions should never be left as plain body text. [L0658.007]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Put a keyword that is not yet targeted elsewhere in the FAQ page's H1 title, next to the words "frequently asked questions" or "FAQ". [L0658.011]

**Authority**

- *Older advice (2021-22):* Voice search results favor high-authority pages, just as image search does. Gaining links from other content on the site strengthens the authority of the FAQ page. [L0658.005]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* If pages that attract many backlinks are linked to the FAQ page, some link value flows into it. This helps when the FAQ page cannot be linked to directly from external sites. [L0658.006]

**Internal links and purchase path**

- *Older advice (2021-22):* At the end of each FAQ answer, add an internal link to the most relevant next page, such as a collection page or product page, if one exists. Answer the question first, then guide the visitor to the logical next step. [L0658.013]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* An FAQ page on its own does not move visitors toward a purchase because it contains no products. Internal links from answers are what carry visitors deeper into the store. [L0658.014]

**Build procedure**

- *Older advice (2021-22):* Procedure:
  1. If the store has no FAQ page, create one, start filling it with questions, and add it to the site navigation.
  2. Add questions and answers to blog posts where customers commonly ask about the topic, using question H2 headings.
  3. Add a question-and-answer section with H2 or H3 headings to relevant product pages, making sure the content is unique.
  4. Add an internal link at the end of each answer to the logical next page.
  5. Keep adding new questions as new products and categories are added to the store. [L0658.015]

**Maintenance**

- *Older advice (2021-22):* Update the FAQ page at least once a year, and add the word "updated" to the end of the title only after the content has actually been revised. Updates can happen more often whenever new customer questions appear. [L0658.012]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* An FAQ page is a living document, because the questions customers ask are discovered only over time. New products and categories regularly bring new questions that should be added. [L0658.016]

### Product Pages, Content Pages and AI Recommendations

- Product and content pages can be tuned so that LLMs recommend the store for detailed buying queries, such as "the best [niche] to buy". A search for the brand name already returns the store. [L1116.053]
- As of mid-2025, there was no proven page change to get recommended by ChatGPT shopping. The approach was to watch trends and test only once a method shows results. [L0972.107]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Once category collection pages and best-of pages are in place, questions that customers ask about the niche become a source of extra pages. Each of these pages can naturally feature your product where it answers the question. [L0768.024]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Have writers draft FAQ content from the questions they have when reading a product description. Send those questions to the manufacturer and upload the answers. This creates useful product content and builds knowledge for the team. Use the same method for FAQ pages and product pages.

  **Example:** The writer's questions become the FAQ entries, and the manufacturer's answers become the published content. [L1396.046]

### Voice Search

- *Older advice (2021-22):* Voice search is a fast-growing search method, driven by voice assistants on phones and smart speakers such as Google Home. Its share is expected to keep rising. [L0658.001]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Voice search targets full questions. Structure pages so that each question is posed and answered directly on the page to improve the chance of being returned as the spoken answer. [L0658.003]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Voice search results favor high-authority pages. Build authority for the FAQ page through internal links from other content on the site. [L0658.005]

## Content Promotion and Distribution

### Promotion Planning

- Older advice (2021-22): Set the promotion plan when the title is planned, not after publishing. Typical channels are social media, a YouTube channel, a Facebook page, backlink outreach, search text ads and Google Display Network ads, and email to your list. [L0384.014]
- Older advice (2021-22): Publishing content is only the first half of content marketing. Without active promotion, new pages rarely get seen. Search engines and algorithms look for signals of quality and relevance, and those signals have to be generated by promoting the content on purpose. Early traffic gets the snowball rolling so that organic discovery can follow later. [L0385.001]
- Older advice (2021-22): Promote every content piece for at least 2 weeks, ideally up to 30 days, before moving on. [L0383.011]
- Older advice (2021-22): Each piece of content needs active promotion for a minimum of two weeks, and for up to 30 days after it is published. The promotion strategy should be planned during content research, before the piece is written. [L0385.002]
- Older advice (2021-22): Content promotion pursues five goals: generating initial traffic and sales, stimulating social sharing, earning backlinks, building an audience, and filling retargeting audiences and the top of the funnel. [L0385.003]
- Older advice (2021-22): For each piece of content, plan three to four promotion methods out of the available list, rather than using all of them. Choose based on the content type and resources, and start with the ones that are easy to execute. [L0385.004]
- Older advice (2021-22): Content must be actively promoted, because promotion helps discovery by real people and signals to search engines that the content is important enough to rank. [L0414.025]
- Promotion is optional, but it speeds up the organic ranking process that would happen anyway. Focus on the few methods with the highest effect rather than trying every channel. [L0899.002]
- The long-term goal is organic Google ranking, and new content can rank from the start. Each share and visit adds to the ranking signals over time, so promotion mainly accelerates a process that continues without it. [L0899.017]

#### The Twelve Promotion Methods

Older advice (2021-22): Use these twelve methods, in the order presented, when choosing the three to four methods for a piece. [L0385.005]

1. Outreach to other websites for sharing or linking (link building). [L0385.005]
2. Organic posting to social media (Facebook, Instagram, Pinterest, YouTube). [L0385.005]
3. Paid ads on Facebook, Google Display Network, Google Search and YouTube. [L0385.005]
4. Email to the existing audience. [L0385.005]
5. Sharing in forums, Facebook groups and online communities in the niche. [L0385.005]
6. Linking to new content from older content already receiving traffic. [L0385.005]
7. Promotion on content networks (Taboola, Outbrain, Quuu Promote). [L0385.005]
8. Expert roundup posts. [L0385.005]
9. Producing a video and posting it to YouTube. [L0385.005]
10. Converting the content into an infographic and sharing it. [L0385.005]
11. Submitting the content to online content communities. [L0385.005]
12. Outreach to people or sites that were featured in a best-of or list-style article. [L0385.005]

### Launch Sequence for New Content

- Launch each article by:
  1. Posting to your business social media channels. [L0274.060]
  2. Emailing your existing subscriber base with a link. [L0274.060]
- This accelerates organic traction beyond waiting for search engine rankings to build naturally. [L0274.060]
- Promotion means actively driving readers to newly published pages and blog posts from day one, without paid traffic. The aim is to send Google early engagement signals that the content is being seen, which raises the chance of a higher organic ranking. [L0899.001]
- A large number of visitors arriving on a new page on launch day tells Google the page is being checked out. If visitors stay on the page for a while, this is a further positive signal of authority. [L0899.004]

### Social Sharing

- Older advice (2021-22): A single organic social post reaches only a small share of followers. On Facebook it is commonly estimated that fewer than 10% of followers see an organic post. Posting once and considering the job done wastes most of the reach potential. [L0385.007]
- Older advice (2021-22): To maximize one piece of content on social media:
  1. Over a period of about one month (two weeks as a minimum), post 5 to 10 times about the same content. [L0385.008]
  2. Split the content into 5 to 10 small snippets of interesting facts, questions and comments related to it. [L0385.008]
  3. Post each snippet as its own social post with a link to the full content piece. [L0385.008]
  4. Use a different image on each post, such as images from the article. [L0385.008]
  5. Make each post's copy give a reason to click by telling readers what they will learn. [L0385.008]
  6. Schedule all posts in advance with a tool such as Buffer or Hootsuite. [L0385.008]
