- Pitch a blog collection page that holds many useful articles when the resource page links to blogs or useful content in general. Choose a page that gives the audience real advice relevant to the resource page's topic. [L1237.011]
- If no existing page of yours fits a content-linking resource page, write a new blog post on a topic the audience is interested in, ideally one that can rank for keywords. Pitch that post to the resource page after it is published, and do not rush it. [L1237.012]
- If a resource page mixes trade bodies, government sources and service providers without a single clear theme, pitch either your homepage as a trusted supplier of the product category or your blog collection page with practical how-to content. Choose the page that best matches the mix of links already on the list. [L1237.022]
- Do not pitch product pages or collection pages to resource pages. These are commercial pages, so the request reads as a sales ask rather than a value offer for the site's audience, which lowers the chance of being listed. [L1237.013]
- Do not pitch pages on your site that are only loosely related to the topic of the resource page. An irrelevant page significantly reduces the chance of being added to that page. [L1237.014]

**Judging neglected sites and mixed lists**

- A site that looks neglected or dated is not automatically disqualified. If the blog has been online for many years and the author still publishes new posts, it can still be worth pitching after you pass the domain authority check. [L1237.021]
- Some blogs offer guest post exchanges, where they publish your guest post in return for one of yours. If the link is to a commercial site, the blog may charge according to rates published on its advertising page, so check that page before agreeing. [L1237.019]

**Example of search volume**

- A search combining a topic keyword with inurl:links in Google Australia returned about 202,000 results. Many hits were ordinary business or service pages rather than resource pages, so every result needs a manual check of what it actually is before you spend time on it. [L1237.024]

### Outreach Emails

- All outreach emails should be short, to the point, and polite. Long messages or off-topic digressions reduce response rates, so keep each message to the request and a clear question. [L0947.020]
- Resource page outreach does not require a bulk email or mail-merge tool. Send each email individually, because the volume of relevant resource pages in one niche is small. [L1237.023]
- The pitch should frame your page as a valuable resource for the site's audience rather than as a way to promote your products. Your page's usefulness to the audience is the reason the owner should add the link. [L1237.015]
- Many blogs do not publish a public email address. Look for a contact page, an about page or an author page, where a named author's email may be listed. If none is found, use Hunter.io. [L1237.017]
- Prepare a standard response to payment and link-exchange requests before you start outreach, and use it consistently. [L1237.018]
- For backlink outreach, send no more than two to three emails to an influencer or publication. If there is no response, switch to a different channel such as a Facebook page or Instagram direct message, then check back in the future. [L1094.099]

**Opening formula for a resource page outreach email**

Formula: name the specific resource page, give one brief reason it helps its readers, name the single page of yours that adds value, and ask directly whether they would consider adding it. It works because it shows you read the page, moves the request from self-promotion to audience value, and gives the owner an easy yes or no.

- Example 1: Hello [Name], your links page on [topic] is a great find for people researching [subtopic]. I have written a guide on [subtopic] that would fit well alongside your listings, so would you consider adding it?
- Example 2: Hi [Name], your resources list on [topic] saves readers a lot of searching. I put together a practical checklist for [subtopic] that could be useful to your audience, and I wondered if you would be open to including it. [L1237.025]

**Template: initial outreach to a site after a recent SEO check (use exactly as written in the source)**

Not provided as a separate unit; see the formula above. [L1237.025]

### Replies and Negotiation

**Hard no versus soft no**

- Respond differently to two kinds of rejection: a hard no (rude or hostile) should not be answered, and the contact should be removed from the outreach list and tagged as do-not-contact for future campaigns. A soft no (polite refusal, often with praise) should be answered to keep the relationship open. [L0947.010]
- Replying to a hostile hard no escalates the situation and rarely changes the outcome. Do not reply; delete the contact from the list, record it as do-not-email, and move on. [L0947.011]
- Outreach is about building relationships with sites in your industry and niche, so a no today does not rule out a link later. Continue to respond politely to soft no replies because other collaboration opportunities may come from the same contact. [L0947.014]

**Declining a paid link while keeping the relationship**

Template: declining a paid-link offer and offering a guest post

```text
Thanks for your reply. Unfortunately, I don't have the budget to pay for links of this kind. If you'd still like to work together, could I write a guest post for your site instead? I'm confident I could create something that adds genuine value for your readers. What do you think?:)
```

[L0947.004]

- Declining a paid link while keeping the relationship works by naming a budget or policy limit, then offering a no-cost alternative that still gives the other site content value and gives you a backlink. The offer is framed as mutual benefit, and the reply can be yes or no.
  - Example 1: Thanks for the quote. Our budget is set for content only this quarter. Would you be open to a short guest article from me instead? Happy to draft a few ideas.
  - Example 2: We don't pay for placements right now, but I can contribute an in-depth guide to your resource section that your readers would find useful. Interested? [L0947.005]

**Link exchanges**

- Link exchanges, where the other site asks you to link back, are generally avoided because search engines may view reciprocal linking unfavorably over time and it spends your own link authority. When a site proposes an exchange, the preferred response is to decline politely and redirect to a guest post. Declining is acceptable and not a sign of a failed outreach. [L0947.006]

Template: declining a link exchange and redirecting to a guest post (version 1)

```text
Thanks for getting back to me. Sadly, I avoid link exchanges because they can cause problems with Google. I understand you need to gain something from these arrangements, so I was wondering whether I could write a guest post for your site. It would be a mutually beneficial way to collaborate. Any thoughts?:)
```

[L0947.007]

Template: declining a link exchange and redirecting to a guest post (version 2)

```text
Thanks for getting back to me. Regrettably, I steer clear of link exchanges. My SEO specialist has warned me that these can sometimes lead to trouble with Google. I completely understand you want to get something out of such exchanges, so I had the idea of writing a guest post for your site. It would be a win-win collaboration. Any thoughts?:)
```

[L0947.008]

Template: closing reply when a site will not write a guest post and you decline the exchange entirely

```text
Totally get it, don't blame you. We'll just have to leave it there, but if you do still want to link to my site, please feel free to do so.
```

[L0947.009]

**Asking for feedback after a soft no**

Template: requesting feedback

```text
Hey, thanks for getting back to me. No problem:) I wanted to check whether you had any feedback. I just want to be sure I wasn't too far off track with this. Thanks
```

[L0947.012]

- After a soft no, asking for feedback opens a second chance. If feedback points to a different content direction, offer to write a guest post on that topic at no cost to them. The pattern is: acknowledge, ask for feedback, then offer a guest post aligned with what they want.
  - Example 1: Thanks for the honest reply. What topic would fit your readers better? I could draft a piece on that and send it over.
  - Example 2: Appreciate you letting me know. If you'd like something more practical for your audience, I can write a guest article on that angle. [L0947.013]

**Clarifying a misunderstood request**

- When a contact did not understand the request (confusion or suspicion they must pay), send a short polite clarification that asks them to update an existing piece of content to include a link to your article. Use the batch spreadsheet to find the target URL before writing. If they stay unclear, offer a phone call to walk them through it. [L0947.015]

Template: clarifying the request

```text
Thanks for writing back. I had a smaller exchange in mind. Would you be open to editing your article at [Their URL] so it links to my article at [Your URL]? I think it would be a valuable addition for your readers. What do you think?
```

[L0947.016]

### Follow-Up and Verification

**Procedure: follow-up when an agreed link never appeared**

1. Check the page for the link; confirm it is missing, and check Google Search Console for the backlink.
2. Reply within the same email thread so the earlier commitment is visible.
3. Write a short, polite message saying you looked but could not find the link and ask whether you looked in the wrong place.
4. Make it sound like you may have missed it, not like they broke a promise, to keep the relationship friendly.
5. Keep the tone polite and light; they usually add the link after this reminder. [L0947.017]

Template: follow-up reminder

```text
That's great! I just checked, but I couldn't find the link. Maybe I was looking in the wrong place?:) Thanks
```

[L0947.018]

- Use Google Search Console to verify whether an agreed backlink has actually gone live on the partner's site. [L0947.019]
- Send follow-up emails at a regular interval. [L1237.004]

### Updating Links to Retired Pages

- When you remove a page, first identify the linking pages with Ahrefs or Ubersuggest so you know exactly where each link sits. [L1414.032]

Template: asking a linking site to update a link to a retired page

```text
Subject: Updating a link to our page on [Their site name]

Hi [Name],

We are removing the page [OLD URL] from our store. Your site currently links to it on [LINKING PAGE URL], in [section or position of the link on that page].

Would you be able to change that link so it points to our new page, [NEW URL]? This keeps the link working for your readers and keeps the link pointing to a good page on our site.

Thank you,
[Your name]
[Brand name]
[Contact email]
```

[L1414.032]

### Risk, Anchor Text and Disavowing

- Disavow spammy backlinks, such as links from adult or unrelated spam sites, before a sale. Buyers look closely at the link profile for these. [L1468.029]
- If anchor text is heavily over-optimized, add more varied and natural anchors to dilute it. Make these link changes early, because a month-before change can damage the site's look to a buyer. [L1468.030]
- Buying backlinks is risky because it can lead to a penalty. Stick with guest posting on sites that already publish guest content. [L1094.101]
- Do not pitch paid links to link farms; remove them from the list instead of negotiating. [L0947.022]

#### Further details

- Paid sponsored posts on large publications can include a link back to the store. [L1227.016]

## YouTube and Video SEO

### YouTube SEO: Why Video Needs Its Own Strategy

- YouTube has its own search system, so video keywords must be researched separately, even when the same topics are in the written content strategy. [L0758.017]
- YouTube uses a simpler ranking logic than Google web search, which uses more than 200 ranking signals. For YouTube SEO, the practical focus is keyword-matched titles and descriptions, plus viewer engagement such as watch time and clicks. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.022]
- YouTube works like a search engine. Videos can stay findable and keep attracting viewers for years. (older, 2021-22) [L1424.002]
- A video can rank in Google results before a comparable text page does. For search terms where competition for text results is strong, a relevant YouTube video that answers the query can earn a first-page spot until the store's own page ranks. (older, 2021-22) [L1424.002]
- Google often shows video results on the first page, sometimes two videos from the same channel, which is unusual for text results. Searches for reviews, best-of queries and how-to queries are especially likely to display YouTube videos. (older, 2021-22) [L1424.003]
- Unlike Google web search, several videos from the same channel can rank for one search term on YouTube. Targeting the same keyword with more than one video is therefore acceptable. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.008]
- Video is optional for content marketing, because written content alone can produce enough traffic. The easiest start is turning existing written content into video. A video can be made for YouTube specifically if it targets terms people already search for. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.017]
- A YouTube channel does not need a big audience to work. A small number of relevant videos can still gain subscribers and website traffic. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.016]
- Video is an extra traffic source. A channel with subscribers and engagement also increases business value if the business is sold later. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.016]
- The number of subscribers is not tied to the number of videos. Five to ten good videos on topics people are interested in can already bring subscribers and website visitors. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.020]

### Choosing Video Topics From Demand

- Compare view counts with video age. A video with many views in a short time signals a topic people want. Use these comparisons to choose content ideas and to spot topics that draw little attention. (older, 2021-22) [L0478.018]
- Example: in one surfing channel, a video posted about a month earlier had around 249,000 views, while a video posted three months earlier had around 13,000 views. The gap shows which subject resonates with the audience and guides the next content choice. (older, 2021-22) [L0478.019]
- Use trend tools to choose video topics people already respond to, then make your own take that is better than what is trending. [L0487.061]
- Watch time matters on YouTube, because longer viewing causes the platform to show the video to more people. [L0487.061]
- Interest-based videos can target the broader topic around your products rather than the product itself. Formats include:
  - Top-10 lists, for example top 10 big wave spots. [L1511.025]
  - Compilations, for example worst wipeouts. [L1511.025]
  - Reviews of related items, for example the best movies in the niche. [L1511.025]
- These formats attract viewers who share the interest, and they can later become customers or website visitors. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.025]

### Keyword Research Tools

| Tool | What it gives for YouTube keywords | Source |
|---|---|---|
| YouTube search auto-suggest | Terms people already search for | [L1511.003, L1511.004] |
| Ahrefs Keyword Explorer | YouTube search volume | [L1511.003] |
| Ubersuggest | Does not show YouTube search volume | [L1511.003] |
| Google Keyword Planner | Does not show YouTube search volume | [L1511.003] |

- The tool information reflects 2021-22 advice and should be checked against current tool features. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.003]

### Keyword Research Procedures

**Auto-suggest keyword method**

1. Type a core product term into the YouTube search bar. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.004]
2. Read the suggested completions, which reflect commonly searched terms. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.004]
3. Add variations by product type, beginner or intermediate level, use case, feature, and brand names. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.004]
4. Check the results with the Report search predictions option only if you want to remove irrelevant suggestions. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.004]
5. Record the chosen terms in a keyword research tracking spreadsheet, grouped by product or brand. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.004]

**Keyword-to-video idea method for a product range**

1. List the products, categories and brands you sell. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.021]
2. For each, search YouTube auto-suggest and note terms with volume signals. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.021]
3. Add the terms to a keyword research spreadsheet. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.021]
4. Turn each term into a video idea, for example beginner guides, reviews of a brand, comparisons, top-10 lists, or entertaining compilations for the niche. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.021]

### Video Titles

- The primary keyword must appear in the video title, as close to the start as possible, the same way it would on a website page. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.001]
- Titles should be plain and descriptive, signalling what the viewer will learn, not hype. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.001]

### Video Descriptions

- The primary keyword should appear in the description no more than three times. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.005, L1511.002]
- The description should stay under 300 words. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.005, L1511.002]
- The description should explain why the viewer should watch and what benefit they get, and include a call to action. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.005, L1511.002]
- Descriptions can contain as many links as needed, such as product pages and social profiles. Links in the description are not a ranking factor, but they let viewers click through to anything mentioned in the video. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.006]
- When a YouTube video sends viewers away from the store, the description should link back to the store. A call to action with a link card should be placed at the end of the video so traffic still reaches the website. (older, 2021-22) [L1424.004]

### Tags

- The tag field accepts up to 10 relevant search terms. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.007]
- Tags can include a range of similar terms, not only the main target keyword. The same keyword may be used across several videos. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.007]
- Tags may repeat keywords used on other videos. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.002]

### Captions, Closed Captions and On-Screen Text

- Add on-screen text to the caption and closed captions of every video so the platform can tell what the video is about. Videos without this text are less likely to be distributed to the right viewers. [L1457.001]

### Video Production and Retention

- Lighting must be good enough that viewers can see the screen. Dark videos hurt engagement. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.011]
- Production does not need to be polished. Keep the video moving by cutting irrelevant material and keeping audio and visuals active. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.011]
- YouTube ranks videos partly on engagement and watch time, so viewers must stay on the video. Watching on YouTube rather than leaving for another platform is what the platform rewards. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.009]
- Hold engagement with a clear intro and a fast-paced video. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.002]
- Passive viewing without likes, comments, saves or shares makes a video far less likely to be pushed beyond the existing followers. Silent viewers are therefore a distribution problem, not only a missed interaction. [L1457.003]

### Channel Setup

**Channel setup on YouTube**

1. Use a Google account to log in and open your channel from the menu. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.018]
2. Add a cover image, which can be a product photo, text, logo, or brand name, linking back to the website and social accounts. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.018]
3. Fill in the About section with description, business enquiry email, location, and links to the website and social profiles. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.018]
4. Upload videos regularly. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.018]
5. Create a static welcome video rather than a changing one. A welcome video only shows to non-subscribers, and its purpose is to get them to subscribe. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.018]

- Subscribers are notified each time a new video is uploaded, so the channel's main goal for non-subscribers is to get them to subscribe. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.019]

### Embedding Videos on Store Pages

- Embedding a YouTube video on a high-traffic store site can help the video rank on YouTube for the store domain name, for example when someone searches the domain on YouTube. The video can also appear on the first page of Google organic results. (older, 2019) [L0467.018]
- Google favors pages that embed relevant videos. A small drop in the Shopify speed score is worth the SEO gain from a YouTube embed. (older, 2022) [L0854.065]

**Embedding a product video without slowing the store** (older, 2022)

1. Create a YouTube channel for the store. [L0854.064]
2. Upload the product videos to this channel. [L0854.064]
3. Click Embed on the YouTube video and copy the embed code. [L0854.064]
4. Paste the embed code onto the relevant product page. [L0854.064]

- Do not host the video in Shopify Files, because Google favors YouTube embeds for SEO and the store page score drop is acceptable. (older, 2022) [L0854.064]
- A video embedded on a product page shows the message `not indexed due to not being on a dedicated watch page` in Search Console. This is not an error. Google only indexes a video as its own result when the page is mostly about that video, so no fix is needed for supplemental videos. [L1287.033]

### Video SEO Checklist

**YouTube SEO procedure** (older, 2021-22)

1. Keyword research for each video, identifying at least one target search term. [L1511.002]
2. Put that keyword in the title, near the start. [L1511.002]
3. Write a plain, descriptive title that tells the viewer what they will learn. [L1511.002]
4. Write a description that explains the benefit, uses the primary keyword no more than three times, stays under 300 words, and includes a call to action. [L1511.002]
5. Add up to 10 relevant search terms in the tag field, which may repeat keywords used on other videos. [L1511.002]
6. Hold engagement with a clear intro and a fast-paced video. [L1511.002]
7. Use clickable thumbnails. [L1511.002]
8. Reply to comments. [L1511.002]
9. Promote each video soon after upload. [L1511.002]

### Promotion

- Promote each new video early after upload, for example via email list, social media profiles, and embedding it on the website where relevant. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.015]
- An embedded YouTube video on a site counts as a view on YouTube, and an early boost of engagement helps the algorithm. (older, 2021-22) [L1511.015]
- Plan the promotion of each video as part of the content plan before publishing, not after. Decide in advance which email list, social profiles, or relevant website pages will receive the video soon after upload, so it gets an early engagement boost. [L1511.027]

## Monitoring, Reporting and Ongoing Maintenance

### Search Console Review Cadence

Sources give different intervals for checking Search Console. Each row keeps its own wording.

| Source | Interval | Scope | IDs |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source A | approximately once per week | General check for errors or warnings, fixing issues that could affect rankings, and monitoring which keywords drive free organic traffic | [L0171.006] |
| Source A | at least once a week | Errors and indexing problems, and tracking which keywords the store ranks for organically | [L0735.010] |
| Source A 2023 | every couple of weeks | Errors and issues that affect organic traffic | [L0835.089] |
| Source A | every two weeks; more often for high-traffic stores, less often for low-traffic stores; better scheduled in a calendar | Full routine for a Shopify store (see below) | [L1271.007] |

The general rules say weekly. The 2023 rule and the Shopify routine say every couple of weeks. The Shopify routine is the only source that adjusts the interval to traffic [L0171.006, L0735.010, L0835.089, L1271.007].

**Weekly check (general procedure)**

1. Check Google Search Console approximately once per week [L0171.006].
2. Look for errors or warnings in your site's data [L0171.006].
3. Fix any issues that could impact your rankings [L0171.006].
4. Monitor which keywords are driving free organic traffic to your store [L0171.006].

**Fortnightly Shopify check**

1. Open Google Search Console > Sitemaps and make sure the sitemap is still submitted [L1271.007].
2. Open the Review snippets report and make sure all pages with reviews are valid and green [L1271.007].
3. Open the Core Web Vitals / Page experience report and check for errors or critical issues and that the site is secure [L1271.007].
4. Open Performance and review impressions, average position and CTR, then note which queries and pages changed [L1271.007].

### Triage of Search Console Issues

- Errors that prevent Google from indexing or ranking pages must be addressed [L0835.089].
- Issues such as soft 404 or missing fields are not urgent unless pages are de-indexed or missing from Google [L0429.074] (older advice, 2022).
- Address non-urgent issues after ad performance is stable, or post screenshots for help [L0429.074] (older advice, 2022).
- The Smart SEO audit score is a guide. Fix errors that can be resolved quickly, but do not chase a perfect score [L1116.051].

### Search Console Reports

**Performance report**

- The report shows impressions, clicks, and the search queries (keywords) that led to clicks and impressions, along with the pages involved. It is used to judge free organic traffic and which keywords rank [L0735.007].
- It shows total clicks, total impressions, average CTR and average position for queries, pages, countries, devices and search appearance over a chosen date range (for example, last 3 months) [L1333.006, L1333.016] (older advice, 2021-2022).
- Default settings: Search type "Web" and date range "Last 3 months". Tabs: Queries, Pages, Countries, Devices, Search appearance. It can be filtered by position and exported with the EXPORT button [L1333.016] (older advice, 2021-2022).
- Data lags. The "Last updated" label shows the delay, for example 5 hours ago, or several hours [L1333.006, L1333.016] (older advice, 2021-2022).
- Limits: the report has limited filtering and detail and works mainly as a quick overview. For deeper keyword and ranking analysis, Ahrefs or SEMrush give more usable data [L1333.006, L1333.017] (older advice, 2021-2022).

**Indexing and user-experience report**

- The report shows how many site pages are indexed, meaning they can appear in free Google search results. It also flags user-experience problems such as slow loading pages [L0735.008].

### Tool Choice

- Google Search Console is the main tool for tracking SEO results, including rankings for product searches versus competitors [L1112.031] (older advice, 2021).
- Ahrefs is an optional advanced tool and is not required [L1112.031] (older advice, 2021).
- Sources differ on third-party tools. The 2021 rule calls Ahrefs optional, while the older advice says dedicated tools give more usable data for deeper analysis. Both apply: Search Console is enough to start, and a third-party tool such as Ahrefs or SEMrush is added when deeper keyword and ranking analysis is needed [L1112.031, L1333.017] (older advice, 2021-2022).

### Checking Search Results Accurately

- To see how a product page appears in organic Google results, including price, stock, review stars and delivery details, search in an incognito or logged-out window [L1116.060].
- A logged-in search for a product term can return only Shopping results and hide the organic listings [L1116.060].

### Diagnosing Traffic Quality

**Checking where organic traffic really comes from**

1. Open Google Search Console and review which keywords produce impressions and clicks, since Google Analytics gives poor search term data [L0971.004].
2. Use Google Analytics to see which countries the visitors come from [L0971.004].
3. Judge whether the traffic is qualified (right countries, right keywords) before optimizing the site for conversion [L0971.004].

**Common mistake**

- A low sales count with many visitors can mean the traffic comes from unrelated search terms or from countries where the store does not sell, not from a weak store [L0971.005].

### Tracking Keywords and Organic Rankings

- Mapping keywords to pages gives SEO work a clear focus and replaces scattered effort [L0879.006] (older advice, 2021-2022).
- Once the top keywords are tracked, ranking progress and organic traffic show whether the SEO strategy works [L0879.006] (older advice, 2021-2022).
- Keep tracking which keywords the store ranks for organically during each Search Console check [L0735.010, L1271.007].

### Tracking TikTok Traffic with UTM Parameters

**Procedure**

1. Search "TikTok utm parameters builder" and use the TikTok Ads Manager link [L0298.044].
2. Enter your product URL and the builder generates a UTM-tagged link [L0298.044].
3. Use this link in TikTok campaigns [L0298.044].
4. View results in Shopify Reports > Sessions by Marketing and select UTM term and UTM content filters [L0298.044].

### Quick-Win Sequence for Top Blog Posts

**Procedure for the top five posts by traffic**

1. Run a diagnostic pass for about 30 to 45 minutes with no edits. Pull query-level Search Console data for the top five posts, identify queries with high impressions but few clicks, and check the brand name search results and the storefront setup for localized URLs (canonicals and hreflang) [L1269.016].
2. Rewrite the title tag, H1, meta description and first intro paragraph of those posts around the high-impression queries the diagnostic found [L1269.016].
3. In the same editing pass, add an on-topic call-to-action block and an email capture to each post [L1269.016].
4. In the same pass, add contextual internal links from those posts to the relevant collections and to the About or mission page, using descriptive anchor text [L1269.016].
5. Fix only the technical issues the diagnostic actually found, not a blanket clean-up [L1269.016].

**Scope of bulk rewrites**

- In a bulk blog rewrite, every blog article can get a row in the CSV, but full title, meta and intro rewrites should go only to the articles that actually carry search demand. In one case that was seven articles, and the rest did not need the same effort [L1269.049].

**Expected results**

- Average position in Search Console blends every query a page ranks for. Part of a low click-through rate can therefore come from weaker, lower-ranked queries [L1269.044].
- Do not project a multiple of current clicks from a target CTR. Present the result as a range and expect real but smaller gains [L1269.044].

**Case: content-led Shopify store**

- Starting point: top blog posts had high search impressions but low click-through rates and no next step for readers [L1269.043].
- The diagnostic pass checked query-level data, the brand search result and the localized storefront setup. The team chose five posts for changes [L1269.043].
- The plan: rewrite titles and intros on those posts around high-impression queries, add an on-topic CTA and email capture, add internal links to collections and the mission page, and fix only technical issues found in the diagnostic [L1269.043].
- Projection for one top post: about 33,858 impressions, 90 clicks, average position about 8, CTR about 0.27 percent. Reaching about 1 percent would mean about 340 clicks [L1269.043].
- Lesson: the case shows the plan and the projected CTR math, but no measured result from the rewrites [L1269.043].

### Content Refreshes

- Update existing blog posts annually by revising the title to indicate the new year (for example, change to "updated 2021") [L0311.005] (older advice, 2021-2022).
- Keeping content current helps maintain top search rankings [L0311.005] (older advice, 2021-2022).
- After the publishing pace is established, shift effort from creating new content to optimizing pages and posts that already perform well [L1321.028].
- Improving existing winners is preferred over constantly producing new content [L1321.028].

### Keeping SEO Work Continuous

- Run the Search Console routine on a fixed schedule, every two weeks for a Shopify store and adjusted for traffic, and place it in a calendar [L1271.007].
- Track keyword rankings and organic traffic sources each time, so that ranking progress shows whether the strategy works [L0879.006, L0735.010].
- Once publishing is established, direct effort to improving pages and posts that already perform well [L1321.028].

## Other details

### Product research checks

- When researching products through Google Shopping, do not judge market viability from the first page of results alone. If cheaper products dominate the first page, filter by price range to check that higher-priced variants exist in sufficient quantity. (older advice, 2020) [L0245.047]

### Keyword research and listing copy

1. On Amazon, research keywords with Amazon's own search suggestions. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L0768.013]
2. Include the chosen keywords in the product title, the description and the bullet points. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L0768.013]
- In 2021, Amazon was described as a pay-for-play system in which organic ranking was not realistic without paid ads. (older advice, 2021-2022) Plan an advertising budget for Amazon from the start. [L0768.014]

### Store naming and file metadata

- Including the niche keyword in the domain is preferred when possible but not required. Creative names that fit the store theme also work well. (2023) [L0934.098]
- Set custom metadata in PDF documents, including the author, affiliated company and keywords. This metadata supports SEO and can later serve as proof of ownership if the file is copied. [L1357.029]

### On-site SEO in Shopify

- Site SEO is covered in the search engine domination lesson. The main focus is on-site optimization in Shopify and organic traffic tools. (older advice, 2022) [L0969.082]
- Free SEO training resources are available as a starting point for improving a store's organic search visibility. (older advice, 2020) [L1087.008]

### Traffic estimates and SEO tools

- Ubersuggest (ubersuggest.com) is a cheap SEO research tool that estimates a domain's organic monthly traffic. Ahrefs or Semrush can be used instead if you already pay for them. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L0893.011]
- Traffic source findings are estimates, not facts, because competitors may run channels that are hard to detect. Treat search results and SEO tool numbers as a best guess and record that uncertainty in the comments. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L0893.012]

### Local search and listings

- Local SEO tactics, such as citations in local newspapers, are specialized and rarely move rankings for a national store. Some local practices can still help a national business, but they are minor. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L0879.008]
- For Australian searchers, search engines tend to show.com.au domains ahead of.com domains. A local page with quality content and links can outrank overseas listicles that rank for the same term. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L0949.052]
- Google Business Profile, 2021 view: it is not necessary for a dropshipping store to rank, so it does not need to be a priority at launch. (older advice, 2021) [L0962.157]
- Google Business Profile, 2025 view: it is optional for this store model, and leaving it off is unlikely to cause harm. The two views agree that it is not a launch priority; the 2025 view is the more recent guidance. [L1116.054]

### Feed work and reviews

- If the store is already optimized for SEO, most of the feed work is already done. Google's organic search and Shopping matching work in similar ways, so the same title, image and description work help both. (older advice, 2021-2022) [L1170.022]
- Junip can add a dedicated reviews page that gathers all store reviews in one place. The vendor says this page helps the store rank when people search for reviews of the brand. [L0589.009]
